# pytest cache directory #

This directory contains data from the pytest's cache plugin,
which provides the `--lf` and `--ff` options, as well as the `cache` fixture.

**Do not** commit this to version control.

See [the docs](https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/how-to/cache.html) for more information.

# ⚡ FlowBridge

### The Enterprise Integration Platform — Built Native on Frappe

*Connect anything. Automate everything. Ship integrations in minutes, not months.*

[User Guide](docs/userguide.md) · [Use Cases](docs/usecases.md) · [Progress Tracker](docs/tracker.md)

[![Frappe Framework](https://img.shields.io/badge/Frappe-v15%20%7C%20v16-blue)](https://frappeframework.com)
[![ERPNext](https://img.shields.io/badge/ERPNext-v15%20%7C%20v16-orange)](https://erpnext.com)

[![Phases](https://img.shields.io/badge/Phases%20Complete-26%20%2F%2026-brightgreen)](#)



---

> **FlowBridge** is a production-grade, no-code / low-code integration platform that lives *inside* your Frappe / ERPNext installation — no separate server, no vendor lock-in, no subscription tax on your own infrastructure.
>
> Build visual workflows on a drag-and-drop canvas, connect to 40+ pre-built connectors, publish integrations to a marketplace, and run AI-powered automations — all with a single `bench install-app`.

---

## Why FlowBridge?

| Pain point | FlowBridge answer |
|---|---|
| Zapier / Make costs scale with usage | Self-hosted, unlimited flows, flat infra cost |
| Every integration needs a developer | Visual Studio + AI Field Mapper builds flows without code |
| Tribal knowledge silos between teams | Shared connector library, RBAC, version history, comments |
| Compliance audits are painful | Immutable audit trail, SOC 2 export, DPDP Act, 21 CFR Part 11 |
| Fragile one-off scripts break in prod | Circuit breaker, DLQ, durable event queue, chaos mode testing |
| India-specific ERP pain (GST, TDS, RBI) | Built-in GST automation, TDS engine, RBI forex, AA connector |

---

## Feature Overview

### Visual Studio
| Capability | Details |
|---|---|
| Drag-and-drop canvas | React Flow canvas with dark theme, minimap, live debug overlay |
| Node types | Trigger, Action, Logic (Condition, Delay), Transform, Loop, Sub-flow, Batch, SOAP, GraphQL |
| Trigger types | Webhook, Schedule (cron), DocType Event, Manual, Polling, CDC (MariaDB binlog) |
| Universal HTTP Connector | 9 auth types, nested field mapping, array wildcards, pagination (page/cursor/offset/link-header), XML/SOAP/JSON/CSV response parsing |
| Pre-built connectors | Shopify, Razorpay, WhatsApp Business, Twilio, SendGrid, Google Sheets, Shiprocket, WooCommerce, Tally, ERPNext REST |
| Flow testing | Step-through debugger, 15-run history, sandbox mode with mock responses |

### AI Engine
| Capability | Details |
|---|---|
| Multi-provider | Claude (Anthropic), OpenAI, Gemini, Ollama, Default Engine (offline, zero-dependency) |
| AI Flow Builder | Describe a workflow in plain English → fully wired flow JSON |
| AI Field Mapper | Sentence-transformers + FAISS cosine similarity + RapidFuzz fallback |
| AI Connector Builder | Plain English → connector.json with endpoints, auth, and field mappings |
| Copilot | In-context AI sidebar in Studio, Error Logs, Analytics, Marketplace, and API Builder |
| ERPNext Agents | Tool-using agents (search, read, create, SQL, run flow) with RAG over ERPNext data |
| ONNX Runtime | 3-tier encoder: sentence-transformers → ONNX Runtime → TF-IDF |
| Custom training | Opt-in correction data collection → fine-tune → export to Hugging Face |

### Marketplace
- Publish flows and connectors with versioning, review, and one-click install
- Razorpay payment integration (one-time + subscriptions)
- Developer revenue dashboard, TDS/GST payout system
- Community Q&A, ratings, and auto-update engine

### Data Platform
- **Two-way sync** with conflict resolution (last-write-wins, source-wins, manual review)
- **ETL pipelines** — extract from any source, transform, load to BigQuery / Snowflake / Redshift / Postgres / ERPNext
- **Reverse ETL** — warehouse → ERPNext
- **CDC** — MariaDB binlog streaming with GTID resume; polling fallback
- **Durable event queue** — persist-first (MariaDB before Redis), DLQ, replay, out-of-order handling
- **Document Intelligence** — PDF extraction, OCR, AI field extraction, human review queue

### Enterprise & Compliance
- Multi-site orchestration (federation, HQ push/pull)
- BPM long-running workflows with durable state and SLA tracking
- Human-in-loop approvals (email, WhatsApp, ERPNext form, multi-approver consensus)
- Team collaboration: RBAC, version history, diff, comments, shared credential pools
- Audit log (immutable), OpenTelemetry observability, PII masking
- Compliance: DPDP Act (India), 21 CFR Part 11, SOC 2 / ISO 27001 evidence export
- OAuth2 token manager with auto-refresh, circuit breaker, response caching, chaos mode

### Ecosystem & Connectivity
| Protocol | Connectors |
|---|---|
| REST/HTTP | Universal Connector, OpenAPI import, Postman import, SOAP/WSDL |
| Messaging | MQTT, Kafka, AMQP/RabbitMQ |
| File/Email | SFTP, FTP, IMAP, Excel/CSV file drop |
| Database | CDC (MariaDB binlog), Postgres, BigQuery, Snowflake, Redshift |
| India-specific | SAP RFC/BTP, Busy Accounting, GST e-Invoice API, ICEGATE, DGFT |
| TCP | Tally XML over TCP socket |

**Industry Packs:** Manufacturing, Pharma, Retail, Construction, NBFC/Finance, Logistics

**Connector SDK:** Category A (connector.json, no code), Category B (Python class), Category C (streaming with ThreadPoolExecutor). Submission → review → marketplace pipeline with bounty program.

### FlowBridge Cloud (Phase 8)
- Multi-tenant isolation with Redis namespace prefixing per tenant
- HSM-backed credential vault: AWS KMS → Azure Key Vault → Frappe AES-256
- Zero-install onboarding: sign up → ERPNext connected in  ~/.flowbridge.toml  trigger()} disabled={loading}>Sync;
}

## API Reference

All endpoints are exposed as Frappe whitelisted methods at `/api/method/`.

### Flows
```
POST  frappe_flowbridge.flowbridge.api.flow.trigger_flow
GET   frappe_flowbridge.flowbridge.api.flow.get_flow_run_status
GET   frappe_flowbridge.flowbridge.api.flow.list_flows
GET   frappe_flowbridge.flowbridge.api.flow.export_flow
POST  frappe_flowbridge.flowbridge.api.flow.import_flow
```

## Connector SDK

Build and publish connectors without writing Frappe code.

### Category A — Declarative (connector.json, no code)

```json
{
  "$schema": "flowbridge/v1",
  "connector_name": "My API",
  "base_url": "https://api.example.com",
  "auth_type": "api_key",
  "endpoints": [
    {
      "endpoint_name": "Get Orders",
      "method": "GET",
      "path": "/orders",
      "response_key": "data"
    }
  ]
}
```

```bash
flowbridge connector validate ./connector.json
flowbridge connector publish ./connector.json
```

### Category B — Python Class

```python
from frappe_flowbridge.flowbridge.api.connector_sdk import FlowBridgeConnector

class MyConnector(FlowBridgeConnector):
    connector_name = "My Connector"
    base_url = "https://api.example.com"
    auth_type = "api_key"

    def execute(self, endpoint_name: str, payload: dict) -> dict:
        # Custom logic
        return {"result": ...}

MyConnector().register()
```

### Category C — Streaming (high throughput)

```python
from frappe_flowbridge.flowbridge.api.connector_sdk import StreamingConnector

class MyStreamConnector(StreamingConnector):
    concurrency = 10

    def _stream_records(self, payload: dict):
        for record in self.fetch_all_pages(payload):
            yield record
```

## Industry Packs

Install a pre-built set of flows for your industry with one API call:

```python
# Python
from frappe_flowbridge.flowbridge.api.industry_packs import install_industry_pack
install_industry_pack("manufacturing")   # Work Order alerts, low-stock auto PO, WO complete → SO
install_industry_pack("retail")          # Shopify sync, loyalty points, WhatsApp receipts
install_industry_pack("logistics")       # Shiprocket booking, AWB tracking, delivery webhooks
install_industry_pack("pharma")          # Batch expiry, 21 CFR Part 11, e-Way Bill
install_industry_pack("nbfc_finance")    # KYC/CIBIL, EMI reminders, RBI MIS
install_industry_pack("construction")    # Milestone → invoice, Material Request → PO
```

## Development

### Setup

```bash
cd apps/frappe_flowbridge
pre-commit install
```

### Code style

- **Python:** ruff + pyupgrade (configured in `pyproject.toml`)
- **JavaScript/TypeScript:** eslint + prettier
- **Commits:** conventional commits preferred

### Running tests

```bash
bench run-tests --app frappe_flowbridge
```

### Using the Flow Testing Framework

```python
from frappe_flowbridge.flowbridge.sdk.python_sdk import FlowTest, MockConnector

def test_shopify_sync():
    mock = (
        MockConnector("Shopify")
        .mock_endpoint("get_order", {
            "id": 42,
            "total_price": "999.00",
            "line_items": [{"title": "Widget", "quantity": 2}],
        })
    )
    result = (
        FlowTest("Shopify to ERPNext")
        .with_payload({"order_id": 42})
        .mock_connector("Shopify", mock)
        .expect_no_errors()
        .expect_context("sales_order_name", lambda v: v.startswith("SAL-"))
        .run_offline(flow_json)
    )
    assert result.passed, result.errors
    mock.assert_called("get_order", times=1)
```

### CI/CD

GitHub Actions workflows are auto-generated by FlowBridge:

```bash
bench execute frappe_flowbridge.flowbridge.api.github_actions.generate_workflow_yaml
```

This creates `.github/workflows/flowbridge.yml` with:
- Flow validation on PR
- Auto-deploy to site on merge to `main`
- Connector SDK validation

## Contributing

1. Fork the repository
2. Create a feature branch: `git checkout -b feat/my-feature`
3. Install pre-commit: `pre-commit install`
4. Make your changes and write tests
5. Run tests: `bench run-tests --app frappe_flowbridge`
6. Open a pull request against `develop`

For connector contributions, use the [Connector SDK](#connector-sdk) and submit via the marketplace review pipeline.

# FlowBridge — Motto & Vision

## The Tagline
> **"Connect anything to ERPNext. Build once. Sell to thousands."**

## The Alternate Tagline
> **"Turn ERPNext into an API platform. Build, connect, publish — everything in one place."**

---

## What FlowBridge Is

FlowBridge is the integration platform that should have been built alongside ERPNext itself.

Not a webhook tool. Not a connector library. The **entire data and API infrastructure layer** for the Frappe ecosystem.

---

## The Analogy

| What they did | What FlowBridge does |
|---|---|
| Zapier for SaaS tools | FlowBridge for Frappe/ERPNext |
| Shopify App Store for e-commerce | FlowBridge Marketplace for integrations |
| WordPress plugins for websites | FlowBridge flows for every ERPNext site |

But with one massive difference:
- **You OWN your integrations.**
- Self-hosted. No per-task pricing. No data leaving your server.

---

## The Core Beliefs

**1. Integrations should be visual, not coded.**
Any implementor should be able to connect Shopify to ERPNext without writing a single line.

**2. ERPNext should be an API platform, not just a backend.**
Every ERPNext business should be able to expose their data like Stripe exposes theirs.

**3. The Frappe community should build the product, not just use it.**
One developer's flow becomes a thousand businesses' solution via the Marketplace.

**4. Performance is a feature.**
Sub-5ms webhook acknowledgement. 100k+ req/s API gateway. The integrations layer cannot be the bottleneck.

**5. AI should be embedded, not bolted on.**
From natural language flow building to field mapping to failure prediction — AI is a native layer, not a module.

---

## The Architecture Philosophy

```
Python  — Where Frappe lives. No fighting the framework.
Rust    — Where speed matters. API gateway, crypto, heavy compute.
Go      — Where concurrency matters. Flow execution, connectors.
```

No Python/Node.js runtimes executing your flows at Zapier speeds.
FlowBridge processes 100,000 webhook events per second with sub-5ms acknowledgement.

---

## The Business Philosophy

- Custom integrations cost ₹50K–5L to build today.
- FlowBridge makes that a one-time ₹999 Marketplace install.
- Developers who build flows earn revenue. FlowBridge takes a small cut.
- Everyone wins. The flywheel spins.

---

## The Product Suite Context

FlowBridge is the third pillar of a Frappe-native product suite:

```
┌──────────────┬──────────────┬───────────────────┐
│ TallyBridge  │ FieldPulse   │ FlowBridge        │
│              │              │                   │
│ Accounting   │ Field Force  │ Integration       │
│ UX for       │ GPS &        │ Studio +          │
│ ERPNext      │ Intelligence │ Marketplace       │
│              │              │                   │
│ ₹10L/mo     │ ₹25L/mo     │ ₹66L/mo          │
└──────────────┴──────────────┴───────────────────┘
         Combined potential: ₹1 Crore/month
```

Each product is independent and valuable on its own. Together they cover the three biggest pain points for ERPNext businesses: accounting UX, field operations, and integrations.

---

## The Vision Ceiling

> At scale, FlowBridge is not an integration tool.
> It is the **operating system for connected business** on Frappe.

**Revenue ceiling: ₹5–10 Crore/month** across Studio, API Platform, Data Platform, Enterprise, and Marketplace.

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_flowbridge/docs/performance.md
# FlowBridge — Performance Architecture

> This document is a cross-cutting reference. Every phase of the API Builder (15, 16, 17, 18) must be evaluated against the targets and strategies defined here. "Fast by default" is not a feature — it is a design constraint.

---

## Performance Targets (hard SLOs)

| Operation | P50 | P95 | P99 | Max |
|-----------|-----|-----|-----|-----|
| GET — L1 memory cache hit |  100,000 req/s |
| Uncached GET (Rust → MariaDB) | > 10,000 req/s |
| Writes (Go worker, single node) | > 5,000 req/s |
| Concurrent SSE connections (Rust) | > 50,000 per node |
| Concurrent WebSocket connections | > 20,000 per node |

---

## The Multi-Layer Cache Architecture

```
Request arrives at Rust/Axum
        │
        ▼
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  L1: In-Process Memory Cache (DashMap in Rust)    │  Hit:  saving)
```

Implemented in Rust via `tower-http::CompressionLayer` — transparent, automatic.

### Partial Response Streaming (large lists)

For endpoints returning > 1,000 rows:

```
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
Transfer-Encoding: chunked
Content-Type: application/json

[                              ← opening bracket sent immediately
{"name":"SINV-001",...},       ← rows streamed as computed
{"name":"SINV-002",...},       ← no buffering entire result set
...
{"name":"SINV-10000",...}
]                              ← closing bracket
```

Frontend gets first rows in  0`:

```
Cloudflare / CloudFront CDN
  → Cache-Control: public, max-age=300, stale-while-revalidate=60
  → ETag: "abc123"
  → Vary: Accept, Accept-Encoding, Accept-Language

CDN cache hit → 0 ms latency (served from edge, nearest to user)
CDN cache miss → hits Rust gateway → L1/L2/L3
```

CDN-friendly endpoints: product catalogs, item lists, public dashboards, dropdown data.

---

## Compute Optimization

### Python → Rust/Go for hot paths

| Operation | Python time | Rust time | Speedup |
|-----------|------------|-----------|---------|
| JSON serialize 1000 rows | ~15 ms | ~0.5 ms | 30× |
| HMAC-SHA256 verify | ~0.5 ms | ~0.01 ms | 50× |
| Rate limit check (Redis) | ~5 ms | ~0.05 ms (memory) | 100× |
| Filter condition evaluation | ~2 ms | ~0.05 ms | 40× |
| Cache key hash (SHA-256) | ~0.1 ms | ~0.005 ms | 20× |

**Rule:** Everything on the hot path (auth, rate limit, cache, simple GET) stays in Rust. Python is only called for: custom_logic snippets, Frappe DocType hooks, and complex business logic.

### Go Worker Concurrency Model

```go
// Configurable worker pool — tune based on MariaDB connection pool size
const MAX_WORKERS = 25

func main() {
    jobs := make(chan WriteJob, 1000)  // buffered channel — absorbs bursts

    for i := 0; i ", 100)
        for _, entry := range entries {
            jobs  dict:
    """Resolve all joins at the same depth level in parallel."""
    loader = BatchLoader()

    # Queue all joins for this level
    for join_cfg in join_configs:
        for row in parent_rows:
            loader.queue(join_cfg.join_doctype, join_cfg.link_field, row[join_cfg.parent_key])

    # One DB query per unique (doctype, link_field) — not per row
    loader.execute_all()

    # Distribute results back
    for row in parent_rows:
        for join_cfg in join_configs:
            row[join_cfg.alias] = loader.get(join_cfg.join_doctype, join_cfg.link_field, row[join_cfg.parent_key])

    return parent_rows
```

### Memoization within a Request

Same `(doctype, name)` fetched multiple times in one deep join traversal:

```python
_request_cache: dict[tuple, dict] = {}  # lives only for this request

def fetch_doc_cached(doctype, name):
    key = (doctype, name)
    if key not in _request_cache:
        _request_cache[key] = frappe.get_doc(doctype, name).as_dict()
    return _request_cache[key]
```

Eliminates duplicate fetches when Account → Parent Account → the same root account is referenced by multiple branches.

---

## Real-Time Performance (Phase 17)

### SSE at Scale

Rust can maintain **50,000+ concurrent SSE connections** on a single 2-core instance because:
- Each SSE connection is a tokio async task — no thread per connection
- Zero memory allocation per message (zero-copy write to socket)
- Redis subscription is shared — one Redis connection, one tokio task, fans out to all SSE clients in memory

```rust
// Fan-out: one Redis message → all subscribers in O(n) in-memory
async fn fan_out(
    slug: &str,
    message: &str,
    subscribers: &DashMap>>,
) {
    if let Some(senders) = subscribers.get(slug) {
        for sender in senders.iter() {
            let _ = sender.try_send(message.to_string());  // non-blocking
        }
    }
}
```

### WebSocket Compression

```
permessage-deflate extension:
  • Compresses each WS frame
  • 10KB JSON message → ~2KB on wire
  • CPU cost: ~0.1ms per message (acceptable)
  • Negotiated in WS handshake — clients that don't support it get uncompressed
```

### Redis Streams vs Pub/Sub for SSE

Phase 17 uses **Redis Streams** (not pub/sub) for the SSE/WS bridge:

| | Redis Pub/Sub | Redis Streams |
|--|--|--|
| Message persistence | ❌ fire and forget | ✅ stored, replayable |
| Reconnect recovery | ❌ missed messages lost | ✅ `Last-Event-ID` resumes |
| Consumer groups | ❌ no | ✅ yes (for write workers) |
| Memory | Lower | Higher (capped with MAXLEN) |

```
Stream key: fb:rt:{slug}
MAXLEN: 1000 entries per slug (older entries auto-trimmed)
SSE reconnect: XREAD COUNT 10 STREAMS fb:rt:{slug} {last_event_id}
```

---

## Benchmarking Suite (Phase 18)

Every release runs the full benchmark suite. Regressions block deployment.

### Tools
| Tool | What |
|------|------|
| `wrk` + Lua scripts | HTTP throughput (req/s, latency histogram) |
| `k6` | Realistic load scenarios (mix of GET/POST/WS) |
| `hey` | Quick p99 checks per endpoint |
| `cargo bench` | Rust microbenchmarks (auth, cache, serialization) |
| `go test -bench` | Go worker microbenchmarks |
| `pytest-benchmark` | Python path benchmarks (DataLoader, computed fields) |
| Grafana + Prometheus | Continuous production monitoring (Phase 16.29) |

### Benchmark Scenarios

```
Scenario 1: Cached GET storm
  1000 concurrent clients × 10,000 GET requests = 10M requests
  All hitting the same cached endpoint
  Pass criteria: p99  100k req/s

Scenario 2: Mixed read/write (realistic)
  80% GET (50% cache hit), 15% POST, 5% PATCH
  500 concurrent clients
  Pass criteria: GET p99  80% under normal load (check Prometheus `cache_hit_ratio`)
- [ ] `wrk -t4 -c100 -d30s` throughput > 1,000 req/s for this specific endpoint
- [ ] p99 latency  300k req/s combined
    │       │
    │       ├── Redis Cluster (3 nodes)            ← L2 cache + SSE pub/sub + write queue
    │       │
    │       ├── MariaDB Primary (1 node, 16 cores) ← writes
    │       ├── MariaDB Replica × 2 (8 cores each) ← reads, routed by ProxySQL
    │       │
    │       └── Go Write Workers (2 nodes, 8 cores) ← async write processing
    │
    ├── Frappe Gunicorn (4 nodes, existing)        ← custom_logic, DocType hooks, Desk
    │
    ├── ClickHouse (1 node, 8 cores)               ← analytics, materialized views
    │
    └── CDN (Cloudflare) → public endpoint caching at edge
```

**Scaling rule:**
- Cached GETs: scale Rust nodes (stateless, linear scaling)
- Uncached GETs: scale MariaDB read replicas + ProxySQL
- Writes: scale Go worker nodes + MariaDB primary vertical scaling
- SSE/WS: scale Rust nodes (each handles 50k connections)
- Heavy Python logic: scale Frappe gunicorn workers

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_flowbridge/docs/phase1.md
# Phase 1 — Studio Core

**Duration:** 3 months
**Goal:** Build the foundational integration builder inside Frappe/ERPNext
**Layer:** Studio (Build & Automate)

---

## What We're Building

The heart of FlowBridge — a visual flow designer that lets any ERPNext implementor build integrations without writing code.

---

## Deliverables

### 1. Visual Flow Designer
Drag & drop canvas with 5 core node types:

**TRIGGER nodes:**
- Webhook (inbound)
- Schedule (cron)
- DocType event (on_submit, on_update etc.)
- Email received
- File uploaded
- Manual trigger

**ACTION nodes:**
- Create DocType record
- Update DocType record
- HTTP API call
- Send Email
- Send WhatsApp
- Send SMS
- Generate PDF
- Run Script
- Call another Flow

**LOGIC nodes:**
- Filter / Condition
- Split / Branch
- Loop
- Merge
- Delay / Wait
- Error handler

**TRANSFORM nodes:**
- Field mapper
- Formula calculator
- JSON transformer
- Date formatter
- AI transformer ✨ (stub, full in Phase 3)

### 2. Universal HTTP Connector (Custom Connector Builder)
When a pre-built connector doesn't exist, build your own in minutes — no code required.

#### 2a. Endpoint Declaration
- Any HTTP method: GET / POST / PUT / PATCH / DELETE
- Dynamic URL path params: `/shipments/{shipment_id}/items/{item_id}`
- Query params (static, dynamic from flow context, conditional — only add if field is non-null)
- Custom headers (static values or injected from credentials/flow context)

#### 2b. Authentication Types (all 9)
| Type | What user fills in |
|---|---|
| Bearer Token | Token → stored in credential vault |
| API Key Header | Header name + key value |
| API Key Query Param | Param name + key value |
| Basic Auth | Username + password |
| OAuth2 Client Credentials | Client ID, Secret, Token URL |
| OAuth2 Authorization Code | Client ID, Secret, Auth URL, Token URL |
| HMAC Signature | Secret + algorithm (SHA256/MD5) + header name to write |
| Custom Headers | Any arbitrary key-value headers |
| No Auth | Public endpoints |

#### 2c. Request Body Formats
- **JSON** — with nested variable injection: `{"receiver": {"name": "{{customer_name}}"}}`
- **Form URL-encoded** — for payment gateways (PayU, old APIs): `amount=5000&currency=INR`
- **XML / Template** — for Tally, government APIs: full XML template with `{{field}}` injection and `{{#each items}}` array loops
- **Multipart / form-data** — for file upload APIs (e-sign, document portals): attach ERPNext File doctype binary alongside JSON metadata
- **Raw text** — for APIs expecting plain string body
- **No body** — for GET/DELETE requests

#### 2d. Nested Key Mapper (Rust path resolver)
The core of the Universal Connector — maps ERPNext fields to any nested API structure and back:

**Flat → Nested (ERPNext → API request):**
```
customer_name        →  shipment.receiver.name
mobile_no            →  shipment.receiver.contact.phone
address_line1        →  shipment.receiver.address.line1
items[*].item_code   →  order.line_items[*].sku          ← array
items[*].qty         →  order.line_items[*].quantity      ← array
items[*].rate        →  order.line_items[*].unit_price    ← array
```

**Nested → Flat (API response → ERPNext):**
```
data.shipment.id            →  custom_tracking_id
data.shipment.status.code   →  custom_status
data.pricing.total          →  grand_total
errors[0].message           →  flow_run.error_message
```

**Path notation features:**
- Dot notation for nesting: `shipment.receiver.address.pin`
- Array wildcard: `items[*].sku` — maps entire child table array
- Index access: `errors[0].message` — first element only
- XPath for XML responses: `//ShipmentResponse/TrackingNumber/text()`

Written in Rust — handles 50,000-row child tables (SAP BAPI responses) without blocking.

#### 2e. Conditional Mapping
Fields mapped only when a condition is met:
```
gst_number       →  tax_id           ONLY IF  country == "India"
discount_amount  →  discount         ONLY IF  discount_amount > 0
items[*].batch   →  items[*].batch_no ONLY IF  has_batch_no == true
shipping_address →  address_display  IF NOT NULL  ELSE  billing_address
```

#### 2f. Field Transform Library (Phase 1 core set)

**Math:** `multiply(n)`, `divide(n)`, `round(n)`, `abs()`, `sum(array_field)`

**String:** `uppercase`, `lowercase`, `titlecase`, `trim`, `concat(a, sep, b)`, `substr(start, len)`, `pad_left(char, width)`

**Date:** `date_format("DD-MM-YYYY")`, `date_format("YYYYMMDD")`, `add_days(n)`, `to_unix_timestamp`, `from_unix_timestamp`, `timezone_convert("Asia/Kolkata")`

**Type coercion:** `to_string`, `to_integer`, `to_float`, `to_boolean`, `parse_json(field)`, `stringify_json(obj)`

**Conditional:** `if(condition, true_val, false_val)`, `coalesce(f1, f2, "default")`, `nullif(value, "N/A")`

**Lookup table:** map a field value through a static dictionary:
```
"DELIVERED" → "Delivered"
"IN_TRANSIT" → "In Transit"
"RTO"        → "Returned"
```

**Default values & null handling:** set a fallback if source field is null/empty

All transforms execute in **Rust** — no Python overhead.

#### 2g. Response Handling
- **Success status codes** — configurable (e.g., 200, 201, 202)
- **Error status codes** — 4xx/5xx trigger error handler
- **Response formats:** JSON (default), XML (XPath extraction), plain text
- **Error message extraction** — define the nested path to the error message: `error.details[0].message`
- **Write response fields back to ERPNext** — using response mapping (nested → flat)

#### 2h. Multi-Step API Chains
Some integrations need sequential API calls where each depends on the previous:
```
Step 1: GET /customers?email={{email}}
        Extract: response[0].id → {{ext_customer_id}}

Step 2: POST /orders
        Body: { customer_id: "{{ext_customer_id}}", ... }
        Extract: response.order_id → {{ext_order_id}}

Step 3: POST /orders/{{ext_order_id}}/fulfillments
```
Declared as a single connector action — executor runs steps sequentially, passes context forward.

#### 2i. Basic Retry
- Retry on 5xx: fixed attempts (configurable, default 3)
- Retry on network timeout: configurable attempts
- Delay between retries: fixed seconds (advanced backoff added in Phase 6)
- On all retries exhausted: route to flow error handler node

#### 2j. Inbound Webhook Security (Basic)
For the Rust/Axum listener URL generated per connector:
- **HMAC-SHA256 signature verification** (Shopify, Razorpay, Stripe style)
- **HMAC-SHA1** (for older APIs like WooCommerce)
- Header name to read signature from: configurable (`X-Shopify-Hmac-Sha256`, etc.)
- **Return 200 in  500
Step 3: Transform ✅ — Mapped 12 fields
Step 4: Action  ❌ — ERROR: Customer not found
         → Suggested fix: Create customer first
```
- Step-by-step execution trace
- Real payload inspection at each node
- Error explanation in plain English
- Suggested fixes
- Sandbox mode (test without affecting real data)

### 5. ERPNext Webhook Integration
- Register FlowBridge as webhook receiver for any DocType
- Replace Frappe's native one-shot webhooks with multi-step flows
- Handle imports (Frappe webhook bug workaround — independent event queue)

### 6. Credential Vault (Basic)
- AES-256 encryption at rest
- Credentials never exposed in UI
- Per-flow credential scoping
- Credential rotation reminders
- Audit log of credential usage

### 7. Flow Analytics & Monitoring (Basic)
- Total runs / successful / failed
- Average execution time
- Failure breakdown by error type
- Auto-retry on failure (configurable)
- Alert on failure threshold breach
- Execution history with full payload log

---

## Architecture Work (Phase 1)

| Component | Language | What to Build |
|---|---|---|
| Flow DocTypes | Python/Frappe | Flow, FlowNode, FlowRun, Connector, ConnectorEndpoint, Credential |
| Connector config DocTypes | Python/Frappe | ConnectorAuth, EndpointMapping, FieldTransform, RetryPolicy |
| Studio UI | React (Frappe desk) | Canvas, node palette, field mapper, test console |
| Universal Connector UI | React | Endpoint builder, nested key mapper, transform editor, sandbox |
| Webhook receiver | Rust/Axum | Inbound endpoint, HMAC-SHA256/SHA1 verify, queue to executor |
| Path resolver engine | Rust | Dot-notation nested key resolver, array wildcard mapper, XPath |
| Transform engine | Rust | Full transform library (math, string, date, lookup, type coercion) |
| Flow executor | Go | Node graph runner, multi-step chain executor, retry logic, telemetry |
| Connector SDK | Go | Interface definition, 10 connector implementations |
| Event queue | Redis | Durable event persistence, sandbox isolation |

---

## Key Differentiators vs Frappe Native Webhooks

> **"FlowBridge makes inbound webhooks a first-class citizen — something Frappe has never done."**
>
> Frappe's native webhooks are outbound-only. Developers receiving external webhooks must write custom `@frappe.whitelist()` methods, wire business logic manually, handle errors manually, and debug blind. FlowBridge replaces all of this with a visual, tested, observable flow.

| Feature | Frappe Native | FlowBridge Phase 1 |
|---|---|---|
| Direction | Outbound only | Inbound + Outbound + Bidirectional |
| Visual builder | None | Drag & drop canvas |
| Multi-step flows | One action per webhook | Unlimited chained steps |
| Inbound webhooks | None (manual `@frappe.whitelist()`) | First-class listener nodes |
| Retry logic | None | Auto-retry with backoff |
| Error handling | None | Per-step error handlers |
| Field mapping | Basic Jinja templating | Visual mapper + AI suggest |
| Pre-built connectors | None | 50+ (Phase 1: 10) |
| Custom API builder | None | Generic HTTP + webhook listener builder |
| Testing / sandbox | None | Step-by-step sandbox debugger |
| Analytics | Basic log only | Full flow health dashboard |
| Rate limiting | None | Per-consumer rate limits |
| Marketplace | None | Publish & monetize flows |
| AI builder | None | Natural language → flow |
| Credential vault | None | AES-256 encrypted vault |
| Triggered by imports | Broken (known bug) | Event queue independent of Frappe import |

# Phase 10 — Developer Experience (DX) Platform

**Type:** External developer tools — NOT a Frappe app
**Distribution:** PyPI / NPM / GitHub / VS Code Marketplace / Homebrew
**Duration:** 3 months
**Goal:** Make FlowBridge the best-in-class developer tool in the Frappe ecosystem — drive connector contributions, lower integration barrier, build the developer community
**Depends on:** Phase 1 (core), Phase 2 (Connector SDK + Marketplace)

---

## Why This Matters

The Connector Marketplace flywheel (Phase 7) only spins fast if developers can:
- Build connectors without a full ERPNext install
- Test locally before deploying
- Use languages and tools they already know
- Integrate FlowBridge into their CI/CD pipelines

Phase 10 is the developer infrastructure that makes all of that possible.

---

## Deliverables

### 1. FlowBridge CLI (`flowbridge-cli`)

**Install:**
```bash
# macOS
brew install flowbridge-cli

# Linux
curl -fsSL https://install.flowbridge.io | sh

# Python (alternative)
pip install flowbridge-cli
```

**Commands:**
```bash
# Local dev environment
flowbridge dev start            # Start local gateway + executor + mock ERPNext
flowbridge dev stop
flowbridge dev status

# Connector development
flowbridge connector new        # Scaffold a new connector.json
flowbridge connector validate ./my-connector.json
flowbridge connector test ./my-connector.json \
  --payload ./fixtures/order.json \
  --mock-response ./fixtures/api-response.json

# Flow management
flowbridge flow list            # List flows on connected site
flowbridge flow run   # Trigger flow manually
flowbridge flow logs  # Tail live logs
flowbridge flow export  --output ./flow.json

# Deploy
flowbridge deploy connector ./my-connector.json \
  --site yoursite.erpnext.com \
  --api-key $FB_API_KEY

flowbridge deploy flow ./my-flow.json \
  --site yoursite.erpnext.com \
  --env production

# Testing
flowbridge test ./tests/         # Run flow test suite
flowbridge test --watch          # Re-run on file change (TDD)
flowbridge test --coverage       # Coverage report

# Publish to Marketplace
flowbridge publish connector ./my-connector.json
flowbridge publish flow ./my-flow.json
```

Built in **Go** — single binary, cross-platform,  {
  console.log(event.type, event.step, event.status);
});

// React hook
import { useFlowRun } from "@flowbridge/react";

function FlowStatus({ flowId }: { flowId: string }) {
  const { run, isLoading } = useFlowRun(flowId);
  return {run?.status};
}
```

### 5. Go SDK (for Connector Developers)

```bash
go get github.com/flowbridge/flowbridge-go
```

```go
package main

import (
    fb "github.com/flowbridge/flowbridge-go"
)

// Implement the Connector interface (Phase 7 Connector SDK)
type DelhiveryConnector struct{}

func (c *DelhiveryConnector) Name() string    { return "Delhivery" }
func (c *DelhiveryConnector) Version() string { return "1.0.0" }
func (c *DelhiveryConnector) AuthType() fb.AuthType { return fb.Bearer }

func (c *DelhiveryConnector) Execute(
    ctx context.Context,
    action string,
    params map[string]any,
    creds fb.Credentials,
) (*fb.Result, error) {
    switch action {
    case "create_shipment":
        return c.createShipment(ctx, params, creds)
    }
    return nil, fb.ErrUnknownAction
}

func main() {
    fb.RegisterConnector(&DelhiveryConnector{})
    fb.Run()  // starts connector server, handles lifecycle
}
```

### 7. Automated Flow Testing Framework

Write tests for flows like unit tests — run in CI:

```python
# tests/test_shopify_sync.py
from flowbridge.testing import FlowTest, MockConnector

class TestShopifySync(FlowTest):
    flow = "shopify-sync"

    def test_new_order_creates_sales_order(self):
        result = self.run_flow(
            trigger_payload=self.load_fixture("shopify_order.json"),
            mocks={
                "shopify": MockConnector(
                    "get_customer", returns={"id": "CUST-001", "name": "John Doe"}
                ),
                "erpnext": MockConnector(
                    "create_sales_order", returns={"name": "SO-2024-001"}
                )
            }
        )

        self.assert_step_passed(2)
        self.assert_field_mapped("customer_name", "John Doe")
        self.assert_field_mapped("grand_total", 5000.0)
        self.assert_erpnext_called("Sales Order", "create", times=1)

    def test_missing_customer_triggers_error_handler(self):
        result = self.run_flow(
            trigger_payload=self.load_fixture("shopify_order_unknown_customer.json"),
            mocks={
                "erpnext": MockConnector("get_customer", raises=NotFoundError)
            }
        )
        self.assert_step_failed(2)
        self.assert_error_handler_triggered()

    def test_large_order_requires_approval(self):
        result = self.run_flow(
            trigger_payload=self.load_fixture("shopify_order_large.json")
        )
        self.assert_step_paused(3)  # human-in-loop pause
        self.assert_approval_requested_to("cfo@company.com")
```

**CI integration:**
```yaml
# .github/workflows/flowbridge.yml
- name: Run FlowBridge Tests
  run: flowbridge test ./tests/ --site ${{ secrets.STAGING_SITE }}
  env:
    FB_API_KEY: ${{ secrets.FB_API_KEY }}
```

```
Test results:
  PASS  test_new_order_creates_sales_order         (340ms)
  PASS  test_missing_customer_triggers_error       (120ms)
  PASS  test_large_order_requires_approval         (89ms)
  FAIL  test_duplicate_order_deduplication         (210ms)
        AssertionError: Expected flow to skip, but it ran
  
  4 tests | 3 passed | 1 failed | Coverage: 78%
```

# Phase 11 — B2B EDI & Supply Chain Compliance

**App name:** `flowbridge_edi`
**Type:** Separate Frappe app
**Duration:** 4 months
**Goal:** Make ERPNext EDI-compliant for retail, pharma, auto, and export supply chains — a market nobody in the Frappe ecosystem touches
**Depends on:** Phase 1 (Universal Connector), Phase 5 (Data Platform)

---

## The Market

EDI (Electronic Data Interchange) is **mandatory**, not optional, for:

| Vertical | Requirement |
|---|---|
| Supplying to Amazon / Flipkart / Walmart | EDI 850/810/856 compliance |
| Pharmaceutical supply chain | CDSCO, DAVA portal, batch traceability |
| Auto / manufacturing OEM suppliers | EDIFACT JIT delivery orders |
| Export documentation | DGFT, customs (ICEGATE), shipping lines |
| Modern retail (D-Mart, Reliance) | Vendor compliance EDI |

Custom integrations for EDI cost ₹5L–20L per trading partner today. `flowbridge_edi` makes it a ₹9,999/mo SaaS install.

---

## EDI Standards Supported

### X12 (North America / Amazon / Walmart)
```
850  Purchase Order           ← receive from buyer
810  Invoice                  ← send to buyer
856  Advance Ship Notice (ASN) ← send before delivery
940  Warehouse Shipping Order  ← from 3PL
997  Functional Acknowledgment ← auto-generated
```

### EDIFACT (International / India Export)
```
ORDERS   Purchase Order
INVOIC   Invoice
DESADV   Despatch Advice (ASN equivalent)
APERAK   Application Error & Acknowledgment
IFTMIN   Freight booking instruction
CUSCAR   Customs cargo report (export)
```

### India-Specific
```
GST e-Invoice (IRP / NIC)   ← already in Phase 4
E-way Bill                  ← already in Phase 4
ICEGATE (Customs)           ← import/export clearance
DGFT (Directorate of Foreign Trade) ← export licence, RCMC
```

---

## Deliverables

### 1. EDI Message Studio (Visual Mapper)

Map ERPNext DocType fields → EDI segments visually — same paradigm as Universal Connector field mapper:

```
ERPNext Purchase Order  →  X12 850 Purchase Order

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ SEGMENT MAPPING                                     │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ BEG01  Transaction Type  →  "00" (static)           │
│ BEG03  PO Number         →  name                    │
│ BEG05  PO Date           →  transaction_date        │
│ DTM01  Date Qualifier    →  "002" (delivery date)   │
│ DTM02  Date              →  schedule_date           │
│ N1*ST  Ship-To Name      →  shipping_address.name   │
│ N3     Address Line 1    →  shipping_address.line1  │
│ N4*CT  City              →  shipping_address.city   │
│ N4*ST  State             →  shipping_address.state  │
│ N4*ZP  ZIP               →  shipping_address.pin    │
│ PO1    Line Items        →  items[*]                 │
│   PO102 Qty              →  items[*].qty             │
│   PO104 Unit Price       →  items[*].rate            │
│   PO107 Buyer Part No    →  items[*].item_code       │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

[Generate Sample] [Validate] [Test with Trading Partner]
```

### 2. Trading Partner Management
```
Trading Partners:
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Amazon Seller Central      [X12 — 850/810/856]      │
│ ├── ISA Sender ID:        123456789                  │
│ ├── GS Application ID:    AMZN                      │
│ ├── Protocol:             AS2                        │
│ ├── AS2 Endpoint:         https://edi.amazon.com/   │
│ ├── Our Certificate:      [Uploaded ✅]              │
│ ├── Their Certificate:    [Uploaded ✅]              │
│ ├── SLA: 856 ASN:         within 24h of shipment    │
│ └── Compliance Score:     98.7%  [View report]      │
│                                                     │
│ Tata Motors (OEM Customer) [EDIFACT — ORDERS/DESADV]│
│ ├── Partner ID:           TATAMOTORS-EDI            │
│ ├── Protocol:             SFTP                      │
│ └── SLA: DESADV:          4h before delivery        │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
[+ Add Trading Partner]
```

Per trading partner:
- EDI standard (X12 / EDIFACT / custom)
- Interchange identifiers (ISA, GS for X12 — UNB, UNG for EDIFACT)
- Transport protocol (AS2 / SFTP / VAN / email)
- SLA rules (which message types, within what time)
- Certificate management for AS2
- Test mode (sandbox EDI endpoint for UAT)

### 3. AS2 Protocol Engine (Rust)

AS2 is the standard secure HTTP-based EDI transport:

```
Send EDI document via AS2:
  1. Serialize ERPNext data → EDI format (X12/EDIFACT)
  2. Sign with our private key (S/MIME PKCS#7)
  3. Encrypt with partner's public key
  4. POST to partner's AS2 endpoint
  5. Receive MDN (Message Disposition Notification)
  6. Verify MDN signature
  7. Store: sent ✅ / error ❌

Receive EDI document via AS2:
  1. Partner POSTs to our AS2 endpoint
  2. Decrypt with our private key
  3. Verify partner's signature
  4. Parse EDI → structured data
  5. Map to ERPNext → create/update DocType record
  6. Send synchronous or async MDN acknowledgment
```

**Why Rust:** AS2 requires S/MIME cryptography (PKCS#7 signing + CMS encryption). Rust's `cms`, `openssl` and `pem` crates handle this efficiently. A single AS2 transaction involves 4–6 crypto operations — must be fast.

### 4. EDI Parsing Engine (Rust)

Parse raw EDI into structured data and serialize structured data back to EDI:

**X12 parsing:**
```
Input:  ISA*00*          *00*          *ZZ*123456789      *ZZ*987654321      *230101*1200*^*00501*000000001*0*P*>~GS*PO*123456789*987654321*20230101*1200*1*X*005010~ST*850*0001~BEG*00*SA*PO-2024-001**20230101~...~SE*47*0001~GE*1*1~IEA*1*000000001~

Output: {
  "transaction_type": "850",
  "po_number": "PO-2024-001",
  "po_date": "2023-01-01",
  "items": [
    {"qty": 100, "unit": "EA", "price": 250.00, "buyer_part": "SKU-001"},
    ...
  ]
}
```

**EDIFACT parsing:**
```
Input:  UNB+UNOC:3+SENDER:14+RECEIVER:14+230101:1200+1'UNH+1+ORDERS:D:96A:UN'BGM+220+ORD001+9'DTM+137:20230101:102'...UNZ+1+1'

Output: { "order_number": "ORD001", "date": "2023-01-01", ... }
```

Error handling:
- Invalid segment → reject with APERAK/997 error acknowledgment
- Unknown segment → log + alert, continue processing rest of document
- Schema validation against trading partner's spec subset

### 5. Automated EDI Flows (ERPNext Events → EDI)

Pre-built flow templates for common EDI scenarios:

**Scenario 1: Amazon — receive PO → create ERPNext Purchase Order**
```
[AS2 Inbound Trigger: 850 from Amazon]
  ↓
[Parse X12 850 → structured data]
  ↓
[Map: buyer_part_no → ERPNext item_code (lookup table)]
  ↓
[Create Purchase Order in ERPNext]
  ↓
[Send 997 Acknowledgment back to Amazon] (auto)
  ↓
[Alert: "New PO from Amazon — ₹4.2L — 14 items"]
```

**Scenario 2: Tata Motors — ship goods → send EDIFACT DESADV**
```
[ERPNext Trigger: Delivery Note submitted]
  ↓
[Check: is customer a EDIFACT trading partner?]
  ↓
[Map Delivery Note → EDIFACT DESADV segments]
  ↓
[Validate against Tata Motors spec]
  ↓
[Send via SFTP to Tata Motors EDI server]
  ↓
[Update Delivery Note: EDI_SENT = true, sent_at = now()]
  ↓
[SLA check: was this sent within 4 hours of dispatch?]
```

**Scenario 3: Export — generate customs documentation**
```
[ERPNext Trigger: Sales Invoice submitted for export customer]
  ↓
[Generate ICEGATE cargo declaration]
  ↓
[Submit to ICEGATE portal API]
  ↓
[Receive Shipping Bill number]
  ↓
[Write back to ERPNext Sales Invoice]
  ↓
[Generate DGFT export data for RCMC renewal]
```

### 6. EDI Compliance Dashboard

```
EDI Compliance Overview — Last 30 Days

Amazon Vendor Central
├── 850 POs Received:      234    ✅
├── 997 Acks Sent:         234    ✅ 100%  (avg 8 seconds)
├── 810 Invoices Sent:     234    ✅ 100%
├── 856 ASNs Sent:         231    ⚠️  98.7%  (3 late)
│   └── Late ASNs: [View] → all 3 were on 15 Apr (server issue)
└── Compliance Score:      98.7%  [Amazon requires > 98%]

Tata Motors
├── ORDERS Received:       89     ✅
├── DESADV Sent:           89     ✅ 100%  (avg 2.1 hours)
└── Compliance Score:     100%   ✅

Alerts:
├── ⚠️  Amazon compliance at 98.7% — minimum is 98% — 2 more late ASNs = penalty
└── ✅  All other partners compliant
```

### 7. EDI Audit Trail (Tamper-Proof)

Every EDI transaction stored with:
- Raw EDI document (exactly as sent/received)
- Parsed structured data
- Mapping applied
- ERPNext record created/updated
- MDN/997 acknowledgment
- Timestamps at every step
- Digital signature verification result

Required for pharma (21 CFR Part 11), DGFT audits, tax assessments.

---

## Architecture Work

| Component | Language | What to Build |
|---|---|---|
| EDI parser (X12 + EDIFACT) | Rust | Segment tokenizer, schema validator, structured output |
| EDI serializer | Rust | Structured data → valid X12/EDIFACT with correct delimiters |
| AS2 engine | Rust | S/MIME sign/encrypt, MDN generation/verification, HTTP server |
| Trading partner DocTypes | Python/Frappe | TradingPartner, EDIMapping, EDITransaction, ComplianceSLA |
| EDI Studio UI | React | Visual segment mapper, partner config, compliance dashboard |
| Automated EDI flows | Python/Go | Pre-built flow templates for 850/810/856/DESADV/ORDERS |
| SFTP transport | Go | SFTP client/server for file-based EDI exchange |
| Compliance engine | Go | SLA tracking, breach detection, alert generation |
| Audit trail | Rust + Frappe | Tamper-proof log with hash chain |
| ICEGATE/DGFT connectors | Python | Indian export portal API integrations |

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_flowbridge/docs/phase12.md
# Phase 12 — Financial Automation Layer

**App name:** `flowbridge_finance`
**Type:** Separate Frappe app (or merge into TallyBridge)
**Duration:** 4 months
**Goal:** Turn ERPNext into a financial automation platform — bank reconciliation, payment automation, GST auto-filing, forex management
**Depends on:** Phase 1 (Universal Connector), Phase 5 (Data Platform), Phase 6 (Human-in-Loop)

---

## The Market

Every ERPNext business has these unsolved pain points:

| Pain | Current solution | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Bank reconciliation | Manual, done monthly, takes 2–5 days | ₹15,000–50,000/mo (accountant time) |
| Vendor payment processing | Manual approval → manual bank transfer | 3–7 day delay, errors |
| GST return filing | CA manually compiles + uploads | ₹5,000–20,000/month |
| Forex rate updates | Someone checks RBI site, enters manually | Daily error-prone task |
| TDS certificates | Downloaded manually, matched manually | Week-long exercise quarterly |

`flowbridge_finance` automates all of this — inside ERPNext, no external tool needed.

---

## Deliverables

### 1. Bank API Connectivity

#### India Account Aggregator (AA) Framework
```
Connect once → pull bank transactions from ALL accounts:

Supported banks (via AA network):
├── HDFC Bank
├── ICICI Bank
├── SBI
├── Axis Bank
├── Kotak Mahindra Bank
├── Yes Bank
├── IndusInd Bank
└── 50+ more via NBFC-AA

Data pulled:
├── Bank account transactions (debit/credit, narration, date, amount)
├── Loan repayment schedule
├── Fixed deposit details
└── Credit card statements (for corporate cards)

Consent model:
├── Customer gives consent via AA mobile app
├── FlowBridge pulls data every hour (or on demand)
├── Consent can be revoked anytime
└── DPDP Act compliant (Phase 6 compliance layer)
```

#### Direct Bank API Connectors
```
RazorpayX (Business Banking):
├── Account balance
├── Transaction history
├── Initiate NEFT/RTGS/IMPS/UPI payouts
└── Bulk payout upload

HDFC SmartHub (Corporate Banking API):
├── Account statement download
├── NEFT/RTGS initiation
└── Bulk payment file generation (Corporate format)

Open Banking (startup-friendly):
├── Virtual accounts
├── Payout APIs
└── Transaction webhooks

SWIFT MT940 / MT942:
├── Corporate bank statement format
├── Auto-import on SFTP drop (Phase 5 SFTP connector)
└── Map to ERPNext Bank Transaction
```

### 2. Auto-Reconciliation Engine

The single biggest time-saver for any ERPNext finance team:

```
Reconciliation Flow (runs automatically every hour):

SOURCE A: Bank Transactions (from AA / direct API / MT940)
SOURCE B: ERPNext Payment Entries + Sales Invoices + Purchase Invoices

MATCHING RULES (applied in order):
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Rule 1: Exact match                                  │
│   Amount matches exactly + date within ±2 days      │
│   Reference in narration matches invoice number     │
│   → Auto-reconcile. Confidence: 99%                 │
│                                                      │
│ Rule 2: Fuzzy reference match                        │
│   "INV-2024-0091" found anywhere in bank narration  │
│   Amount matches ±5% (forex fluctuation allowed)    │
│   → Auto-reconcile. Confidence: 92%                 │
│                                                      │
│ Rule 3: Bulk payment match                           │
│   One bank credit = sum of multiple invoices        │
│   Customer name appears in narration                │
│   → Auto-match to invoice set. Confidence: 85%      │
│                                                      │
│ Rule 4: Partial payment                             │
│   Bank amount  ₹50,000 need CFO WhatsApp approval (Phase 9)

  Action:
  ├── Create Payment Entry in ERPNext
  ├── Initiate payout via RazorpayX / HDFC API
  ├── Record UTR number on Payment Entry
  └── Notify vendor via email/WhatsApp

  Result:
  → Same-day payment for approved invoices (no manual bank login needed)
  → Full audit trail: who approved, when initiated, UTR confirmed
```

**Payment methods supported:**
```
NEFT  — up to ₹2L, T+1 settlement
RTGS  — above ₹2L, same-day (only bank hours)
IMPS  — 24/7, up to ₹5L per transaction
UPI   — for small vendors, instant, up to ₹1L
Bulk  — batch payment file to bank (HDFC/ICICI corporate format)
```

**Dual approval workflow (WhatsApp — Phase 9 integration):**
```
Bot → CFO WhatsApp:
"💰 Payment Approval Required

 Vendor: Tata Steel Pvt Ltd
 Invoice: PI-2024-0234
 Amount: ₹6,23,450
 Due: 28 Apr 2026
 Bank: ICICI RTGS

 [✅ Approve] [❌ Reject] [📋 View Invoice]"

CFO taps ✅ → RTGS initiated automatically
```

### 4. GST Auto-Filing Flows

```
GSTR-1 (Monthly — outward supplies):
  Trigger: 25th of month at 6:00 AM

  Step 1: Pull all Sales Invoices for the month from ERPNext
  Step 2: Aggregate by:
    ├── B2B supplies (GSTIN-wise)
    ├── B2C large (>₹2.5L)
    ├── B2C others
    ├── Zero-rated / exempt / nil-rated
    └── HSN/SAC summary
  Step 3: Generate GSTR-1 JSON (NIC schema v1.3)
  Step 4: Validate JSON against NIC schema
  Step 5: Upload to GST Portal via IRP API
  Step 6: Fetch ARN (Acknowledgment Reference Number)
  Step 7: Store ARN in ERPNext Company doctype
  Step 8: Send WhatsApp to CA:
    "GSTR-1 for March 2026 filed ✅
     ARN: AA123456789012
     Total taxable: ₹82.4L
     Total tax: ₹14.8L
     View filed return: [link]"

GSTR-3B (Monthly — summary + payment):
  Same flow — but also:
  Step 9: Calculate net GST payable (output - ITC)
  Step 10: If payable > 0:
    → Create Journal Entry for GST payment
    → Initiate NEFT to GST PMT challan
    → Update GSTR-3B with payment reference
```

### 5. TDS Automation

```
TDS DEDUCTION (on vendor payment):
  Trigger: Purchase Invoice approved for payment

  Check:
  ├── Is vendor subject to TDS? (PAN + vendor category)
  ├── Has threshold been crossed? (Section 194C: > ₹30,000 single / ₹1L aggregate)
  └── What rate? (2% for company, 1% for individual)

  Action:
  ├── Create TDS Journal Entry in ERPNext
  ├── Pay net amount to vendor (gross - TDS)
  ├── Accumulate in TDS Payable account
  └── Track for quarterly 26Q filing

FORM 26Q (Quarterly — TDS return):
  Trigger: 7th of month after quarter end

  Step 1: Aggregate all TDS deductions by deductee PAN
  Step 2: Generate Form 26Q (FVU format for TRACES)
  Step 3: Validate via TRACES utility (local Java tool — invoked via CLI)
  Step 4: Upload to TRACES portal
  Step 5: Download Form 16A certificates
  Step 6: Email Form 16A to each vendor
```

### 6. Forex & Treasury Automation

```
FOREX RATE UPDATE (daily):
  Trigger: 9:00 AM every business day

  Step 1: GET https://api.rbi.org.in/exchange-rates
  Step 2: Update ERPNext Currency Exchange for USD, EUR, GBP, AED, SGD
  Step 3: If rate moved > 1% since yesterday:
    → Alert: "USD/INR moved ₹0.87 today (1.04%) — 3 open FCY invoices affected"
    → Show: unrealised gain/loss on open FCY receivables

INVOICE REVALUATION (month-end):
  Trigger: Last business day of month

  Step 1: Find all open FCY Sales Invoices
  Step 2: Get current exchange rate vs rate at invoice date
  Step 3: Calculate unrealised gain/loss per invoice
  Step 4: Create Journal Entry (FASB 52 / Ind AS 21 compliant)
  Step 5: Post to Forex Gain/Loss account
  Step 6: Generate forex exposure report → email to CFO

FORWARD CONTRACT TRACKING:
  Step 1: Import forward contract details (maturity, rate, amount, bank)
  Step 2: Match to open FCY invoices (hedged vs unhedged)
  Step 3: Alert 7 days before maturity: "Forward contract matures 28 Apr"
  Step 4: On maturity: create settlement Journal Entry
```

### 7. Cash Flow Forecasting (AI-Assisted)

```
Cash Flow Forecast — Next 30 Days

Inflows (expected):
├── Overdue receivables (likely):    ₹12.4L  (aged < 30 days, 85% collection rate)
├── Overdue receivables (uncertain): ₹3.2L   (aged 30–60 days, 60% rate)
├── Advance orders (confirmed):      ₹8.1L
└── GST refund pending:              ₹1.8L
                              Total: ₹25.5L

Outflows (committed):
├── Approved vendor payments:        ₹18.2L
├── Salary (25th):                   ₹6.4L
├── GST payment (20th):              ₹2.1L
└── EMI (bank loan):                 ₹1.5L
                              Total: ₹28.2L

⚠️  Net cash position on 28 Apr: -₹2.7L
→ Suggestion: Delay ₹3L Tata Steel payment by 5 days (within credit terms)
→ Or: Collect from Customer A (₹4L, 8 days overdue) proactively
```

Uses Default AI Engine (Phase AI) — IsolationForest on historical collection patterns, not an LLM.

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_flowbridge/docs/phase13.md
# Phase 13 — Business Intelligence & Analytics Layer

**App name:** `flowbridge_analytics`
**Type:** Separate Frappe app (also deployable as standalone web app)
**Duration:** 3 months
**Goal:** Turn FlowBridge's unique position as the data middleware into a business intelligence platform — auto-dashboards, natural language queries, scheduled reports, anomaly alerts with root cause
**Depends on:** Phase 1 (data flowing through FlowBridge), Phase 3 (AI engine), Phase 5 (ClickHouse data store)

---

## The Unique Advantage

FlowBridge is the only system that sees data flowing across ALL of a business's systems simultaneously:

```
Shopify orders → FlowBridge → ERPNext Sales Orders
Razorpay payments → FlowBridge → ERPNext Payment Entries
Shiprocket shipments → FlowBridge → ERPNext Delivery Notes
WhatsApp leads → FlowBridge → ERPNext Leads
Bank statements → FlowBridge → ERPNext Bank Transactions
```

No BI tool has this — they all need to pull from each system separately.
FlowBridge already HAS the unified data stream. Phase 13 makes it queryable.

---

## Deliverables

### 1. FlowBridge Data Warehouse (Built-In ClickHouse)

Every flow run auto-populates the warehouse — no ETL setup needed:

```
Tables auto-created and populated:

fb_events          ← every trigger received (type, source, timestamp, size)
fb_flow_runs       ← every flow execution (flow_id, start, end, status, steps)
fb_field_maps      ← every field mapping applied (from_field, to_field, from_val, to_val)
fb_api_calls       ← every outbound API call (connector, endpoint, latency, status)
fb_errors          ← every failure (type, message, flow, step, resolved_at)
fb_records_synced  ← every ERPNext record created/updated (doctype, name, flow, ts)
fb_connector_health← latency, uptime, error_rate per connector per hour
```

Zero setup — data accumulates automatically from day 1 of using FlowBridge.

### 2. Auto-Generated Business Dashboards

FlowBridge detects which connectors are active and auto-builds the right dashboards:

**Revenue Dashboard** (auto-built when Shopify + Razorpay connectors active):
```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Revenue Intelligence — Last 30 Days                    │

│  Orders:          1,847     ↑12%                        │
│  Avg Order Value: ₹6,713    ↑5.4%                       │
│  Payment Success: 97.8%     ↓0.3pp ← alert             │
│                                                         │
│  [Revenue trend chart — daily bars]                     │
│                                                         │
│  Top Products (by revenue synced):                      │
│  1. Product A   ₹18.4L  (234 orders)                   │
│  2. Product B   ₹12.1L  (189 orders)                   │
│  3. Product C   ₹9.8L   (156 orders)                   │
│                                                         │
│  Failed Payments: 41 orders, ₹2.8L value               │
│  [View failed] [Retry] [Alert ops team]                 │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Operations Dashboard** (auto-built when Shiprocket/Delhivery connectors active):
```

```

### 3. Natural Language Query Engine

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Ask FlowBridge Analytics                               │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  "How many Shopify orders failed to sync last week?"   │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

→ Query generated (shown to user for transparency):
   SELECT count(*) FROM fb_errors
   WHERE flow LIKE '%shopify%'
   AND timestamp BETWEEN '2026-04-14' AND '2026-04-21'

→ Result:
   47 orders failed between 14–21 Apr
   Top reason: Customer not found (31 orders)
   Second reason: API timeout (16 orders)

   [Show details] [Export CSV] [Set alert for this]

More examples:
"What's our average delivery time this month?"
"Which product category has the most failed payments?"
"How much revenue did we sync on last Diwali vs this year?"
"Show me flows that failed more than 10 times this week"
"What's the GST liability for Q4?"
```

**Implementation:**
- LLM (Claude/GPT via AI Engine) translates natural language → SQL
- SQL runs against ClickHouse (read-only, sandboxed)
- Result → formatted human-readable response
- Default Engine fallback: pre-built query templates for top 20 common questions (no LLM needed)

### 4. Scheduled Reports via WhatsApp / Email

```
REPORT SCHEDULES

Daily Digest (8:00 AM → WhatsApp to CFO):
  ├── Revenue synced yesterday
  ├── Orders processed
  ├── Failed flows (with status)
  └── Top alert if any

Weekly Summary (Monday 9:00 AM → Email to leadership):
  ├── Week-on-week revenue trend
  ├── Integration health summary
  ├── Top 5 errors and resolutions
  └── Connector health report

Monthly Finance Report (1st of month → Email to CA):
  ├── Total GMV synced
  ├── Payment success rate
  ├── GST filing status
  ├── TDS deductions summary
  └── Bank reconciliation completion %

Custom Report (user-defined):
  ├── Pick metrics, filters, date range
  ├── Choose delivery channel (email / WhatsApp / Slack)
  ├── Set schedule (daily / weekly / monthly / custom cron)
  └── [Test send now]
```

### 5. Anomaly Detection with Root Cause Analysis

Extends Phase 3's anomaly detection — Phase 13 adds the WHY and the WHAT TO DO:

```
🚨 ANOMALY DETECTED — 2:34 PM

"Payment sync volume dropped 64% in last 2 hours"

What happened:
├── Razorpay webhook receipts: 8/hour (normally 23/hour)
├── 47 flows timed out → dead letter queue
├── Razorpay API latency: 4,200ms (normally 340ms)
└── Razorpay Status Page: "Investigating webhook delays" (posted 1:45 PM)

Impact:
├── ₹3.4L in payments NOT reconciled yet
├── 47 Sales Invoices still showing as "Unpaid"
└── 3 customers may get payment reminder incorrectly

Recommended actions:
├── [Increase timeout to 30s & retry DLQ — likely fixes 40/47]
├── [Pause payment reconciliation flow until Razorpay resolved]
├── [Suppress payment reminder flow for next 4 hours]
└── [Notify: ops@yourcompany.com]

Resolved automatically if Razorpay recovers within 2 hours.
```

**Root cause engine:**
- Correlates anomaly timestamp with connector health metrics
- Checks external status pages (stored connector health data from Phase 6)
- Cross-references dead letter queue contents
- Estimates impact (₹ value, record count)
- Suggests specific actions ranked by likelihood of fixing it
- Uses Default AI Engine (IsolationForest + rule engine) — no LLM required

### 6. Custom Dashboard Builder

For teams who want more than auto-generated dashboards:

```
Dashboard Builder:
├── Drag & drop metric widgets
├── Chart types: line, bar, pie, number, table, funnel
├── Data sources: any ClickHouse table + ERPNext DocTypes
├── Filters: date range, connector, flow, status
├── Drill-down: click a number → see the records behind it
├── Share: private / team / public link
└── Embed: iframe embed in Frappe portal pages
```

Pre-built widget library:
```
FlowBridge widgets:
├── Flow run count (with trend)
├── Success rate gauge
├── Connector latency (P50/P99)
├── Error breakdown (pie)
├── Top failing flows (ranked list)
└── Data volume moved (area chart)

Business widgets (from synced ERPNext data):
├── Revenue synced (by source connector)
├── Orders by status
├── Payment collection rate
├── Delivery SLA compliance
└── GST filing status
```

### 7. Data Export & API

```
Export options:
├── CSV / Excel — any query result
├── PDF — dashboard snapshot (for board decks)
├── Scheduled email with CSV attachment
└── Webhook — push report data to external BI tools

Analytics API (for custom BI tools):
  GET /api/flowbridge/analytics/query
  POST { "sql": "SELECT ...", "format": "json" }
  → Returns query result as JSON

  GET /api/flowbridge/analytics/metrics/daily
  → Pre-aggregated daily metrics (no SQL needed)

  Compatible with:
  ├── Grafana (via JSON datasource plugin)
  ├── Metabase (via REST API connector)
  └── Power BI (via REST connector)
```

## Architecture Work

| Component | Language | What to Build |
|---|---|---|
| ClickHouse schema | SQL | Tables for events, runs, mappings, API calls, errors |
| Data pipeline (FlowBridge → ClickHouse) | Go | Write flow run data to ClickHouse in real-time |
| NL query engine | Python + LLM | NL → SQL translator, ClickHouse executor, result formatter |
| Auto-dashboard generator | Python + React | Detect active connectors → generate appropriate dashboards |
| Report scheduler | Python + Go | Cron-based report generation, WhatsApp/email delivery |
| Anomaly + root cause engine | Python (scikit-learn) + Go | IsolationForest, connector correlation, impact calculator |
| Custom dashboard builder | React | Drag & drop widget canvas, chart library (Recharts/Victory) |
| Analytics API | Rust/Axum | Query endpoint, pre-aggregated metrics API |
| Frappe DocTypes | Python | Dashboard, Report, AlertRule, ScheduledReport |

# Phase 14 — White-Label SaaS Builder

**Type:** Separate cloud platform (NOT a Frappe app — lives on FlowBridge's servers)
**Duration:** 5 months
**Goal:** Turn every Frappe implementor into a SaaS company — they build the product once, FlowBridge handles infrastructure, billing, multi-tenancy, and distribution
**Depends on:** Phase 8 (FlowBridge Cloud), Phase 2 (Marketplace), Phase 6 (Embedded iPaaS)

---

## The Vision

Today:
```
Frappe implementor builds a custom Shopify integration for Client A
→ Bills ₹1,50,000 one-time
→ Does it again for Client B (₹1,50,000)
→ Does it again for Client C (₹1,50,000)
→ 3 clients, 3 separate projects, 0 recurring revenue
```

With Phase 14:
```
Frappe implementor builds "ShopifyConnect for ERPNext" ONCE
→ Publishes on FlowBridge SaaS Builder
→ 200 ERPNext businesses subscribe at ₹999/mo
→ ₹1,99,800/mo recurring revenue
→ Zero additional dev work per new customer
→ FlowBridge handles billing, hosting, support infrastructure
```

**Every Frappe implementor becomes a SaaS founder. FlowBridge takes 10%.**

---

## Deliverables

### 1. SaaS Product Builder (Partner Studio)

**Self-service onboarding flow for end customer:**
```

Step 3: Connect Shopify → OAuth consent
Step 4: Connect Razorpay → API key entry
Step 5: Choose plan → Razorpay subscription payment
Step 6: Flows go live → real-time confirmation
→ Done in < 10 minutes, zero partner involvement
```

### 3. Multi-Tenant SaaS Architecture (per partner product)

```
Partner: "ShopifyConnect"
  ├── Tenant: Customer A (Pro plan)
  │   ├── Isolated flow execution (Phase 8 Cloud)
  │   ├── Isolated credential vault
  │   ├── Isolated logs and analytics
  │   └── Billing: ₹999/mo → partner receives ₹899/mo (after 10% cut)
  │
  ├── Tenant: Customer B (Starter — Free)
  │   └── Resource limits enforced (3 flows, 500 runs/mo)
  │
  └── Tenant: Customer C (Business plan)
      └── Billing: ₹2,999/mo → partner receives ₹2,699/mo

Total partner MRR: ₹200 customers × avg ₹1,500 = ₹3L/mo
FlowBridge earns: 10% = ₹30,000/mo from this one partner product
```

### 4. SaaS Billing Engine (Fully Managed)

FlowBridge handles ALL billing on behalf of the partner:

```
Customer signs up → Razorpay subscription created automatically
Customer upgrades → prorated billing handled
Customer churns → cancellation, access revocation, data retention policy
Failed payments → dunning: retry D+1, D+3, D+7, cancel if all fail
                  WhatsApp/email notification to customer at each step

Partner receives:
├── Monthly payout: (total MRR from their customers) × 90%
├── Payout via NEFT to partner bank account by 5th of next month
├── Tax invoice from FlowBridge for the 10% commission
└── Revenue dashboard with per-customer breakdown

Tax handling:
├── GST on subscription charged to end customer
├── GST invoice issued by FlowBridge in partner's name (if GSTIN provided)
└── TDS deducted on payout if applicable
```

| Partner operations dashboard | React + Frappe | Customer list, MRR, churn, NPS, flow health |

| Vertical SaaS templates | Flow JSON + connector.json | Pre-built product templates per vertical |
| Partner marketplace | React (Next.js, SEO-optimised) | apps.flowbridge.io — discovery, reviews, install |
| Partner payout system | Python | Monthly NEFT payout, TDS, GST invoice |
