# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_flowbridge/docs/phase16.md
# Phase 16 — High-Performance API Platform

> **Goal:** Evolve the Phase 15 no-code API Builder into a production-grade API platform.
> Three pillars:
> 1. **Feature completeness** — DELETE, bulk ops, caching, OpenAPI, exports, field-level auth, aggregation, nested writes, autocomplete, flow triggers, versioning
> 2. **Performance** — Rust/Axum sidecar handles GET reads at  3. **Event-driven APIs** — endpoints fire on scheduler ticks, DocType events (on_submit, on_cancel), and chain into each other

---

## Architecture — Why Rust + Go here

```
                     ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
                     │           FlowBridge API Platform            │
                     │                                              │
  Frontend/           │  Port 8080 — Rust/Axum API Gateway          │
  External  ─────────▶│  • HMAC / Bearer auth in ` |
| 16.23 | **Redis GET cache** — serves cached responses in `; no Redis round-trip for rate limit checks |
| 16.26 | **Bearer auth** — validates Frappe API key/secret | Fetches hashed key from MariaDB on first request, caches in memory 5 min; constant-time compare |
| 16.27 | **CORS preflight handler** — OPTIONS in  These patterns come directly from analyzing production Frappe app APIs (sales dashboards, HR hierarchies, KPI engines). No-code configuration for these is what separates FlowBridge from a generic API builder.

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 16.39 | **Hierarchy traversal endpoint** | `hierarchy_mode` Select (Flat list / Tree / Subtree only / Immediate reportees); `hierarchy_link_field` (e.g. `reporting_head`); resolver walks the org tree recursively using BFS; builds `{name, label, children:[...]}` tree structure or flat subtree list; `max_depth` config |
| 16.40 | **Role-aware data scoping** | `role_scope_rules` child table — `frappe_role`, `scope` (All / Own / Subtree / None); when user hits the endpoint their Frappe roles are checked and the right scope applied automatically; admin sees all, sales rep sees only their subtree — zero custom Python |
| 16.41 | **Multi-block composite response** | `response_blocks` child table — each block has its own `source_doctype`, `fields`, `filters`, `alias`; one GET call returns `{block1: [...], block2: {...}, block3: [...]}` — like `get_data` returning KPIs + coverage + conversion + lost accounts in one shot |
| 16.42 | **Distinct values endpoint** (`?distinct=field`) | `?distinct=state` or `?distinct=district` on any GET endpoint returns `{values: ["Maharashtra","Gujarat",...]}` — feeds frontend dropdowns without a separate endpoint per filter field |
| 16.43 | **Background job trigger endpoint** | `run_as_background_job` Check on POST endpoints; instead of running synchronously, enqueues via `frappe.enqueue`; returns `{queued: true, job_name: "...", message: "..."}` immediately; `job_queue` Select (default/long/short); `job_timeout` Int |
| 16.44 | **User-scoped Redis cache** | `cache_scope` Select (Global / Per User / Per User+Params); cache key built as `fb:apicache:{slug}:{user}:{hash(params)}`; per-user scoping ensures one user's data doesn't leak to another; TTL from `cache_ttl_seconds` |
| 16.45 | **KPI / proration aggregation** | `proration_config` on aggregation child — `prorate_by` Select (Date Range / Fiscal Year / None); computes `_range_days / 365.0` factor and multiplies aggregated totals; matches production pattern of `total_revenue * _proration_factor` |
| 16.46 | **Print format / PDF download proxy** | `pdf_mode` Check on GET endpoint; `print_format` Data field; `?name=SINV-001` → calls `frappe.utils.print_format.download_pdf` and streams the PDF; replaces the direct Frappe PDF endpoint with an authenticated FlowBridge proxy that respects `allowed_roles` |
| 16.47 | **OR-filters support** | `or_filters` child table on FB API Endpoint Filter — marks filter rows as OR group; maps to `frappe.get_all(or_filters=[...])` for search-across-fields (e.g. employee_name like OR employee_code like OR name like) |
| 16.48 | **`verify_current_user` mode** | `auto_resolve_user` Check; when the frontend omits the key param (e.g. `employee_id`), the endpoint auto-resolves it from `frappe.session.user` via a configurable lookup (DocType + email field); mirrors `verify_employee()` pattern without custom Python |

### 16-F: Deep Linked Document Resolver (unlimited depth)

> Handles the real-world case: Employee → Salary Structure Assignment → Salary Structure → Salary Detail → Account → Account Type → ... → as deep as the data model goes.
> There is **no hard depth cap** — the resolver walks the full graph until it reaches leaf nodes or hits a circular reference. A `max_depth` field is available as a safety governor (default: unlimited, set explicitly to limit traversal for performance-sensitive endpoints).

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 16.36 | **Recursive Join config** — unlimited depth traversal | Each `FB API Endpoint Join` row gains a `sub_joins_json` Code field (JSON array of nested join definitions); resolver walks the full graph recursively with **no hard cap**; `max_depth` Int field available as optional safety governor (0 = unlimited); circular reference guard via visited-set of `(doctype, name)` tuples prevents infinite loops on self-referencing DocTypes |
| 16.37 | **Cross-context computed fields** — full `doc` available in expressions | Computed field expressions upgraded from `row`-only scope to `doc` (full resolved object including all nested joins) + `results` (full list); enables cross-join aggregations like `sum(c['amount'] for c in doc['salary']['components'] if c['type']=='Earning')` |
| 16.38 | **Materialized / pre-computed endpoint** — scheduler-run heavy queries | `is_materialized` Check + `materialize_cron` Data field on FB API Endpoint; on cron tick: runs full deep traversal + calculations, stores flat JSON in `FB API Endpoint Result` DocType; GET requests serve the stored result instantly ( Frappe stores all attachments in the `File` DocType linked to a parent document. Every real-world app needs to read, upload, replace, and remove attachments via API — profile photos, invoice PDFs, KYC documents, signed contracts, etc. FlowBridge exposes this as first-class, no-code configured operations on any endpoint.

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 16.49 | **GET attachments** — list all files attached to a record | `enable_attachments` Check on FB API Endpoint; `GET ?endpoint=slug&name=DOC-001&_attachments=1` returns `{attachments:[{file_name, file_url, file_size, attached_to_field, is_private, thumbnail_url}]}`; filters by `attached_to_field` if specified |
| 16.50 | **GET single attachment / download** | `?_attachment=filename.pdf` streams the file as a binary download with correct `Content-Type` and `Content-Disposition`; respects Frappe private/public file path; auth enforced via endpoint's `auth_required` + `allowed_roles` |
| 16.51 | **POST attachment — upload new file** | Multipart `form-data` POST with file + `docname` field; saves to Frappe `File` DocType linked to the configured `primary_doctype`; `attachment_folder` config (e.g. `/files/invoices/`); `max_file_size_kb` guard; `allowed_extensions` whitelist (e.g. `pdf,jpg,png,xlsx`); returns `{file_name, file_url, name}` |
| 16.52 | **POST attachment — Base64 upload** | Alternative to multipart; accepts `{"file_name":"doc.pdf","file_content":"","docname":"SINV-001"}`; useful for mobile apps and systems that cannot send multipart; decodes and saves identically to 16.51 |
| 16.53 | **PATCH attachment — replace / rename existing file** | `PATCH ?endpoint=slug` with `{"attachment_name":"FILE-001","new_file_name":"contract_v2.pdf"}` or with new file content in body; replaces file content in-place, preserving the `File` doc name and all links; version-stamps old file to `FB API Endpoint Result` as history |
| 16.54 | **DELETE attachment** | `DELETE ?endpoint=slug&attachment_name=FILE-001`; calls `frappe.delete_doc("File", name)`; validates the file belongs to a doc of the configured `primary_doctype` before deleting (no cross-doc deletion) |
| 16.55 | **Thumbnail generation for images** | After upload (16.51/16.52), if file is an image (jpg/png/webp), auto-generates a 200×200 thumbnail stored as `{original_name}_thumb.webp`; `thumbnail_url` included in all attachment GET responses |
| 16.56 | **Attachment field binding** | `attached_to_field` config — links uploaded file to a specific field on the parent doc (e.g. uploading to `profile_photo` field updates the field value on the Employee doc automatically after save) |
| 16.57 | **Private vs public file control** | `file_visibility` Select (Public / Private / From Request); Public files served without auth; Private files require session; `From Request` lets the caller pass `is_private=1` in the upload body |
| 16.58 | **Attachment metadata endpoint** | `?_attachment_meta=FILE-001` returns full metadata: `{file_name, file_url, file_size, file_type, attached_to_doctype, attached_to_name, attached_to_field, is_private, creation, owner, thumbnail_url}` — without downloading the file |

#### Attachment API usage examples

```
# List all attachments on a Sales Invoice
GET /fb/api/invoices?name=SINV-0001&_attachments=1
→ {"attachments": [{"file_name":"invoice.pdf","file_url":"/files/invoice.pdf","file_size":45230}]}

# Download a specific attachment
GET /fb/api/invoices?name=SINV-0001&_attachment=invoice.pdf
→ streams binary PDF

# Upload new attachment (multipart)
POST /fb/api/invoices
Content-Type: multipart/form-data
Body: file=, docname=SINV-0001
→ {"file_name":"invoice.pdf","file_url":"/files/invoice.pdf","name":"FILE-00123"}

# Upload via Base64 (mobile friendly)
POST /fb/api/employee-kyc
Body: {"file_name":"aadhar.jpg","file_content":"","docname":"EMP-001","is_private":1}
→ {"file_name":"aadhar.jpg","file_url":"/private/files/aadhar.jpg","name":"FILE-00124"}

# Replace existing attachment
PATCH /fb/api/invoices
Body: {"attachment_name":"FILE-00123","file_content":""}
→ {"success":true,"file_url":"/files/invoice_v2.pdf"}

# Delete attachment
DELETE /fb/api/invoices?attachment_name=FILE-00123
→ {"success":true}
```

---

### 16-H: Advanced Auth & Permissions

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 16.59 | **JWT token issuance** | `enable_jwt` Check + `jwt_secret_key` (stored in FB FlowBridge Settings vault) + `jwt_expiry_minutes` Int; `POST ?_action=token` with Frappe credentials returns `{access_token, expires_in, token_type:"Bearer"}`; all subsequent requests accept `Authorization: Bearer `; works with React Native / Electron / third-party SPAs that cannot use Frappe session cookies |
| 16.60 | **Per-endpoint API keys** | `FB API Endpoint Key` child DocType — `key_name`, `api_key` (hashed, readonly after creation), `allowed_methods`, `expires_at`, `is_active`; revoke one key without affecting others or global keys |
| 16.61 | **Record-level permissions (owner filter)** | `owner_filter` Check — auto-appends `[["owner","=",frappe.session.user]]` to every GET/PUT/PATCH/DELETE; users see and modify only their own records; overridable for System Manager role |
| 16.62 | **Time-based access** | `access_from_time` + `access_to_time` (Time fields) + `access_days` (MultiSelect Mon–Sun); requests outside window get `403 Access outside allowed hours`; timezone from site config |
| 16.63 | **IP allowlist per endpoint** | `ip_allowlist` Small Text (CIDR or IP, one per line); evaluated before auth; `X-Forwarded-For` aware; overrides global gateway allowlist |
| 16.64 | **Request signing (HMAC-SHA256)** | `require_request_signing` Check + `signing_secret` (vault-stored); caller signs `method+path+timestamp+body_hash` with HMAC-SHA256; FlowBridge verifies + checks `X-Timestamp` within ±5 min to prevent replay attacks; for server-to-server integrations |

---

### 16-I: Workflow & State Actions

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 16.65 | **Submit / Cancel / Amend actions** | `POST ?_action=submit&name=SINV-001` triggers `doc.submit()`; `?_action=cancel` triggers `doc.cancel()`; `?_action=amend` triggers `frappe.copy_doc` with `amended_from`; full Frappe lifecycle hooks fire; `enable_submit`, `enable_cancel`, `enable_amend` Checks on the endpoint config |
| 16.66 | **Workflow state transition** | `POST ?_action=workflow&transition=Approve&name=SINV-001`; validates the transition is allowed for the user's role via Frappe Workflow rules; updates `workflow_state`; fires workflow action email if configured |
| 16.67 | **Bulk submit / cancel** | `POST ?_action=submit&_bulk=1` + `["SINV-001","SINV-002",...]`; atomic (all-or-none) or best-effort; returns `{success:[],failed:[{name,error}]}` |
| 16.68 | **Custom DocType method call** | `POST ?_action=method&_method=send_invoice_email&name=SINV-001`; calls a `@frappe.whitelist()` method on the DocType controller; `allowed_methods` whitelist on endpoint config prevents arbitrary method execution |

---

### 16-J: Advanced Validation & Transformation

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 16.69 | **JSON Schema validation on POST/PUT body** | `request_schema` Code field (JSON Schema draft-07); validated before any DB touch; returns `{error:"Validation failed", details:[{field,message}]}`; uses `jsonschema` Python lib with `Draft7Validator` |
| 16.70 | **Cross-field validation rules** | `FB API Endpoint Validation` child — `condition_expr` (e.g. `body['status']=='Submitted'`), `assert_expr` (e.g. `body['amount']>0`), `error_message`; evaluated after JSON Schema, before save |
| 16.71 | **Enum / value mapping** | `FB API Endpoint Enum Map` child — `fieldname`, `raw_value`, `display_value`; applied to response (e.g. `0→"Inactive"`, `1→"Active"`, `"D"→"Draft"`); reverse mapping applied on POST/PUT (frontend sends display, DB gets raw) |
| 16.72 | **Null handling policy** | `null_policy` Select (Keep null / Omit null fields / Replace with default); `null_default_value` Data for Replace mode; applied per-response after all transforms |
| 16.73 | **Date format normalization** | `date_format` Select (ISO 8601 / DD-MM-YYYY / DD/MM/YYYY / Unix timestamp ms / Unix timestamp s); applied to all Date and Datetime fields in response; request body accepts any of these formats (auto-detected) |
| 16.74 | **Request body sanitization** | `sanitize_request` Check — strips HTML tags (`bleach.clean`), trims whitespace, normalizes unicode (NFC), removes null bytes from all string fields in POST/PUT body before validation |
| 16.75 | **Auto type coercion on request** | `auto_coerce` Check — `"123"→123`, `"true"→True`, `"2025-01-01"→date`; prevents type-mismatch errors from frontend form submissions that send everything as strings |

---

### 16-K: Advanced Caching

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 16.76 | **Doc-event cache invalidation** | Hooks into Frappe `doc_events` for the endpoint's `primary_doctype`; on `after_insert / on_update / on_submit / on_cancel / on_trash` → scans and deletes all Redis keys matching `fb:apicache:{slug}:*`; ensures zero stale data after writes |
| 16.77 | **Cache warming** | `warm_on_startup` Check + `warm_params_json` Code (array of param sets to pre-warm); runs in a background job during `after_install` / `bench migrate` and on daily scheduler; logs warm status to `FB API Endpoint Result` |
| 16.78 | **Stale-while-revalidate** | `stale_ttl_seconds` Int; when cache is stale (past TTL but within stale_ttl), serve the old value immediately + enqueue background refresh via `frappe.enqueue`; zero perceived latency for the user |
| 16.79 | **CDN-friendly HTTP headers** | Emits `Cache-Control: public, max-age={ttl}, stale-while-revalidate={stale_ttl}`, `ETag: "{hash}"`, `Last-Modified`, `Vary: Authorization` on all GET responses; conditional `304 Not Modified` when client sends `If-None-Match` |
| 16.80 | **Per-field cache TTL override** | `FB API Endpoint Field` gains `cache_ttl_override` Int; high-volatility fields (live stock qty → 30s) coexist with stable fields (employee name → 86400s); response assembled from field-level cache buckets |

---

### 16-L: Integration Hooks

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 16.81 | **Outbound webhook on every call** | `notify_webhook_url` Data + `notify_on` MultiSelect (GET/POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE/Error); after request completes, async POST `{endpoint,method,user,ip,response_time_ms,status_code,response_summary}` to the URL; uses `frappe.enqueue` so it never blocks the caller |
| 16.82 | **FlowBridge flow trigger on GET** | `on_read_flow` Link (FB Flow) + `on_read_flow_mode` Select (Every call / On cache miss / On error); pipes `{endpoint, params, results_count}` as context; enables audit trails, downstream sync, or enrichment flows triggered by reads |
| 16.83 | **Push notification after write** | `post_write_notify` Check + `notify_roles` Small Text + `notify_template` Small Text; after POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE succeeds, sends WhatsApp/Telegram notification (via Phase 9 messenger) to users with configured roles; message template supports `{docname}`, `{method}`, `{user}` placeholders |

---

### 16-M: Advanced Pagination

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 16.84 | **Cursor-based pagination** | `pagination_mode` Select (Offset / Cursor / Keyset); Cursor mode: encodes `{last_id, last_modified, page}` as base64 opaque token in `next_cursor` / `prev_cursor` response fields; stable across inserts — no "page shift" when rows are added mid-browse |
| 16.85 | **Keyset pagination** | `?after_id=SINV-0500` — WHERE clause uses `name > 'SINV-0500'` instead of OFFSET; uses primary key index; dramatically faster on tables with millions of rows |
| 16.86 | **Total count toggle** | `?_count=0` skips the `SELECT COUNT(*)` query; response has `total: null`; removes the most expensive query on large tables; `include_total_by_default` Check controls default behaviour |

---

### 16-N: Mobile-Friendly API Features

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 16.87 | **FCM / APNs push notification after write** | `enable_push_notify` Check on FB API Endpoint; `push_notify_on` MultiSelect (POST / PUT / PATCH / DELETE); `push_title_template` + `push_body_template` (support `{docname}`, `{field}`, `{user}` placeholders); `push_target_roles` comma list — sends to all devices of users with those roles; FCM (Android) via Firebase Admin SDK; APNs (iOS) via `httpx` HTTP/2 to `api.push.apple.com`; device tokens stored in `FB Push Device` DocType (name, user, platform, token, is_active); no separate backend or mobile app server needed — FlowBridge is the push backend |
| 16.88 | **Bandwidth-optimized response mode** (`?_mobile=1`) | Strips all null fields from response; applies maximum Brotli compression (level 9 instead of level 4); switches serialization to MessagePack automatically (no `Accept` header required); omits audit metadata fields (`creation`, `modified_by`, `owner`, `docstatus`) unless explicitly in `selected_fields`; limits default page size to 10 (overridable); truncates long text fields to 200 chars (configurable via `mobile_text_truncate_chars` Int); returns image fields as thumbnail URL instead of full URL when thumbnail exists; response size typically 60–80% smaller than standard JSON response |

---

| CORS preflight | ~80 ms | < 0.5 ms |

# Phase 17 — Developer Experience, Real-Time & Observability

> **Goal:** Make FlowBridge APIs a joy to build with and monitor in production.
> Four pillars:
> 1. **Real-Time** — SSE, WebSocket, live doc-change streaming for frontends
> 2. **Developer Tooling** — Playground, mock mode, dry run, debug mode, cURL/Postman generation, request replay
> 3. **Observability & Analytics** — per-endpoint dashboards, slow query alerts, dependency maps
> 4. **Localization** — Accept-Language labels, currency/number formatting, timezone normalization

---

## Architecture

```
                    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
                    │            Phase 17 Layer                       │
                    │                                                  │
  Frontend          │  Real-Time Engine (Rust/Axum + Redis pub/sub)   │
  EventSource ──────▶  /fb/rt/{slug}  SSE stream                     │
  WebSocket  ──────▶  /fb/ws/{slug}   WS connection                  │
                    │       │                                          │
                    │       │  Frappe doc_events → Redis channel       │
                    │       │  → fan-out to all subscribers            │
                    │                                                  │
  Developer         │  API Playground (Frappe Desk widget)            │
  Browser   ────────▶  Interactive param form + live response         │
                    │  Mock mode / Dry run / Debug mode               │
                    │  cURL generator / Postman export                │
                    │  Request replay from audit log                  │
                    │                                                  │
  Ops / PM          │  Per-endpoint Analytics Dashboard               │
                    │  Slow query detector → WhatsApp/email alert     │
                    │  Error rate tracker → auto-incident creation    │
                    │  Dependency map → impact analysis               │
                    │                                                  │
  Global Users      │  Localization Layer                             │
                    │  Accept-Language → translated labels            │
                    │  Currency/number formatting by locale           │
                    │  Timezone normalization on all datetime fields  │
                    └────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                                      │
                          Phase 15/16 API Builder
                          MariaDB + Redis + Frappe
```

---

## Phase 17 Deliverables

### 17-A: Real-Time APIs

> Frappe already has a Redis pub/sub realtime system. Phase 17 taps into it to push live doc changes to any frontend subscribed to a FlowBridge endpoint — no polling needed.

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 17.1 | **Server-Sent Events (SSE) endpoint** | `enable_sse` Check on FB API Endpoint; `GET /fb/rt/{slug}` keeps connection open; Rust/Axum `text/event-stream` response; pushes `data: {event, doc, timestamp}\n\n` on every matching doc change; frontend: `new EventSource('/fb/rt/my-orders')` |
| 17.2 | **WebSocket endpoint** | `enable_ws` Check; `ws://host/fb/ws/{slug}`; Rust/Axum `axum::extract::WebSocketUpgrade`; bidirectional — client can send `{"action":"filter","filters":{"status":"Open"}}` to narrow the stream |
| 17.3 | **Single-record watch** | `GET /fb/rt/{slug}?watch=SINV-0001` — SSE stream scoped to one document name; pushes only changes to that specific record; used for live order status, live approval state |
| 17.4 | **Redis pub/sub bridge** | Frappe `doc_events` hook on `primary_doctype` publishes `{"doctype","name","event","data"}` to Redis channel `fb:rt:{slug}`; Rust SSE/WS handlers subscribe to that channel and fan out to all connected clients |
| 17.5 | **SSE heartbeat + reconnect** | Sends `: heartbeat\n\n` every 15s so proxies don't close idle connections; `Last-Event-ID` header support for resuming missed events on reconnect |
| 17.6 | **Filter-aware streaming** | SSE/WS respects endpoint's `default_filters` + runtime filter params; a client watching `status=Open` only receives events where the doc matches that filter after the change |

---

### 17-B: Developer Tooling

> Every tool in this group eliminates a class of friction between writing an API config and shipping a working frontend.

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 17.7 | **Interactive API Playground** | Frappe Desk widget rendered inside the FB API Endpoint form (below the config); auto-generates param input fields from the endpoint schema; user fills values, clicks Run, sees live response; shows raw JSON + formatted preview; logs each run to audit trail |
| 17.8 | **Mock / Sandbox mode** | `mock_response` Code field (JSON) on FB API Endpoint; when `mock_mode_enabled=1`, every request returns that static JSON regardless of DB state; `mock_mode_expires_at` Datetime — auto-disables mock after that; lets frontend teams develop before real data exists |
| 17.9 | **Dry Run mode** (`?_dry_run=1`) | On POST/PUT/PATCH: runs all validation (JSON Schema, cross-field, required fields, custom_logic) but wraps in a transaction that is always rolled back; returns `{valid:true, would_create:{...}}` or `{valid:false, errors:[...]}` — zero DB side effects |
| 17.10 | **Debug mode** (`?_debug=1`) | Appends `_debug` object to every response: `{sql_queries:[{sql,params,time_ms}], timings:{filter_build_ms, db_query_ms, join_resolution_ms, computed_fields_ms, custom_logic_ms, total_ms}, cache_hit:bool, rows_scanned:int, joins_resolved:int}`; only available to System Manager role |
| 17.11 | **cURL command generator** | Inside the FB API Endpoint form, a readonly Code field auto-renders the exact `curl` command for each enabled method (GET list, GET single, POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE); updates live as config changes; copyable in one click |
| 17.12 | **Postman collection export** | `export_postman_collection()` API — generates full Postman Collection v2.1 JSON for all active endpoints; includes auth, example request bodies, example responses; importable into Postman/Insomnia with one click |
| 17.13 | **Request replay** | From the `FB Webhook Log` (audit log), a Replay button re-fires the exact request (same params, same user context, same body) and shows the new response side-by-side with the original; for debugging production issues |
| 17.14 | **Response diff viewer** | Compare responses between two endpoint versions (e.g. v1 vs v2) or between two timestamps from the audit log; highlights added/removed/changed fields; helps detect breaking changes before deploying |
| 17.15 | **SDK code generator** | From any endpoint config, generates ready-to-paste client code in: JavaScript (`fetch`), TypeScript (typed interface + fetch), Python (`requests`), React hook (`useQuery` with FlowBridge TypeScript SDK); displayed in a tabbed Code panel in the form |

---

### 17-C: Observability & Analytics

> Every endpoint is a product. Products need dashboards, alerts, and dependency maps.

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 17.16 | **Per-endpoint analytics dashboard** | Embedded chart panel inside FB API Endpoint form; shows: calls/day (bar), avg + p95 + p99 latency (line), error rate % (line), top 10 callers by user (table), top 5 error messages (table); data from Phase 13 ClickHouse or MariaDB aggregation of FB Webhook Log |
| 17.17 | **Slow query detection + alert** | `slow_query_threshold_ms` Int on endpoint config; if p95 latency in a 5-min window exceeds threshold → creates `FB SLA Breach Alert` + fires WhatsApp/email via Phase 9 messenger; auto-resolves when p95 drops below threshold |
| 17.18 | **Error rate alerting** | `error_rate_alert_threshold` Float (0–100 %) + `error_rate_window_minutes` Int; if error rate in window > threshold → alert; debounced — one alert per 30 min per endpoint |
| 17.19 | **Top callers report** | `GET ?_stats=callers` on any endpoint (System Manager only) returns `[{user, ip, call_count, avg_latency_ms, last_called_at}]` sorted by call_count; useful for billing, quota planning, and abuse detection |
| 17.20 | **Endpoint dependency map** | `GET ?_stats=dependencies` returns a JSON graph: `{endpoint, primary_doctype, joined_doctypes:[{doctype, join_type, depth}], flows_triggered:[{flow_name,on_event}], cached:bool, sse_enabled:bool}`; renders as an interactive node graph in the Desk widget; used for impact analysis before schema changes |
| 17.21 | **Usage anomaly detection** | Hourly scheduler compares call volume to the trailing 7-day average; if volume drops >80% (possible outage) or spikes >500% (possible abuse/bug) → creates FB Connector Incident + alerts; uses same Phase 7 incident system |

---

### 17-D: Localization

> A FlowBridge API served to a global frontend should feel native everywhere — translated labels, local number formats, correct timezones.

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 17.22 | **Accept-Language label translation** | If `enable_i18n` Check is on and request has `Accept-Language: hi` (Hindi) or any Frappe-supported language, all field labels in the response (from `label_override` or DocType meta) are translated using `frappe._(label, lang=lang)`; fallback to English if translation missing |
| 17.23 | **Currency formatting by locale** | `currency_locale` Select (System Default / From Accept-Language / Fixed locale); formats all Currency fields: `₹1,00,000.00` for en-IN, `$100,000.00` for en-US, `€100.000,00` for de-DE; raw numeric value also included as `{field}_raw` |
| 17.24 | **Number formatting by locale** | Decimal separator (`.` vs `,`) + thousands separator (`,` vs `.` vs ` `) applied to all Float/Int fields; `number_locale` config separate from currency_locale |
| 17.25 | **Timezone normalization** | `response_timezone` Data (e.g. `Asia/Kolkata`, `UTC`); all Date and Datetime fields converted from Frappe server timezone to the configured response timezone; if `From Request` mode: reads `X-Timezone` header from frontend; offset also included as `{field}_tz_offset` |
| 17.26 | **RTL response metadata** | For Arabic/Hebrew/Urdu locales, adds `{_meta: {direction:"rtl", locale:"ar"}}` to response; frontend can use this to flip layout without sniffing the locale itself |

# Phase 18 — Performance Hardening & Benchmarking

> **Goal:** Make every generated API — GET, POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE, bulk, deep join, SSE, WebSocket, attachment, report — perform at the targets defined in [performance.md](performance.md). No generated endpoint should be slow by default. Performance is a design constraint, not a post-launch concern.

> **Reference:** [performance.md](performance.md) — full architecture, cache design, DataLoader pattern, compression, HTTP/2, connection pooling, benchmarking suite.

---

## The Core Principle

Every API that FlowBridge generates passes through the Rust/Go layer first:

```
Any Request
     │
     ▼
Rust/Axum (port 8080)
  L1 memory cache → if hit: return in >` with LRU eviction; max 10,000 entries; TTL checked on read; hot responses served in  1,000 rows: `Transfer-Encoding: chunked`; Rust streams rows from MariaDB cursor as they arrive; frontend receives first rows in ` — no Redis round-trip for rate limit decisions; bucket refills at `rate_limit_rpm/60` tokens/second; evicts idle buckets after 5 min |

### 18-C: Go Worker Performance

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 18.16 | **Configurable goroutine pool** | `MAX_WORKERS` env var (default 25, tune to MariaDB pool size); buffered job channel (capacity 1,000) absorbs write bursts without blocking Rust gateway |
| 18.17 | **Bulk write batching in single transaction** | For bulk POST/PATCH: wraps all inserts/updates in a single `BEGIN...COMMIT`; 1,000 inserts in one transaction is 50× faster than 1,000 individual transactions |
| 18.18 | **Prepared statement cache (Go side)** | `db.PrepareContext` for INSERT/UPDATE patterns; cached per doctype; eliminates SQL parse + plan overhead on repeated operations |
| 18.19 | **Async flow trigger (non-blocking goroutine)** | Flow trigger after write fires in a `go func(){}()` — never blocks the write response path; result published to Redis Streams regardless of flow trigger outcome |
| 18.20 | **Back-pressure handling** | If job channel is full (> 1,000 pending): Rust gateway returns `503 Service Busy` with `Retry-After: 1` header instead of blocking; prevents memory exhaustion under extreme load |

### 18-D: Real-Time Performance (SSE / WebSocket)

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 18.21 | **Tokio async SSE — 50k connections per node** | Each SSE connection is a `tokio::task` (not a thread); 50,000 connections use ~500 MB RAM; one shared Redis Streams reader fan-outs to all via `mpsc::Sender` channels |
| 18.22 | **Redis Streams for SSE persistence** | `fb:rt:{slug}` Redis Stream with `MAXLEN 1000`; SSE reconnect uses `XREAD ... {Last-Event-ID}` to replay missed events; never loses events during temporary disconnects |
| 18.23 | **WebSocket per-message deflate** | `permessage-deflate` compression negotiated in WS handshake; 10 KB JSON message → ~2 KB on wire; CPU cost ", "exec")` called once at endpoint save time, stored as bytecode in Redis; `exec(bytecode, ...)` on each request — skips parsing overhead |
| 18.29 | **Attachment streaming (no in-memory buffering)** | File download uses `frappe.response["filecontent"]` with `wsgiref.util.FileWrapper` — streams from disk without loading entire file into Python memory; critical for large files |
| 18.30 | **Bulk operation generator pattern** | Bulk POST/PATCH uses a Python generator to yield `frappe.get_doc().insert()` calls inside a single `frappe.db.begin()` context; avoids building a list of 1,000 docs in memory before starting |

### 18-F: Cache Performance

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 18.31 | **Cache stampede prevention (Redis SETNX lock)** | On L2 cache miss: `SETNX fb:lock:{key} 1 EX 10`; only one request computes; others serve stale (stale-while-revalidate) or wait 50ms + retry; eliminates thundering herd on cache expiry |
| 18.32 | **Incremental materialization** | For materialized endpoints: when a doc changes, recompute only that doc's contribution to the result, not the entire dataset; `FB API Endpoint Result` stores per-record cache blobs that are assembled on GET |
| 18.33 | **Redis pipeline for multi-key operations** | Cache warming + bulk invalidation use Redis pipelining (`MULTI`/`EXEC`) — batch 100 SET/DEL operations into a single round-trip instead of 100 separate commands |
| 18.34 | **ClickHouse for materialized analytics endpoints** | Aggregation endpoints with `materialize_cron` store results in ClickHouse (columnar); scans of 10M rows for SUM/COUNT complete in  0` emit `Cache-Control: public, s-maxage={ttl}`; Cloudflare/CloudFront caches at edge → 0 ms latency globally |

### 18-G: Benchmarking Suite

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 18.36 | **`wrk` scenario suite** | Lua scripts for 7 standard scenarios (cached GET storm, mixed read/write, deep join stress, SSE fan-out, bulk write, attachment upload, cache stampede); run in CI on every merge to `main` |
| 18.37 | **`k6` realistic load test** | TypeScript k6 script simulating realistic user mix (80% GET, 15% POST, 5% PATCH) with think time; 500 VUs; ramp up/down; threshold assertions gate deployment |
| 18.38 | **Rust microbenchmarks (`cargo bench`)** | `criterion` benchmarks for: auth token verification, cache key hashing, rate limit check, JSON serialize 1k rows, MessagePack serialize 1k rows, HMAC-SHA256 verify |
| 18.39 | **Go microbenchmarks (`go test -bench`)** | Benchmarks for: bulk insert (100/1000/10000 rows), field validation (100 fields), Redis stream publish, flow trigger HTTP call |
| 18.40 | **Python benchmarks (`pytest-benchmark`)** | Benchmarks for: DataLoader (50 employees × 5 join levels), computed fields (1000 rows × 5 expressions), custom_logic exec (pre-compiled bytecode vs live compile), attachment streaming (100 MB file) |
| 18.41 | **Regression gate in CI** | GitHub Actions: `wrk` p99 must be within 20% of baseline; `cargo bench` must be within 10% of baseline; any regression blocks merge; baseline auto-updated on release tags |
| 18.42 | **Continuous production profiling** | Rust: `pprof` CPU profiling sampled at 100 Hz → flame graphs in Grafana; Go: `net/http/pprof` endpoint; Python: `py-spy` profiler for gunicorn workers; all integrated into Phase 13 ClickHouse + Grafana |

### 18-H: Infrastructure Hardening

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 18.43 | **ProxySQL deployment + config** | Query routing rules for FlowBridge read/write split; connection pool config (max_connections, max_idle); query cache for identical SELECTs; monitoring via `SHOW MYSQL STATUS` |
| 18.44 | **MariaDB tuning for FlowBridge workload** | `innodb_buffer_pool_size` = 70% of RAM; `innodb_read_io_threads = 8`; `innodb_write_io_threads = 4`; `query_cache_type = 0` (use Redis instead); `max_connections = 500`; slow query log threshold 100ms |
| 18.45 | **Redis tuning** | `maxmemory-policy: allkeys-lru`; `save ""` (disable AOF for cache layer); `hz = 20`; separate Redis instance for write queue (persistence enabled) vs cache (no persistence) |
| 18.46 | **Nginx performance config** | `worker_processes auto`; `worker_connections 65535`; `sendfile on`; `tcp_nopush on`; `gzip off` (Rust handles compression); `keepalive_timeout 65`; upstream keepalive 64 |
| 18.47 | **Kubernetes HPA for Rust nodes** | HorizontalPodAutoscaler on `requests_per_second` Prometheus metric; scale from 2→10 nodes in  100,000 req/s |

---

## Phase 18 complete when
- `wrk` scenario 1 (cached GET storm) achieves > 100,000 req/s with p99 < 3 ms on a 4-core Rust node
- `wrk` scenario 3 (deep join) achieves p99 < 300 ms, `?_debug=1` confirms ≤ depth SQL queries (DataLoader working)
- `wrk` scenario 7 (cache stampede) confirms exactly 1 DB query per expiry cycle (lock working)
- SSE fan-out: 10,000 clients receive a doc change within 500 ms verified by k6 websockets script
- Bulk POST: 1,000 records committed in < 2 s, all in single transaction
- Attachment: 10 MB file uploaded + thumbnail generated + download streamed in < 1 s p99
- Zero full table scans on any standard endpoint (EXPLAIN verified in CI)
- ProxySQL routing verified: 0% write queries hitting read replicas
- Regression gate blocks a synthetic 50% slowdown in CI
- Flame graphs show < 5% CPU in JSON serialization (MessagePack path verified)
- HTTP/2 multiplexing verified with `nghttp -n --no-dep -v` tool
- Kubernetes HPA scales from 2 to 5 Rust nodes in < 60s under load

# Phase 19 — AI-Native API Builder

> **Goal:** Make the API Builder self-configuring. A user describes what they want in plain English and FlowBridge builds the endpoint config — joins, filters, computed fields, custom logic, schema. AI also monitors running endpoints and surfaces insights, anomalies, and security threats automatically.

---

## Phase 19 Deliverables

### 19-A: Natural Language → Endpoint Config

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 19.1 | **NL → FB API Endpoint** | Text input in Desk: "Create an API that returns all unpaid Sales Invoices for a customer with their outstanding amount and last payment date" → AI configures primary_doctype, selected_fields, default_filters, joined_doctypes, computed_fields, and custom_logic; user reviews and saves |
| 19.2 | **DocType-aware field suggestions** | On selecting `primary_doctype`, AI fetches DocType meta + common query patterns from ClickHouse audit log and suggests: top 10 most-queried fields, most-used joins, most-used filters for that DocType — pre-populates the form |
| 19.3 | **Smart join suggestions** | AI analyzes Link fields on the primary DocType and suggests which are worth joining based on: frequency of access in audit log, number of useful fields in the linked DocType, depth usefulness |
| 19.4 | **`custom_logic` code generation** | Code assistant inside the custom_logic Code field; user describes logic in a comment: `# calculate net salary from earnings minus deductions`; AI generates the Python expression; user reviews; runs dry-run automatically to validate |
| 19.5 | **`expression` code generation for computed fields** | Same as 19.4 but for computed field expressions; shows preview value on sample data from the DocType |
| 19.6 | **Auto-generated JSON Schema for `request_schema`** | After POST/PUT fields are configured, AI generates a JSON Schema draft-07 `request_schema` from the DocType meta and `post_required_fields` config — no manual writing |

### 19-B: Auto-Generated Documentation

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 19.7 | **Endpoint description generation** | AI generates a 2–3 sentence human-readable description of what the endpoint does, what parameters it accepts, and what it returns — stored in `endpoint_description` field; used in developer portal and OpenAPI spec |
| 19.8 | **Example request/response generation** | AI generates realistic example request bodies (POST/PUT) and example responses (GET) from the DocType meta and actual data samples; stored as `example_request_json` and `example_response_json`; used in Postman export and playground |
| 19.9 | **Auto CHANGELOG entry on config save** | Every time an FB API Endpoint config is saved, AI diffs the old vs new config and writes a human-readable changelog entry: "Added `customer_group` filter. Removed `amended_from` from selected fields. Added computed field `net_outstanding`." |
| 19.10 | **Breaking change warning on save** | Before saving a config change to a Published endpoint, AI checks if the change is breaking (removed field, changed type, added required param) → warning dialog with impact: "3 consumers may be affected" |

### 19-C: AI Monitoring & Insights

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 19.11 | **Usage pattern insights** | Weekly AI report per endpoint: busiest hours, most common filter combinations, fields never actually read by consumers (candidates for removal), slow param combinations — delivered to endpoint owner via WhatsApp/email |
| 19.12 | **Anomaly detection (security)** | ML model on audit log: detects credential theft (new IP, unusual hours), scraping (sequential `name=` queries at high rate), data exfiltration (unusually large responses, bulk export at unusual times); creates `FB Security Alert` + alerts |
| 19.13 | **Auto-generated test cases** | From endpoint config + sample data, AI generates a `FlowTest`-compatible Python test suite covering: happy path GET, empty result, POST with valid data, POST with missing required field, PATCH on non-existent record, auth failure; downloadable from the Desk form |
| 19.14 | **Performance recommendations** | After 7 days of usage, AI analyzes `?_debug=1` query logs and recommends: indexes to create, fields to cache, joins to pre-materialize, filter combinations to add as default_filters |
| 19.15 | **Semantic endpoint search** | `GET /api/method/...api_builder.search_endpoints?q=invoice payment` — vector similarity search across all endpoint descriptions; returns the most semantically relevant endpoints even without exact keyword match |

---

## Phase 19 complete when
- NL input "unpaid invoices for a customer" produces a correctly configured FB API Endpoint in < 10 seconds
- AI field suggestions cover ≥ 80% of what the user would have manually selected (measured on test set of 20 real endpoints)
- `custom_logic` code gen produces runnable Python on first try for 70% of simple expressions
- Auto-generated test cases catch at least the happy path + missing required field case
- Anomaly detection fires on a simulated credential theft scenario within 5 minutes
- Breaking change warning fires correctly when a selected field is removed from a Published endpoint

# Phase 2 — Marketplace Alpha

**Duration:** 2 months
**Goal:** Enable developers to publish flows and businesses to install them in one click
**Layer:** Marketplace (Distribute & Earn)
**Depends on:** Phase 1 complete

---

## What We're Building

The distribution engine. Every flow built in Studio can be packaged, published, and installed by any Frappe/ERPNext site in the world.

---

## The Flywheel

```
Developer builds flow in Studio
        ↓
Publishes to Marketplace
        ↓
1000 businesses install it
        ↓
Businesses customize in their Studio
        ↓
Some publish their customization back
        ↓
Marketplace grows richer
        ↓
More businesses join
        ↓
More developers build
        ↓
🔄 FLYWHEEL SPINS FASTER
```

---

## Deliverables

### 1. Flow Package Format
When a developer publishes a flow, the package contains:
```
flow_package/
├── flow.json          ← visual flow definition
├── connectors.json    ← which connectors needed
├── field_map.json     ← default field mappings
├── config_schema.json ← what user needs to fill in
├── README.md          ← setup instructions
├── screenshots/       ← how it looks
└── changelog.md       ← version history
```
User installs → fills in credentials → live in 2 minutes.

### 2. Developer Publishing Portal
- Package builder UI (wrap your flow into a package)
- Versioning (semver)
- Changelog editor
- Screenshot uploader
- Category & tag selection
- Pricing configuration (free / one-time / subscription)
- Submit for review

### 3. Marketplace Browse & Install
```
🛒 FlowBridge Marketplace
"Ready-made integrations for Frappe/ERPNext"

FILTERS        POPULAR THIS WEEK
Category       [Shopify ↔ ERPNext]  [Razorpay Auto-recon]
├ Free         ⭐4.9(234) FREE       ⭐4.8(189) ₹499/mo
├ Paid
Rating         [WhatsApp Notifier]  [Tally XML Importer]
├ 4.5+         ⭐4.7(156) ₹299/mo   ⭐4.9(98) ₹999 once
```

- Search & filter (category, rating, price, platform)
- Preview before install (screenshots, README)
- One-click install
- Auto-dependency check (connectors needed)
- Post-install config wizard (fill credentials)

### 4. Payment Integration (Razorpay)
- Paid flow purchase (one-time)
- Subscription billing (monthly)
- Developer payout system
- Revenue tracking per flow
- Refund handling

### 5. Rating & Review System
- Star rating (1–5)
- Written review
- Verified install badge
- Developer response
- Report abuse
- Review moderation

### 6. Developer Revenue Dashboard
- Total installs (free + paid)
- Revenue this month / all time
- Per-flow breakdown
- Payout history
- Revenue share: 80% developer / 20% FlowBridge (one-time)
- Revenue share: 75% developer / 25% FlowBridge (subscription)

### 7. Marketplace Features

**For Buyers:**
- Search & filter
- Preview before install
- One-click install
- Auto-updates (when developer publishes new version)
- Ratings & reviews
- Request custom flows
- Community Q&A

**For Developers:**
- Developer dashboard
- Revenue analytics
- Version management
- Review management
- Support ticket handling
- API documentation
- Beta tester program

### 8. Connector Marketplace
Custom connectors built via the Universal Connector (Phase 1) can be packaged and published — same flywheel as flows but at the connector layer:

**Connector package format:**
```
connector_package/
├── connector.json       ← endpoint definitions, auth type, field schemas
├── auth_schema.json     ← what credentials the user needs to fill
├── sample_payloads/     ← sample request/response per endpoint
├── README.md            ← setup instructions
└── changelog.md         ← version history
```

**Publishing flow:**
- Developer exports their Universal Connector as a package
- Submits to Marketplace (free or paid)
- FlowBridge team reviews for security (no credential leakage, HMAC not exposed)
- Approved → listed in Connector Marketplace with install count, rating

**For buyers:**
- Browse → one-click install connector
- Fill in their own credentials
- Connector appears in their Studio flow builder immediately

**Revenue:** same 80/20 split (developer/FlowBridge) as flow packages

### 9. Connector Versioning & Rollback
- Every save of a connector definition creates a version (semver)
- Visual diff between versions (what endpoints/mappings changed)
- One-click rollback to any previous version
- Flows pinned to a specific connector version (breaking changes don't auto-update live flows)

---

## Architecture Work (Phase 2)

| Component | Language | What to Build |
|---|---|---|
| Marketplace DocTypes | Python/Frappe | FlowPackage, ConnectorPackage, PackageInstall, Review, Payout |
| Marketplace UI | React | Browse (flows + connectors), package detail, install wizard, dev dashboard |
| Package validator | Python | Schema validation, connector compatibility check, credential field security scan |
| Install engine | Python/Go | Unpack flow/connector, configure, register in Studio |
| Billing integration | Python | Razorpay subscription + one-time payment |
| Review system | Python/Frappe | Rating, moderation, notification |
| Connector version manager | Python/Frappe | Semver storage, diff engine, flow-version pinning |

---

## Revenue Model (Phase 2)
- Free flows → build reputation, get installs
- Paid one-time → developer gets 80%, FlowBridge gets 20%
- Paid subscription → developer gets 75%, FlowBridge gets 25%
- Enterprise custom → negotiated split

**Projection:**
> 500 installs × ₹999 = ₹4.99L per flow
> 100 paid flows × avg ₹50K GMV × 22% = ₹11L/mo

# Phase 20 — API Governance & Lifecycle

> **Goal:** Give engineering teams full control over the lifecycle of every API — from design through deprecation. Every Published endpoint has an owner, an SLA, a consumer registry, a changelog, and a sunset date. Config changes to Published endpoints go through review. Consumers are notified before breaking changes.

---

## Phase 20 Deliverables

### 20-A: Lifecycle State Machine

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 20.1 | **Endpoint lifecycle states** | `lifecycle_state` Select on FB API Endpoint: Draft → In Review → Published → Deprecated → Retired; state shown as colored badge in list view; transitions enforced (cannot skip Review → Deprecated) |
| 20.2 | **State transition rules** | Draft: editable freely; In Review: read-only, awaiting approver action; Published: config changes require new review cycle; Deprecated: read-only, returns `Deprecation-Notice` header on every response; Retired: returns `410 Gone` with `Link` header pointing to successor endpoint |
| 20.3 | **Config change review workflow** | When a Published endpoint config is saved, a `FB API Change Request` doc is created with the diff; assigned to `reviewer_user`; reviewer sees side-by-side old vs new config; Approve → applies change; Reject → reverts; integrates with Phase 6 approval system |
| 20.4 | **Sunset date enforcement** | `sunset_date` Datetime field; a daily scheduler checks for endpoints past sunset → auto-transitions to Retired; all consumers receive email/WhatsApp 30 days, 7 days, and 1 day before sunset |
| 20.5 | **Successor endpoint link** | `successor_endpoint` Link (FB API Endpoint) on Deprecated/Retired endpoints; `410 Gone` response includes `Link: ; rel="successor-version"` header; developer portal shows migration guide |

### 20-B: Consumer Registry

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 20.6 | **Auto-detected consumer registry** | `FB API Endpoint Consumer` DocType — `endpoint`, `consumer_type` (User / API Key / External IP / Frontend App), `consumer_id`, `first_seen`, `last_seen`, `avg_daily_calls`; auto-populated from audit log; no manual entry |
| 20.7 | **Consumer impact analysis** | Before any breaking change: "This endpoint has 4 active consumers in the last 30 days — 2 users, 1 API key (mobile-app-prod), 1 external IP (192.168.1.x)"; shown in the review workflow |
| 20.8 | **Consumer deprecation notifications** | When endpoint moves to Deprecated: email/WhatsApp to all consumers from audit log with: endpoint name, sunset date, successor URL, migration guide link |
| 20.9 | **Consumer self-registration** | API key owners can tag their key with `app_name` and `contact_email`; shown in consumer registry; used for deprecation notifications |

### 20-C: API Catalog

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 20.10 | **API catalog dashboard** | Frappe Desk page showing all FB API Endpoints: filterable by lifecycle_state, primary_doctype, owner, SLA status, last_changed; columns: name, state, DocType, owner, calls/day, p99 latency, error rate |
| 20.11 | **Endpoint owner assignment** | `owner_user` Link (User) + `owner_team` Data on FB API Endpoint; owner receives: weekly usage digest, SLA breach alerts, consumer change notifications, review requests |
| 20.12 | **API SLA definition** | `sla_p99_ms` Int + `sla_availability_pct` Float; daily scheduler checks Phase 17 analytics — if p99 > sla_p99_ms or error rate causes availability < target → creates `FB SLA Breach Alert` + notifies owner |
| 20.13 | **API tags and categories** | `tags` Small Text (comma-separated) + `category` Select (Finance / HR / Sales / Inventory / Custom) on FB API Endpoint; used for catalog filtering and developer portal browsing |
| 20.14 | **Cross-site API registry** | `FB Federated API Registry` — syncs endpoint metadata (not data) across Phase 5 federated sites; HQ can see all endpoints published by all regional Frappe instances |

### 20-D: Contract Testing

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 20.15 | **Response schema snapshot** | On `Publish` transition, FlowBridge fires the endpoint with sample params and stores the response structure (field names + types) as `schema_snapshot` JSON; not the data — just the shape |
| 20.16 | **Contract test on config save** | Every time a Published endpoint config is saved (via review), FlowBridge re-fires and compares response shape to snapshot; if fields removed or types changed → blocks save with "Contract broken: field `grand_total` missing" |
| 20.17 | **Versioned schema history** | Every approved config change saves a new schema snapshot version; history viewable in the Desk form; diff between any two versions shown |
| 20.18 | **Consumer-provided contracts** | Consumer registers their expected response shape (JSON Schema) via API; FlowBridge validates the real response against it on every deploy; failure → alert consumer |

### 20-E: Changelog & Audit

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 20.19 | **Auto-generated CHANGELOG** | `FB API Endpoint Changelog` child — every approved config change generates an entry with: version number, changed_by, change_date, human-readable summary (AI-generated per Phase 19.9), breaking_change flag |
| 20.20 | **Public changelog in developer portal** | Changelog entries for Published endpoints visible in the Phase 21 developer portal; consumers see what changed and when |

---

## Phase 20 complete when
- A Published endpoint config change goes through Draft → Review → Approved flow before applying
- Consumer registry auto-populates within 1 hour of first API call
- Sunset date enforcement transitions endpoint to Retired and returns 410 on the exact date
- Contract test blocks a config change that removes a field from the response schema
- API catalog dashboard loads all endpoints with live stats in < 2 s
- Deprecation notifications reach consumers via WhatsApp 30 days before sunset (verified with test endpoint)

# Phase 21 — API Monetization & Developer Portal

> **Goal:** Let Frappe site owners monetize their data APIs — charge per call, offer subscription tiers, manage external developer access — all without building a separate billing backend. And give external developers a self-service portal to discover, try, subscribe, and integrate with FlowBridge APIs.

---

## Phase 21 Deliverables

### 21-A: API Monetization

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 21.1 | **Pay-per-call billing** | `is_billable` Check + `price_per_call` Currency (INR) on FB API Endpoint; every request logged to `FB API Usage` with `api_key`, `endpoint`, `timestamp`, `response_time_ms`, `status_code`; `frappe.enqueue` — never blocks the request |
| 21.2 | **API subscription tiers** | `FB API Subscription Plan` DocType — `plan_name`, `calls_per_day`, `calls_per_month`, `price_monthly`, `allowed_endpoints` (comma or wildcard); developer subscribes → gets API key scoped to that plan |
| 21.3 | **Usage metering + quota enforcement** | Redis counter `fb:usage:{api_key}:{date}` incremented on every call; Rust gateway checks before processing — `429 Quota Exceeded` when limit hit; resets at midnight UTC |
| 21.4 | **Monthly invoice generation** | Monthly scheduler: aggregates `FB API Usage` per API key → calculates amount (calls × price_per_call OR subscription fee) → generates `FB API Invoice` doc → Razorpay payment link sent via email; GST calculated (18%); TDS handled for partner payouts |
| 21.5 | **Razorpay subscription for API plans** | Plan purchase creates Razorpay subscription; webhook updates `FB API Subscription` status; failed payment → grace period (3 days) → quota reduced to free tier; restored on successful payment |
| 21.6 | **Free tier + overage** | `free_calls_per_month` Int per endpoint; calls above free tier billed at `price_per_call`; overage invoiced monthly; shown in developer portal as "X of Y free calls used" |
| 21.7 | **Revenue split for API publishers** | If endpoint is owned by a partner (Phase 14), 90% of billing revenue goes to partner; `FB API Payout` doc created monthly; RazorpayX NEFT disbursement; TDS (194J 10%) + GST TDS (2%) deducted |

### 21-B: Developer Portal

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 21.8 | **Public API documentation site** | Auto-generated static site from all Published FB API Endpoints with `is_public=1`; sections: Overview, Authentication, Endpoints (grouped by category), Changelog, Status; hosted at `/developer` or custom domain; no Frappe desk login required |
| 21.9 | **Interactive playground (public)** | Try-it widget on every endpoint documentation page; pre-filled with `example_request_json` (Phase 19.8); user can edit params and fire a real request against a sandbox environment; response shown with syntax highlighting |
| 21.10 | **Developer self-registration** | External developers sign up with email → verify → get `FB Developer Account` doc; no Frappe system user created; manages their own API keys, subscriptions, usage dashboard |
| 21.11 | **API key self-management** | Developer portal: create key (name, expiry, allowed endpoints), rotate key, revoke key, view per-key usage stats; all without contacting the site admin |
| 21.12 | **Subscription management** | Developer portal: browse API plans, subscribe via Razorpay, view invoice history, upgrade/downgrade plan, cancel — fully self-service |
| 21.13 | **Usage dashboard for developers** | Per-developer dashboard: calls today/this month vs quota, breakdown by endpoint, latency trends, error rate, cost this month, estimated next invoice |
| 21.14 | **API sandbox environment** | Sandbox API keys hit a read-only copy of the DB with anonymized data; sandbox calls never billed; `X-FlowBridge-Sandbox: true` header in all sandbox responses; sandbox reset nightly |
| 21.15 | **Webhook subscription for consumers** | Developer subscribes to endpoint events (new record, update, delete) via developer portal; FlowBridge delivers webhooks to their registered URL — like GitHub webhooks; HMAC-signed; retry on failure |
| 21.16 | **SDK download from portal** | Portal generates and serves downloadable SDKs (Python, TypeScript, Flutter) pre-configured with the developer's API key and the endpoints they've subscribed to; generated per Phase 17.15 SDK code generator |

### 21-C: Admin Controls

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 21.17 | **API monetization dashboard (admin)** | Frappe Desk: total revenue this month, active subscribers, top endpoints by revenue, top developers by spend, failed payment count, payout queue |
| 21.18 | **Developer account management** | Admin can: view all developer accounts, suspend/unsuspend, increase quota manually, view full usage history, impersonate for debugging (audit logged) |
| 21.19 | **Endpoint pricing management** | Admin sets `price_per_call` + `free_calls_per_month` per endpoint; bulk update via import; price change takes effect from next billing cycle with 7-day notice to existing subscribers |

---

## Phase 21 complete when
- External developer signs up, creates API key, makes a billable call, receives invoice — full flow in < 10 minutes
- Monthly invoice generated correctly for 3 test developers with different usage patterns
- Razorpay subscription payment failure triggers grace period and quota reduction correctly
- Developer portal documentation page loads in < 1 s (static generation verified)
- Sandbox environment returns `X-FlowBridge-Sandbox: true` and never touches production data
- Webhook delivery retries on 5xx with exponential backoff, succeeds on retry
- Revenue split calculates correctly (90/10) and payout request created for partner

# Phase 22 — Expanded Integration Ecosystem

> **Goal:** FlowBridge speaks every protocol — REST (done), GraphQL, gRPC, Kafka, MQTT, SOAP, edge computing. Any system, anywhere, can integrate with Frappe through FlowBridge regardless of what protocol it speaks.

---

## Phase 22 Deliverables

### 22-A: GraphQL Gateway

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 22.1 | **Auto-generated GraphQL schema** | Every Published FB API Endpoint with `enable_graphql=1` is reflected as a GraphQL type + query/mutation; `FB API Endpoint` → `type Invoice { name: String, grand_total: Float, customer: Customer }` + `query invoices(filters, limit, page): InvoiceList`; served at `/fb/graphql` |
| 22.2 | **GraphQL mutations (POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE)** | Enabled methods on each endpoint become mutations: `mutation createInvoice(input: InvoiceInput!): Invoice`; same validation, custom_logic, auth as REST path |
| 22.3 | **GraphQL subscriptions (real-time)** | `subscription invoiceUpdated(name: String): Invoice` — backed by Phase 17 SSE/Redis Streams; client receives live updates over WebSocket |
| 22.4 | **DataLoader for GraphQL N+1** | GraphQL resolver uses Phase 18 BatchLoader to batch all nested field queries; `invoice { customer { name, city } }` fires 2 queries (invoices + customers IN [...]) not N+1 |
| 22.5 | **GraphQL playground (GraphiQL)** | Built-in GraphiQL IDE at `/fb/graphql/playground`; schema introspection; auto-complete; query history; auth header injection |
| 22.6 | **Persisted queries** | Client sends SHA-256 hash of query instead of full query string; Rust stores registered queries in Redis; reduces request size by 80% for mobile clients |

### 22-B: gRPC Support

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 22.7 | **Auto-generated `.proto` from endpoint config** | `get_proto_schema(slug)` generates Protocol Buffer v3 definition: `message InvoiceResponse { string name = 1; double grand_total = 2; }` + `service InvoiceService { rpc List(ListRequest) returns (InvoiceList); rpc Get(GetRequest) returns (InvoiceResponse); }`; downloadable from developer portal |
| 22.8 | **Rust/Tonic gRPC server** | `tonic` crate serves gRPC on port 9090; auto-generated service implementations route to same backend as REST (cache → MariaDB); 5× faster than REST for internal service-to-service calls |
| 22.9 | **gRPC-Web proxy** | `grpc-web` protocol support in Rust (browsers cannot call raw gRPC); enables browser frontends to use gRPC |
| 22.10 | **Bidirectional streaming** | `rpc WatchInvoices(WatchRequest) returns (stream InvoiceEvent)` — server-side streaming backed by Redis Streams; replaces SSE for gRPC clients |

### 22-C: Apache Kafka Connector

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 22.11 | **Kafka → Frappe consumer** | `FB Kafka Source` DocType — `bootstrap_servers`, `topic`, `consumer_group`, `target_doctype`, `field_mapping` (JSON); Go worker (`kafka-go`) consumes messages → creates/updates Frappe docs; offset committed after successful Frappe write |
| 22.12 | **Frappe → Kafka producer** | `FB Kafka Sink` DocType — on Frappe doc_events, serialize doc as JSON/Avro and publish to configured Kafka topic; `schema_registry_url` for Avro schema registration |
| 22.13 | **Kafka offset management + DLQ** | Failed messages (Frappe error or schema mismatch) moved to `{topic}-dlq`; `FB Kafka DLQ` DocType shows failed messages with error reason; one-click replay |
| 22.14 | **Kafka lag monitoring** | Consumer group lag exposed on Phase 17 per-endpoint analytics dashboard; alert when lag > `max_lag_threshold` messages |

### 22-D: MQTT (IoT)

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 22.15 | **MQTT → Frappe subscriber** | `FB MQTT Source` DocType — `broker_url`, `topic_pattern` (supports wildcards `+` and `#`), `target_doctype`, `field_mapping`; paho-mqtt Python client or Go `eclipse/paho.mqtt.golang`; creates/updates Frappe docs on message receipt |
| 22.16 | **Frappe → MQTT publisher** | On Frappe doc_events, publish JSON payload to configured MQTT topic; `qos_level` Select (0 / 1 / 2); `retain` Check |
| 22.17 | **Time-series data ingestion** | MQTT messages with `timestamp` field auto-written to ClickHouse `fb_iot_readings` table (sensor_id, metric, value, timestamp) for Phase 13 analytics; bulk insert via ClickHouse batch API |

### 22-E: Edge Computing

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|

# Phase 23 — No-Code Frontend Builder

# Phase 24 — Security Hardening

> **Goal:** Enterprise-grade security across every generated API, config, and data path. SOC 2 readiness, India DPDP Act compliance, OWASP coverage, zero-trust networking, automated VAPT, and immutable audit trails.

---

## Phase 24 Deliverables

### 24-A: Automated Security Testing

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 24.1 | **VAPT automation (OWASP ZAP)** | Weekly scheduled scan of all Published FB API Endpoints using OWASP ZAP in daemon mode; tests for: SQLi, XSS, IDOR, broken auth, excessive data exposure, rate limit bypass; findings create `FB Security Alert` docs with severity (Critical/High/Medium/Low); critical findings alert via WhatsApp immediately |
| 24.2 | **Secret scanning in custom_logic** | On every save of `custom_logic` or `sub_joins_json` Code fields, regex + ML scanner checks for hardcoded secrets: API keys, passwords, connection strings, private keys, Aadhaar/PAN patterns; blocks save with "Secret detected at line X" if found; patterns from truffleHog ruleset |
| 24.3 | **Dependency vulnerability scanning** | Daily: scans Python (`pip audit`), Rust (`cargo audit`), Go (`govulncheck`) dependencies for CVEs; findings create `FB Security Alert`; critical CVEs trigger alert to System Manager within 1 hour |
| 24.4 | **Fuzzing on POST/PUT endpoints** | Automated fuzzer sends malformed inputs (oversized strings, SQL fragments, Unicode edge cases, negative numbers, future dates) against all POST/PUT endpoints; any 500 error → `FB Security Alert` |

### 24-B: Zero-Trust & Network Security

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 24.5 | **mTLS between Rust gateway and MariaDB** | Mutual TLS on all internal connections: Rust → MariaDB, Rust → Redis, Go → MariaDB; certificates managed by cert-manager (Kubernetes) or manual rotation script; no plaintext internal traffic |
| 24.6 | **API threat intelligence** | `FB Threat Feed` DocType — ingests IP blocklists (Spamhaus, AbuseIPDB, Cloudflare Radar) via daily scheduler; Rust gateway checks requesting IP against in-memory blocklist (DashMap) before any processing; auto-blocked IPs logged to `FB Security Alert` |
| 24.7 | **Rate limit by country (geo-IP)** | `geo_rate_limit` child table on FB API Endpoint — `country_code`, `calls_per_minute`; Rust checks `X-Forwarded-For` → MaxMind GeoLite2 DB lookup (in-memory, updated weekly) → applies country-specific limit; "Unknown" country gets strictest limit |
| 24.8 | **Request size limits** | `max_request_body_kb` Int (default 1024 KB); enforced at Rust layer before body is parsed; `413 Payload Too Large` immediately; prevents memory exhaustion attacks |
| 24.9 | **Response header hardening** | All responses include: `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin`, `Permissions-Policy: interest-cohort=()`; configurable per endpoint |

### 24-C: PII & Data Protection

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 24.10 | **PII auto-detection** | On endpoint save: AI scans `selected_fields` for PII patterns — field names containing `aadhaar`, `pan`, `passport`, `mobile`, `email`, `dob`, `account_number` → auto-adds to `mask_fields` with admin notification: "PII fields auto-masked: aadhaar_no, mobile" |
| 24.11 | **DPDP Act compliance (India)** | `FB DPDP Consent` integration on GET endpoints that expose personal data: checks `FB DPDP Consent` for the requesting user before returning data; if no consent on record → `403 Consent Required` with consent URL; `data_principal_field` config maps response field to the data principal |
| 24.12 | **Right to erasure (DELETE personal data)** | `enable_erasure` Check on endpoint; `POST ?_action=erase&identity_field=email&value=user@example.com` → finds all records where `identity_field=value` across configured DocTypes → anonymizes (replaces with "ERASED-{hash}") rather than deleting (preserves referential integrity); audit trail of erasure |
| 24.13 | **Data residency per endpoint** | `data_residency_zone` Select (India / EU / US / Global) on FB API Endpoint; Rust gateway rejects requests from outside the zone's allowed IP ranges; integrates with Phase 8 residency zones |
| 24.14 | **Field-level encryption in response** | `encrypted_fields` comma list; specified fields are AES-256 encrypted in the response (not masked, but encrypted); consumer must have the decryption key; useful for fields that need to be stored client-side but protected |

### 24-D: Immutable Audit Trail

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 24.15 | **Hash-chained API config audit log** | Every FB API Endpoint config save appends a hash-chained entry to `FB Security Audit Log` — `{timestamp, user, action, diff_hash, prev_hash}`; chain integrity verifiable; tampering detectable; same pattern as Phase 11 EDI audit |
| 24.16 | **Immutable request audit log** | `FB Webhook Log` gains `entry_hash` field — SHA-256 of `{timestamp, endpoint, user, ip, params_hash, response_hash}`; chained with `prev_hash`; stored in append-only ClickHouse table; cannot be deleted via Frappe UI |
| 24.17 | **SOC 2 evidence export** | `export_soc2_evidence(from_date, to_date)` — generates ZIP with: all config change logs, all access logs (summarized by endpoint+user), rate limit violation logs, security alert history, uptime report; formatted for SOC 2 Type II auditor review |
| 24.18 | **Session timeout enforcement** | JWT-authenticated sessions (Phase 16.59): configurable `jwt_expiry_minutes`; refresh token pattern (short-lived access token + long-lived refresh token stored in HttpOnly cookie); forced logout on suspicious activity (new country, new device type) |

### 24-E: Compliance Reporting

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 24.19 | **DPDP compliance dashboard** | Shows: total personal data endpoints, consent coverage %, recent erasure requests, PII auto-masked fields, data residency violations; exportable as PDF for DPA reporting |
| 24.20 | **Security posture score** | Automated score (0–100) based on: % endpoints with auth required, % with rate limiting, % with HTTPS, % with PII masked, VAPT findings open, secret scan clean; shown on FlowBridge Settings page; trends over time |

# Phase 25 — Search & Discovery

> **Goal:** Every generated API endpoint supports instant, intelligent, typo-tolerant, semantically-aware search — far beyond SQL LIKE. Typesense for instant full-text, vector embeddings for semantic search, faceted filtering for filter UIs, and a global search across all endpoints from a single call.

## Phase 25 Deliverables

### 25-A: Typesense Full-Text Search

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 25.1 | **Typesense integration** | `enable_typesense` Check + `typesense_collection` Data on FB API Endpoint; `typesense_host`, `typesense_api_key` in FB FlowBridge Settings; `?search=` param on GET endpoint uses Typesense instead of SQL LIKE when enabled |
| 25.2 | **MariaDB → Typesense sync via CDC** | On Frappe `after_insert / on_update / on_trash` doc_events for the endpoint's `primary_doctype`: upsert/delete the corresponding Typesense document; fields synced = `selected_fields` + `search_fields`; no polling |
| 25.3 | **Initial collection backfill** | `backfill_typesense(endpoint_slug)` — bulk-imports all existing records to Typesense using batch API (1000 records per batch); progress logged to `FB API Endpoint Result`; run on first enable or on demand |
| 25.4 | **Typo-tolerant search** | Typesense `num_typos: 2` default; `?search=invioce` finds "invoice"; configurable via `typesense_typo_tokens` Int on endpoint config |
| 25.5 | **Search highlighting** | `?search=acme&highlight=1` → response fields contain `Acme Corporation` HTML highlighting around matched terms; useful for search results UI |
| 25.6 | **Search ranking config** | `typesense_sort_by` Data (e.g. `grand_total:desc,_text_match:desc`) on endpoint config; controls how results are ordered when `?search=` is active; default: text match score |

### 25-B: Faceted Search

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 25.7 | **Facet configuration** | `FB API Endpoint Facet` child DocType — `fieldname`, `max_values` Int (default 10), `sort_by` Select (count / value / alpha); facets computed by Typesense (if enabled) or by SQL GROUP BY |
| 25.8 | **Facet response format** | `?facets=status,customer_group` appends `{facets: {status: {Open: 45, Paid: 120, Overdue: 8}, customer_group: {Retail: 80, Wholesale: 93}}}` to response; frontend renders filter checkboxes from this |
| 25.9 | **Multi-select facet filtering** | `?filter[status][]=Open&filter[status][]=Overdue` — OR within a facet, AND between facets; standard faceted navigation pattern |

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|

| 25.13 | **OpenAI Embeddings fallback** | If `semantic_engine=openai` on endpoint config: uses `text-embedding-3-small` model (1536-dim, better quality); API key from FlowBridge Settings vault; ~3× slower than local but 2× more accurate |
| 25.14 | **Approximate nearest neighbor search** | `?semantic_search=find overdue invoices from construction clients` → embed query → cosine similarity search in ClickHouse using `cosineDistance(vector, query_vector)` ORDER BY score; returns top-K semantically similar records |
| 25.15 | **Hybrid search (text + semantic)** | `?search=acme&semantic_weight=0.4` — merges Typesense keyword results with vector similarity results using Reciprocal Rank Fusion (RRF); keyword match score weighted (0.6) + semantic score (0.4); configurable ratio |
| 25.16 | **Semantic cache** | Semantically similar queries return same cached results; query vector stored in Redis; cosine similarity > 0.95 with a cached query → return cached response; avoids re-embedding + re-searching for near-identical questions |

### 25-D: Search Analytics

| # | Deliverable | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 25.17 | **Search query logging** | Every `?search=` and `?semantic_search=` query logged to ClickHouse `fb_search_log` table: endpoint, query, result_count, top_result_name, response_time_ms, user, timestamp |
| 25.18 | **Zero-result queries report** | Daily: queries that returned 0 results grouped by endpoint; surfaces: missing data, wrong field in `search_fields`, spelling patterns users expect; shown in Phase 17 per-endpoint analytics |
| 25.19 | **Popular searches** | `?_stats=searches` on endpoint → `[{query, count, avg_results, avg_time_ms}]` sorted by count; helps understand what users actually look for |
| 25.20 | **Search suggestion / autocomplete** | `?suggest=1&q=ac` → Typesense prefix search returning `[{value:"ACME Corp", label:"ACME Corp - Customer"}]` in < 5 ms; uses `suggest_value_field` + `suggest_label_field` from Phase 16.12 |
