# Phase 26 — API Performance Hardening

> **Goal:** Make every generated API as fast as possible — faster cache lookups, zero cold-start
> latency, RAM-speed reads for hot endpoints, and self-tuning bulk throughput.
> No new features; pure speed.

---

## 26.1 — xxHash64 Cache Key Generation

**Problem:** MD5 is used today in `gateway/src/api_gateway.rs` (`make_cache_key`) and
`frappe_flowbridge/flowbridge/api/cache_perf.py`. MD5 is a cryptographic hash — it's 8× slower
than necessary for a cache key where collision resistance doesn't matter.

**Solution:** Replace MD5 with xxHash64 everywhere a cache key is computed.
xxHash64 produces a 64-bit key in ~2 ns vs ~16 ns for MD5.

### Changes

**`gateway/src/api_gateway.rs`**
```rust
// Before
use md5;
fn make_cache_key(slug: &str, params: &str) -> String {
    format!("{slug}:{:x}", md5::compute(params))
}

// After
use xxhash_rust::xxh3::xxh3_64;
fn make_cache_key(slug: &str, params: &str) -> String {
    format!("{slug}:{:016x}", xxh3_64(params.as_bytes()))
}
```

Add to `gateway/Cargo.toml`:
```toml
xxhash-rust = { version = "0.8", features = ["xxh3"] }
```

**`frappe_flowbridge/flowbridge/api/cache_perf.py`**
```python
# Before
import hashlib
key = hashlib.md5(params_str.encode()).hexdigest()

# After
import xxhash
key = xxhash.xxh3_64_hexdigest(params_str.encode())
```

Add to `requirements.txt`:
```
xxhash>=3.4.1
```

### Acceptance Criteria
- [ ] `make_cache_key` in Rust uses `xxh3_64`, no md5 import remaining
- [ ] `cache_perf.py` uses `xxhash.xxh3_64_hexdigest`, no hashlib.md5 remaining
- [ ] Benchmark: key generation ≥ 5× faster than MD5 on 100k iterations
- [ ] Existing cached entries gracefully miss once (different key format) then repopulate

---

## 26.2 — Cache Schema Versioning

**Problem:** When a generated API's field list changes (fields added/removed, type changed),
cached responses for old schema stay in Redis and are served as valid. Callers silently get
stale or wrong-shaped data until TTL expires.

This is the same bug `frappe_snapshot` solved with `cache_schema_version`.

**Solution:** Add `cache_schema_version` (Int, default 1) to `FB API Endpoint`. The cache key
becomes `{slug}:{version}:{params_hash}`. Incrementing the version makes all old keys misses
instantly — no purge needed.

### Changes

**`FB API Endpoint` DocType** — add field:
```
cache_schema_version    Int    default 1    label "Cache Schema Version"
```

**`gateway/src/api_gateway.rs`** — update `make_cache_key`:
```rust
fn make_cache_key(slug: &str, schema_version: u32, params: &str) -> String {
    format!("{slug}:v{schema_version}:{:016x}", xxh3_64(params.as_bytes()))
}
```

**`frappe_flowbridge/flowbridge/api/cache_perf.py`**:
```python
def make_cache_key(slug: str, schema_version: int, params: dict) -> str:
    params_str = json.dumps(params, sort_keys=True)
    h = xxhash.xxh3_64_hexdigest(params_str.encode())
    return f"{slug}:v{schema_version}:{h}"
```

**Desk UX:** When `cache_schema_version` is incremented from the Endpoint form, show a
warning: _"Incrementing version will invalidate all cached responses for this endpoint."_

### Acceptance Criteria
- [ ] `cache_schema_version=1` (default) — cache key is `{slug}:v1:{hash}`
- [ ] Incrementing to `2` — old `v1` keys become misses, new responses cached as `v2`
- [ ] Decrementing version is blocked (validate in `before_save`)
- [ ] `frappe_flowbridge.api.cache_perf.make_cache_key` includes version in all call sites

---

## 26.3 — Warm-on-Deploy Hook

**Problem:** After `bench migrate` or a service restart, the Redis cache is cold. The first N
requests to every enabled endpoint pay full DB/computation latency. On a busy site this causes
a visible spike for 1–5 minutes post-deploy.

**Solution:** An `after_migrate` hook that enqueues prefetch jobs for all active, cache-enabled
endpoints ordered by call volume descending. The top 50 endpoints are warm before the first
real request arrives.

### Changes

**`frappe_flowbridge/frappe_flowbridge/hooks.py`** — add:
```python
after_migrate = ["frappe_flowbridge.install.warm_cache_after_migrate"]
```

**`frappe_flowbridge/install.py`**:
```python
def warm_cache_after_migrate():
    endpoints = frappe.get_all(
        "FB API Endpoint",
        filters={"is_active": 1, "enable_cache": 1},
        fields=["name", "slug"],
        order_by="total_calls desc",
        limit=50,
    )
    for ep in endpoints:
        frappe.enqueue(
            "frappe_flowbridge.api.cache_perf.prefetch_endpoint",
            endpoint_slug=ep.slug,
            queue="long",
            enqueue_after_commit=True,
        )
    frappe.logger("frappe_flowbridge").info(
        f"Warm-on-deploy: enqueued {len(endpoints)} cache prefetch jobs"
    )
```

**`frappe_flowbridge/flowbridge/api/cache_perf.py`** — add `prefetch_endpoint(slug)`:
```python
def prefetch_endpoint(endpoint_slug: str):
    ep = frappe.get_doc("FB API Endpoint", {"slug": endpoint_slug})
    if not ep.enable_cache:
        return
    # Execute with empty params to populate the default cached response
    from frappe_flowbridge.api.api_gateway import execute_endpoint
    execute_endpoint(endpoint_slug, params={}, _prefetch=True)
    frappe.logger("frappe_flowbridge").info(f"Prefetched: {endpoint_slug}")
```

### Acceptance Criteria
- [ ] `after_migrate` hook is registered in `hooks.py`
- [ ] `warm_cache_after_migrate` runs after every `bench migrate`
- [ ] Only `is_active=1, enable_cache=1` endpoints are enqueued
- [ ] Endpoints ordered by `total_calls desc` — highest-traffic warmed first
- [ ] Capped at 50 endpoints to avoid overloading the queue
- [ ] Log line confirms N jobs enqueued

---

## 26.4 — Go Vanguard Prefetch Sidecar

**Problem:** Even with Redis, cache reads have ~2ms network overhead. For the top 10–20 hot
endpoints called thousands of times per minute, this adds up to real latency.

**Inspiration:** `frappe_rpg` Phase 42 Vanguard pattern — a Go sidecar that holds the most
frequently accessed data in RAM. Reads hit a `sync.Map` in Go process memory: **~0.1ms vs 2ms**
for Redis, **20× faster**.

**Solution:** A Go worker `executor/cmd/prefetch_worker/` that:
1. Polls `FB API Request Log` every 60s for top-N endpoints by call frequency
2. Fetches their responses from origin (or Redis) and stores in a `sync.Map`
3. Exposes an HTTP endpoint `GET /hot-cache/{slug}` on `localhost:7779`
4. The Rust gateway checks `localhost:7779` before Redis (zero-copy JSON passthrough)

### Architecture

```
Request → Rust Gateway
              │
              ├─1─► Go Sidecar :7779/hot-cache/{slug}?params=...
              │       └── sync.Map hit  → return ~0.1ms  ✅
              │       └── sync.Map miss → fall through
              │
              ├─2─► Redis cache
              │       └── hit → return ~2ms  ✅
              │       └── miss → fall through
              │
              └─3─► Origin (DB query / Python handler)
                      └── return ~50ms, write to Redis + optionally notify sidecar
```

### New file: `executor/cmd/prefetch_worker/main.go`

```go
package main

import (
    "encoding/json"
    "net/http"
    "sync"
    "time"
    "log"
    "io"
    "fmt"
    "os"
)

type HotCache struct {
    mu    sync.RWMutex
    store map[string]cachedEntry
}

type cachedEntry struct {
    body      []byte
    storedAt  time.Time
    ttl       time.Duration
}

var hotCache = &HotCache{store: make(map[string]cachedEntry)}

func (h *HotCache) Get(key string) ([]byte, bool) {
    h.mu.RLock()
    defer h.mu.RUnlock()
    e, ok := h.store[key]
    if !ok || time.Since(e.storedAt) > e.ttl {
        return nil, false
    }
    return e.body, true
}

func (h *HotCache) Set(key string, body []byte, ttl time.Duration) {
    h.mu.Lock()
    defer h.mu.Unlock()
    h.store[key] = cachedEntry{body: body, storedAt: time.Now(), ttl: ttl}
}

func (h *HotCache) Evict(key string) {
    h.mu.Lock()
    defer h.mu.Unlock()
    delete(h.store, key)
}

// HTTP handler: GET /hot-cache/{slug}
func handleGet(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    slug := r.PathValue("slug")
    params := r.URL.Query().Get("params")
    key := slug + ":" + params
    if data, ok := hotCache.Get(key); ok {
        w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
        w.Header().Set("X-Cache", "HIT-RAM")
        w.Write(data)
        return
    }
    http.NotFound(w, r)
}

// HTTP handler: POST /hot-cache/{slug}  (called by Rust gateway after origin fetch)
func handleSet(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    slug := r.PathValue("slug")
    params := r.URL.Query().Get("params")
    ttlSec := 300 // default 5 min
    key := slug + ":" + params
    body, err := io.ReadAll(r.Body)
    if err != nil {
        http.Error(w, "bad body", 400)
        return
    }
    hotCache.Set(key, body, time.Duration(ttlSec)*time.Second)
    w.WriteHeader(204)
}

// DELETE /hot-cache/{slug} — called on invalidation
func handleEvict(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    slug := r.PathValue("slug")
    params := r.URL.Query().Get("params")
    hotCache.Evict(slug + ":" + params)
    w.WriteHeader(204)
}

// Background: poll Frappe for top-N endpoints and prefetch
func prefetchLoop(frappeURL, apiKey, apiSecret string) {
    for {
        time.Sleep(60 * time.Second)
        prefetchTopEndpoints(frappeURL, apiKey, apiSecret)
    }
}

func prefetchTopEndpoints(frappeURL, apiKey, apiSecret string) {
    url := fmt.Sprintf("%s/api/method/frappe_flowbridge.api.cache_perf.get_top_endpoints?limit=20", frappeURL)
    req, _ := http.NewRequest("GET", url, nil)
    req.Header.Set("Authorization", fmt.Sprintf("token %s:%s", apiKey, apiSecret))
    resp, err := http.DefaultClient.Do(req)
    if err != nil {
        log.Printf("prefetch poll error: %v", err)
        return
    }
    defer resp.Body.Close()
    var result struct {
        Message []struct {
            Slug   string `json:"slug"`
            Params string `json:"default_params"`
        } `json:"message"`
    }
    if err := json.NewDecoder(resp.Body).Decode(&result); err != nil {
        return
    }
    for _, ep := range result.Message {
        fetchAndStore(frappeURL, apiKey, apiSecret, ep.Slug, ep.Params)
    }
    log.Printf("Prefetched %d hot endpoints", len(result.Message))
}

func fetchAndStore(frappeURL, apiKey, apiSecret, slug, params string) {
    url := fmt.Sprintf("%s/api/method/frappe_flowbridge.api.api_gateway.execute?slug=%s&%s", frappeURL, slug, params)
    req, _ := http.NewRequest("GET", url, nil)
    req.Header.Set("Authorization", fmt.Sprintf("token %s:%s", apiKey, apiSecret))
    resp, err := http.DefaultClient.Do(req)
    if err != nil {
        return
    }
    defer resp.Body.Close()
    body, _ := io.ReadAll(resp.Body)
    hotCache.Set(slug+":"+params, body, 5*time.Minute)
}

func main() {
    frappeURL := os.Getenv("FRAPPE_URL")
    apiKey := os.Getenv("FRAPPE_API_KEY")
    apiSecret := os.Getenv("FRAPPE_API_SECRET")
    port := os.Getenv("HOT_CACHE_PORT")
    if port == "" {
        port = "7779"
    }

    mux := http.NewServeMux()
    mux.HandleFunc("GET /hot-cache/{slug}", handleGet)
    mux.HandleFunc("POST /hot-cache/{slug}", handleSet)
    mux.HandleFunc("DELETE /hot-cache/{slug}", handleEvict)
    mux.HandleFunc("GET /health", func(w http.ResponseWriter, _ *http.Request) {
        w.Write([]byte(`{"ok":true}`))
    })

    // Start prefetch background loop
    go prefetchLoop(frappeURL, apiKey, apiSecret)
    // Initial warm on startup
    go prefetchTopEndpoints(frappeURL, apiKey, apiSecret)

    log.Printf("Hot cache sidecar listening on :%s", port)
    log.Fatal(http.ListenAndServe(":"+port, mux))
}
```

**`frappe_flowbridge/flowbridge/api/cache_perf.py`** — add:
```python
@frappe.whitelist()
def get_top_endpoints(limit=20):
    return frappe.db.sql("""
        SELECT e.slug, e.default_params
        FROM `tabFB API Endpoint` e
        JOIN `tabFB API Request Log` l ON l.endpoint = e.name
        WHERE e.is_active = 1 AND e.enable_cache = 1
        GROUP BY e.name
        ORDER BY COUNT(l.name) DESC
        LIMIT %(limit)s
    """, {"limit": int(limit)}, as_dict=True)
```

**`gateway/src/api_gateway.rs`** — check sidecar before Redis:
```rust
// Try hot sidecar first (RAM, ~0.1ms)
if let Ok(resp) = hot_cache_get(&slug, &params_str).await {
    return Ok(resp.with_header("X-Cache", "HIT-RAM"));
}
// Fall through to Redis...
```

### Acceptance Criteria
- [ ] `executor/cmd/prefetch_worker/main.go` compiles: `go build ./cmd/prefetch_worker/`
- [ ] `GET /hot-cache/{slug}` returns 404 on miss, 200 with `X-Cache: HIT-RAM` on hit
- [ ] `POST /hot-cache/{slug}` stores entry, subsequent GET returns it
- [ ] `DELETE /hot-cache/{slug}` evicts entry
- [ ] Background prefetch loop runs every 60s, populates top-20 endpoints
- [ ] Rust gateway sends `X-Cache: HIT-RAM` header on sidecar hits
- [ ] TTL enforced — entries older than configured TTL return miss
- [ ] `GET /health` returns `{"ok":true}`

## 26.5 — Sub-Range Cache Filtering

**Problem:** If an endpoint was cached for `2026-01-01` to `2026-03-31` and the caller asks for
`2026-02-01` to `2026-02-28`, the current code treats it as a miss and re-fetches from origin.
This is wasteful — the cached payload is a **superset** of what was requested.

**Solution:** During cache lookup, if a stored entry's `[from_date, to_date]` fully contains the
requested range, filter the JSON records in-process and return the subset. No origin call needed.

### Changes

**`gateway/src/api_gateway.rs`** — `check_cache` function:
```rust
// Current: exact key match only
// New: also query for superset entries

async fn find_superset_cache(
    redis: &RedisPool,
    slug: &str,
    schema_version: u32,
    from_date: NaiveDate,
    to_date: NaiveDate,
    params: &HashMap,
) -> Option> {
    // Query Redis for all keys matching {slug}:v{version}:*
    // Decode stored metadata to find entries where
    //   stored_from = to_date
    // Filter records where date_field in [from_date, to_date]
    // Return filtered subset
}
```

**`frappe_flowbridge/flowbridge/api/cache_perf.py`**:
```python
def find_superset_cache(slug: str, schema_version: int, from_date: str, to_date: str, params: dict):
    """
    Returns filtered records from a superset cached entry, or None if no superset exists.
    Avoids origin re-fetch when the requested date range is fully covered.
    """
    # Scan Redis for keys matching {slug}:v{schema_version}:*
    # Deserialize stored entries (payload + metadata: stored_from, stored_to)
    # Find one where stored_from = to_date
    # Filter records by date
    # Return (records, [])  — no missing ranges
```

**Cache entry metadata format** (stored alongside payload):
```json
{
    "stored_from": "2026-01-01",
    "stored_to": "2026-03-31",
    "date_field": "posting_date",
    "schema_version": 1,
    "records": [...]
}
```

### Acceptance Criteria
- [ ] Request for `Feb 1–28` against a cached `Jan 1–Mar 31` entry returns filtered rows, `X-Cache: HIT-SUBRANGE`
- [ ] Request for `Dec 1–Jan 31` against `Jan 1–Mar 31` returns miss (partial overlap is not a superset hit)
- [ ] `X-Cache` header distinguishes: `HIT-EXACT`, `HIT-SUBRANGE`, `HIT-RAM`, `MISS`
- [ ] Filtering is applied on the `date_field` configured on the `FB API Endpoint`
- [ ] Performance: sub-range filter on 10k records completes in 0 = overrides)
```

### Logic in `gateway/src/api_gateway.rs`

```rust
fn get_effective_batch_size(config: &EndpointConfig) -> usize {
    if config.adaptive_batching && config.learned_batch_size > 0 {
        config.learned_batch_size as usize
    } else {
        config.batch_size as usize
    }
}

async fn update_learned_batch_size(
    ep_name: &str,
    config: &EndpointConfig,
    batch_size: usize,
    elapsed_ms: u64,
) {
    if !config.adaptive_batching { return; }
    let target = config.target_batch_duration_ms as f64;
    let ratio = target / f64::max(elapsed_ms as f64, 1.0);
    let adjustment = f64::max(0.8, f64::min(1.2, ratio));
    let new_size = (batch_size as f64 * adjustment) as i64;
    let new_size = new_size
        .max(config.min_batch_size as i64)
        .min(config.max_batch_size as i64);
    if new_size != config.learned_batch_size as i64 {
        // Write back to Frappe via internal API
        update_endpoint_field(ep_name, "learned_batch_size", new_size).await;
    }
}
```

After each batch:
```rust
let batch_start = std::time::Instant::now();
process_batch(&batch).await?;
let elapsed_ms = batch_start.elapsed().as_millis() as u64;
update_learned_batch_size(&ep.name, &ep, current_batch_size, elapsed_ms).await;
current_batch_size = get_effective_batch_size(&ep);  // re-read for next batch
```

### Acceptance Criteria
- [ ] `adaptive_batching=0` — always uses `batch_size`, `learned_batch_size` never written
- [ ] `adaptive_batching=1` — `learned_batch_size` updates after each batch
- [ ] Fast batches ( target_ms) — batch size decreases toward `min_batch_size`
- [ ] Adjustment never exceeds ±20% per batch (no oscillation)
- [ ] `learned_batch_size` visible in FB API Endpoint form (read-only via Desk)
- [ ] Converges to stable value within 10 batches on constant-latency source

# Phase 3 — Intelligence & AI

**Duration:** 3 months
**Goal:** Embed AI throughout FlowBridge — building, mapping, monitoring, and acting
**Layer:** Intelligence (AI & Insights)
**Depends on:** Phase 1 complete (Phase 2 can run in parallel)

---

## What We're Building

AI as a native layer, not a bolt-on module. From natural language flow building to ERPNext-grounded agents to a persistent Copilot that watches every flow and helps fix problems before they escalate.

> **All AI features in this phase route through the AI Engine layer.** See [phase_ai_engine.md](phase_ai_engine.md) for the full multi-provider architecture (Claude, OpenAI, Gemini, Ollama, and the Default Engine that requires zero LLMs). Phase 3 defines *what* the AI does. Phase AI Engine defines *how* it does it.

---

## Deliverables

### 1. AI Flow Builder
Describe what you want in plain English → FlowBridge builds the flow.

```
You type:
"When a new Razorpay payment comes in,
 find the matching Sales Invoice in ERPNext,
 mark it as paid, and send a WhatsApp
 receipt to the customer"

FlowBridge AI builds:
[Razorpay Webhook] → [Find Invoice] →
[Update Payment] → [WhatsApp Message] ✨

Ready in 10 seconds. Edit if needed.
```

- Natural language → node graph
- Connector-aware (knows which connectors exist)
- ERPNext DocType-aware (knows your schema)
- Suggests field mappings automatically
- Preview before accepting

### 2. AI Field Mapper (Full)
Upgrade from Phase 1's basic AI suggest:
- Vector similarity matching (semantic, not just string match)
- Learns from user corrections over time
- Handles nested objects & arrays automatically
- Cross-language field matching (English label → Hindi label)
- Confidence scoring per mapping
- Written in Rust (vector ops at machine speed)

### 3. ERPNext-Grounded AI Agents
Not generic LLM calls — agents that know your DocTypes, customers, stock.

```
FlowBridge AI Agent Node:
├── "Answer customer queries from ERPNext data"
├── "Auto-classify and route support tickets"
├── "Generate purchase orders from email requests"
└── "Reconcile payments using invoice history"
```

Agent capabilities:
- Read any ERPNext DocType via MCP (see MCP Server below)
- Create / update records with approval gates
- Multi-step reasoning with tool use
- ERPNext context injection (company, fiscal year, currency)
- Audit trail for every agent action

**Extended AI capabilities (beyond basic agents):**
- **RAG pipelines** — retrieval-augmented generation over ERPNext documents, emails, PDFs
- **Vector stores** — index ERPNext data (items, customers, orders) for semantic search
- **Chatbots** — deploy an ERPNext-aware chatbot for internal teams or customer-facing portals
- **Custom tools** — expose any ERPNext action as an AI tool callable by agents

### 4. MCP Server — AI Agent Ready
Auto-generates MCP (Model Context Protocol) server from ERPNext DocTypes.

```
FlowBridge MCP Server:
├── Auto-generates MCP tools from your DocTypes
├── Claude/GPT can directly:
│   ├── "Create a Purchase Order for 500 units of Item X"
│   ├── "What's the outstanding amount for Customer Y?"
│   ├── "Mark all overdue invoices and notify customers"
│   └── "Reconcile last week's Razorpay payments"
└── Business owners talk to ERPNext in plain English
```

- Auto-generates schema from Frappe DocType metadata
- Auth-aware (respects ERPNext roles & permissions)
- Read + write tools per DocType
- Expose as MCP endpoint for Claude Desktop, ChatGPT, etc.
- Built in Rust/Axum

### 8. Crypto & Advanced Transforms
Transforms that require cryptographic operations — run in Rust:

```
CRYPTO
  hmac_sha256(payload, secret)     ← sign API request body
  hmac_sha1(payload, secret)       ← for legacy APIs
  base64_encode(value)             ← binary/file encoding
  base64_decode(value)
  md5(value)                       ← some legacy APIs use MD5 signing
  aes_decrypt(value, key)          ← decrypt encrypted API responses
  sha256(value)                    ← content hash for integrity check

REGEX
  regex_extract("INV-(\d+)", 1)    ← extract invoice number from string
  regex_replace("-", "_")          ← clean up field values
  regex_match("^[A-Z]{5}\d{4}[A-Z]$")  ← validate PAN format

ARRAY (full set)
  join(items[*].code, ",")         ← array → comma string
  first(items)                     ← first element
  last(items)                      ← last element
  filter(items, qty > 0)           ← filter array before mapping
  count(items)                     ← length of child table
  pluck(items, "item_code")        ← extract one field from every row
  sum_field(items, "amount")       ← aggregate
  sort(items, "rate", "desc")      ← sort child table rows
```

All crypto transforms run in **Rust** via the same transform engine from Phase 1.

Published APIs:
├── GET  /api/flowbridge/products   → 1,200 calls today

└── GET  /api/flowbridge/stock      → 890 calls today

Per consumer limits:
├── Consumer A (Website):   1000 calls/hour  [Active]
├── Consumer B (Mobile App): 500 calls/hour  [Active]
└── Consumer C (Partner):    200 calls/hour  [Suspended]
```

- Generate & manage API keys
- Per-key rate limiting (lock-free Rust data structures)
- IP whitelisting
- Usage analytics per consumer
- Auto-suspend on abuse

# Phase 4 — API Platform

**Duration:** 3 months
**Goal:** Turn every ERPNext site into a proper API platform with GraphQL, WebSockets, developer portal, governance, and monetization
**Layer:** API Platform (Expose & Govern)
**Depends on:** Phase 1, Phase 3 (API Key Manager)

---

## What We're Building

Every ERPNext business deserves to expose their data like Stripe exposes theirs. Phase 4 turns FlowBridge into the API management layer that MuleSoft and Kong provide at enterprise scale — but for Frappe, self-hosted, at a fraction of the cost.

---

## Deliverables

### 1. Custom REST API Builder (Full)
Expose any ERPNext data as a REST endpoint:

```
/api/flowbridge/v1/orders
/api/flowbridge/v1/products
/api/flowbridge/v1/stock
```

- Define custom endpoint paths
- Choose which DocType fields to expose
- Control auth (API key, OAuth, JWT)
- Set rate limits per consumer
- Auto-generate Swagger/OpenAPI docs
- Request/response transformation
- Built on Rust/Axum (100k+ req/s)

### 2. GraphQL Gateway for ERPNext
ERPNext speaks only REST today. FlowBridge auto-generates a GraphQL API from DocType metadata:

```graphql
# One query instead of 5 REST calls:
query OrderDashboard($customer: String!) {
  customer(name: $customer) {
    name
    outstandingAmount
    orders(last: 10) {
      name
      grandTotal
      status
      items { itemName qty rate }
    }
    invoices(status: "Unpaid") {
      name
      dueDate
      outstandingAmount
    }
  }
}
```

- Auto-generated schema from Frappe DocType metadata
- Strongly typed → early error detection
- Mobile apps stop over-fetching data → faster performance
- Frontend teams work independently from backend
- No more building 20 custom API endpoints per client app
- Built in Rust/Axum

### 3. WebSocket / SSE — Real-Time ERPNext
ERPNext has zero real-time data push story today. FlowBridge fills this entirely:

```
ws://yoursite/flowbridge/live/stock-levels
→ Mobile warehouse app sees qty update instantly

ws://yoursite/flowbridge/live/orders
→ Dashboard shows new orders without refresh

ws://yoursite/flowbridge/live/payments
→ Finance team sees Razorpay payments land live

sse://yoursite/flowbridge/stream/alerts
→ Operations gets real-time threshold alerts
```

- Subscribe to any DocType change stream
- Filter by conditions (e.g., only orders > ₹10K)
- SSE fallback for one-way streams
- Auth-aware (ERPNext roles respected)

### 4. gRPC Support
gRPC for internal service communication and enterprise integrations:

```
Studio (Python) → Executor (Go):     gRPC
Executor (Go)   → Compute (Rust):    gRPC
Gateway (Rust)  → Queue:             gRPC
External:       expose ERPNext data  as gRPC for partner microservices
```

- 10x faster than REST for internal service calls
- Protobuf schema auto-generated from DocType metadata
- Enterprise customers integrate FlowBridge with their own microservice architectures directly

### 5. Developer Portal — Auto-Generated
Every ERPNext business suddenly has a professional API portal like Stripe — without building one:

```
Auto-Generated Developer Portal:
├── 📖 OpenAPI/Swagger docs for every custom API
├── 🧪 Live API playground (try it in browser)
├── 🔑 API key self-service (consumers generate own keys)
├── 📊 Usage dashboard per consumer
├── 📋 Changelog (API version history)
├── 🤝 Partner onboarding workflow
└── 💬 Support ticket per API endpoint
```

- Built in Frappe + React
- White-label ready (your branding)
- Embeddable in your own website

### 6. API Governance & Policy Engine
Policies applied per API or per consumer:

**Auth policies:**
- OAuth2, JWT, API Key, HMAC, mTLS
- IP whitelist/blacklist

**Traffic policies:**
- Rate limit (req/min per consumer)
- Quota (req/month per plan)
- Circuit breaker (auto-pause on errors)

**Transform policies:**
- Request/response header injection
- Field masking (hide sensitive data)
- Version translation (v1→v2 adapter)

**Compliance policies:**
- PII field detection & masking
- Audit log everything
- GDPR/DPDP data residency enforcement

### 7. AsyncAPI Specification Support
Auto-generate AsyncAPI specs for all event flows:

```yaml
asyncapi: '2.6.0'
info:
  title: FlowBridge - Shopify Orders
  version: '1.0.0'
channels:
  shopify/order/created:
    subscribe:
      message:
        payload:
          type: object
          properties:
            order_id: { type: string }
            total:    { type: number }
```

- Interoperable with MuleSoft, Kong, Azure API Management
- Opens the door to large enterprise deals
- Enterprise clients import your event APIs into their own tools

### 8. OpenAPI / Postman / WSDL Import — Zero-Setup Connector
Instead of manually declaring endpoints, user imports a spec and FlowBridge auto-generates the entire connector:

**Import sources:**
```
○ OpenAPI / Swagger URL
  https://api.myservice.com/openapi.json  [Import]

○ Postman Collection   [Upload .json]

○ Paste raw OpenAPI YAML or JSON

○ WSDL URL (SOAP APIs)
  https://einvoice1.gst.gov.in/service?wsdl  [Load]

○ RapidAPI / API Hub search   "Shiprocket"
```

**What gets auto-generated:**
- All endpoints with correct HTTP methods
- Auth type detection (Bearer, OAuth2, API Key, Basic)
- Request schema per endpoint (field names, types, required/optional)
- Response schema per endpoint
- Error response shapes
- User picks which endpoints to use — everything else is ready

**SOAP / WSDL connector:**
- Parses WSDL, auto-detects all operations
- FlowBridge builds the SOAP envelope automatically
- User just maps fields using the same nested key mapper from Phase 1
- Critical for: GST e-Invoice (IRP), E-way Bill, Income Tax Portal, SAP BAPI via SOAP gateway
```xml


  Import Data
  
    
      {{posting_date | date_format: "YYYYMMDD"}}
      {{customer_name}}
      {{#each items}}
      
        {{item_name}}
        {{qty}}
      
      {{/each}}
    
  

```

**GraphQL as outbound connector target:**
Not just inbound (Phase 4 GraphQL Gateway) — FlowBridge can also CALL external GraphQL APIs:
```graphql
# User writes query once in connector definition
query GetOrder($id: ID!) {
  order(id: $id) {
    id
    status
    lineItems { sku qty price }
  }
}
# Variables injected from ERPNext context
# Response mapped via same nested key mapper
```

For ERPNext businesses that want to sell their data:

API Monetization:

# Phase 5 — Data Platform

**Duration:** 3 months
**Goal:** Build the data movement and transformation layer — ETL, CDC, Unified Data Model, Two-Way Sync
**Layer:** Data Platform (Move & Transform)
**Depends on:** Phase 1

---

## What We're Building

FlowBridge Phase 1 handles event-driven flows (one event → one action). Phase 5 adds the data pipeline layer — bulk data movement, real-time change capture, and a unified semantic layer that makes integrations self-describing.

This puts FlowBridge in competition with Fivetran, Airbyte, and Merge.dev — in the ERPNext world specifically.

---

## Deliverables

### 1. Two-Way Sync Engine
The hardest problem in integrations — keeping two systems perfectly in alignment without infinite loops.

```
Sync Engine features:
├── Conflict resolution rules (which system wins?)
├── Deduplication fingerprinting
├── Loop prevention (don't re-trigger on synced changes)
├── Delta sync (only changed fields)
└── Sync health dashboard (drift detection)
```

**Use case:** Keep Shopify products ↔ ERPNext items perfectly in sync, both ways, forever.

Implementation:
- Each record gets a FlowBridge sync fingerprint (hash of synced fields)
- Outgoing changes tagged with FlowBridge origin header
- Incoming changes from FlowBridge-tagged sources skip re-trigger
- Conflict resolution: last-write-wins / source-wins / manual review queue

### 2. ETL / Data Pipeline Builder
A data pipeline mode alongside the flow mode:

```
ETL Pipeline Mode:
├── EXTRACT
│   ├── Pull from ERPNext (any DocType, paginated)
│   ├── Pull from external DBs (MySQL, Postgres)
│   └── Pull from APIs (paginated, scheduled)
│
├── TRANSFORM
│   ├── Visual transform designer
│   ├── Deduplication rules
│   ├── Data quality checks
│   ├── Schema normalization
│   └── Aggregations & rollups
│
└── LOAD
    ├── → Google BigQuery
    ├── → Snowflake
    ├── → Redshift
    ├── → PostgreSQL data warehouse
    └── → Another ERPNext site
```

**Reverse ETL (warehouse → ERPNext):**
Sync enriched analytics data back into ERPNext DocTypes for operations.
> e.g., BigQuery customer lifetime value scores → ERPNext Customer record

### 3. Change Data Capture (CDC) Engine
True real-time change detection — not polling, not Frappe doc_events:

```
CDC Engine:
├── Watches MariaDB binlog directly
├── Detects ANY change to ANY DocType table
├── Streams changes as events to flow engine
├── No polling — true real-time at DB level
└── 10x more efficient than Frappe's doc_events
```

Use cases:
- External data warehouse sync (BigQuery, Snowflake)
- Real-time reporting dashboards
- Audit trail for every field change ever
- Trigger flows on DB changes from imports, migrations, scripts
- Multi-site replication

Written in Rust — parses MariaDB binlog at machine speed.

**Why CDC > Frappe doc_events:**
- Works even during data imports (Frappe webhook bug — webhooks don't fire during import)
- Works for changes made via raw SQL, migrations, scripts
- Zero overhead on the Frappe Python process

### 4. Unified Data Model Layer
The hardest semantic problem in integration — every system calls the same thing differently.

```
FlowBridge Universal Schema:
├── "Customer" means the same thing whether it comes from:
│   Shopify (customer), Salesforce (Account),
│   Zoho (Contact), or ERPNext (Customer)
│
├── Unified models for:
│   ├── 👤 Customer / Contact
│   ├── 📦 Product / Item
│   ├── 📋 Order / Sales Order
│   ├── 💳 Payment / Journal Entry
│   ├── 🚚 Shipment / Delivery
│   └── 🏢 Company / Supplier
│
└── Map ANY external system to this unified model once →
    works with ALL flows automatically
```

- Map any connector to the unified model once
- New flows using that connector inherit the mapping
- AI-assisted mapping for new connectors
- ERPNext DocTypes are the source of truth for the unified schema
- This is what Merge.dev charges $650/month for — FlowBridge builds it natively

### 5. Document Intelligence Node
Intelligent document processing inside the flow:

```
Document Intelligence:
├── Extract data from uploaded PDFs/invoices
├── Auto-create Purchase Invoice from vendor PDF
├── Parse shipping manifests → update Delivery Notes
├── OCR + AI field extraction
└── Confidence scoring + human review queue
```

- PDF text extraction (Rust — fast, memory-safe)
- OCR for scanned documents
- AI field extraction with confidence scores
- Human review queue for low-confidence extractions
- Auto-create ERPNext records from extracted data

**This turns FlowBridge into an AP automation tool — a massive standalone market.**

### 6. Pagination Handler — Auto-Loop Any REST API
For GET APIs that return paged results — user declares the strategy once, FlowBridge handles the loop in Go:

**Supported strategies:**
```
○ Page number:    GET /orders?page=1&per_page=100  → auto-increments until empty
○ Cursor-based:   GET /orders?page_info={{cursor}} → reads next_cursor from response
○ Offset/Limit:   GET /items?offset=0&limit=250    → increments offset by limit
○ Link header:    reads RFC 5988 Link: ; rel="next" header
○ None:           single-page response (default)
```

**Config per endpoint:**
```
Page param name:   page
Size param name:   per_page
Page size:         100
Data field:        response.orders          ← where the array lives
Total field:       response.meta.total      ← optional, for progress
Stop condition:    response.orders.length == 0
```

- Go runs the pagination loop concurrently — pages streamed into flow as a continuous event
- Each page feeds into the next flow node (Transform / Load) without waiting for all pages
- Progress visible in flow run log: "Page 12/44 — 3,000/11,000 records"

### 7. Polling Trigger — Turn Any REST API into a Real-Time Trigger
For APIs with no webhook support (most Indian government APIs, many legacy services):

```
POLLING TRIGGER
├── Endpoint:     GET /api/orders?status=pending
├── Every:        5 minutes
│
├── DEDUPLICATION
│   Track by field:  order_id
│   → Only fire flow for NEW items since last poll
│
├── WATERMARK
│   Use field:    updated_at
│   → Next poll adds: ?updated_after={{last_seen_ts}}
│
└── BACKFILL
    On first run: go back 7 days
    Normal runs:  since last poll
```

- Go runs the poll loop — Redis stores watermark and seen-ID set
- Deduplication: seen IDs stored per connector per trigger, never fires twice for same record
- Jitter: randomize poll times slightly to avoid stampede on shared APIs

### 8. Batch API Support — Bulk Data Push/Pull
For sending or receiving thousands of ERPNext records to/from an external API:

**Outbound batch (push):**
```
Source:    ERPNext Items (filtered by item_group = "Pharma")
Total:     4,328 items

Strategy:  ○ Chunk into batches of 100
           ○ Parallel (10 batches concurrently via goroutines)
           ○ Sequential (one batch at a time)

Endpoint:  POST /items/bulk
Body:      { "items": [ ...100 items... ] }

On partial failure:
  ○ Stop all    ○ Skip failed batch    ○ Retry failed batch

Progress:  [████████░░░░░░░░] 2,300/4,328
```

**Inbound batch (pull all pages + stream to ERPNext):**
- Combined with Pagination Handler to pull all records from external API
- Transform & write to ERPNext DocType in batches (avoids DB lock on huge inserts)

### 9. CSV / Binary Response Handling
Extend response types for data-heavy APIs:

**CSV response:**
```
Row 0 (header):  order_id, status, amount, customer_email
Row 1+:          each row → one iteration of the next flow node

Column map:
  order_id      →  name
  status        →  custom_status
  amount        →  grand_total
  customer_email →  customer_email
```

**Binary / PDF response:**
```
Response:  application/pdf   (e.g., GST invoice PDF, label from Shiprocket)
Action:    Save to ERPNext Files doctype
           Attach to:  Sales Order  {{so_name}}
           File name:  "Invoice_{{name}}.pdf"
```

**Multipart response parsing:**
```
Part 1 (JSON):    metadata → map to DocType fields
Part 2 (binary):  file → save to ERPNext Files
```

### 10. Durable Event Queue & Guaranteed Delivery
Every trigger is persisted before processing — enterprise-grade reliability:

```
Event Queue:
├── Every trigger persisted before processing
├── At-least-once delivery guarantee
├── Dead letter queue for failed events
├── Replay any event from history
└── Out-of-order event handling (sequence correction)
```

- Critical for payment webhooks, compliance flows, pharma traceability
- Redis-backed durable persistence
- Full event history queryable from Studio
- One-click replay of any historical event

---

## Architecture Work (Phase 5)

| Component | Language | What to Build |
|---|---|---|
| Two-way sync engine | Go | Fingerprinting, conflict resolution, loop prevention |
| ETL pipeline runner | Go | Paginated extractor, transformer, loader |
| Pagination handler | Go | Page/cursor/offset/link-header strategies, stream to next node |
| Polling trigger engine | Go + Redis | Watermark store, deduplication set, jitter scheduler |
| Batch executor | Go | Chunked goroutine pool, progress tracking, partial failure handling |
| CDC engine | Rust | MariaDB binlog parser, change event emitter |
| Unified data model | Python/Frappe | Schema definitions, connector mapping storage |
| Document intelligence | Rust | PDF parser, OCR integration, AI field extractor |
| CSV response parser | Rust | Row streaming, header mapping, type inference |
| Binary response handler | Go + Frappe | Save to ERPNext Files doctype, attachment |
| Event queue (durable) | Rust + Redis | Persist-first queue, dead letter, replay API |
| Data warehouse connectors | Go | BigQuery, Snowflake, Redshift, Postgres adapters |

# Phase 6 — Enterprise & Compliance

**Duration:** 3 months
**Goal:** Multi-site orchestration, compliance certifications, team collaboration, embedded iPaaS, mobile app
**Layer:** Enterprise (Govern & Scale)
**Depends on:** Phase 1, Phase 2, Phase 4

---

## What We're Building

Phase 6 is what enterprise procurement teams specifically look for. It separates toy integrations from production-grade infrastructure and unlocks the ₹50,000+/mo enterprise tier.

---

## Deliverables

### 1. Multi-Site / Multi-Company Orchestration
Unique to the Frappe ecosystem — many enterprise customers run multiple ERPNext sites (one per company/branch). No competitor addresses this:

```
Multi-Site Flows:
├── Trigger on Site A → Action on Site B
├── Consolidate data from 5 company sites → HQ dashboard
├── Intercompany journal entry automation
├── Centralized credential management across sites
└── Flow templates pushed from HQ to subsidiaries
```

- Central FlowBridge hub manages multiple ERPNext sites
- Cross-site flow execution with auth federation
- HQ can push/pull flows to/from subsidiary sites
- Consolidated monitoring dashboard across all sites

**This alone is a reason for large ERPNext enterprise clients to pay for FlowBridge.**

### 2. Long-Running Workflow Engine (BPM)
Flows that span days or weeks — BPM (Business Process Management):

```
Purchase Approval Flow (3 days):
  Day 0: PO created → notify manager
  Day 1: If no response → escalate
  Day 3: If still pending → auto-reject

Onboarding Flow (30 days):
  Day 1:  Welcome email + portal access
  Day 7:  Check if KYC submitted
  Day 14: Follow-up if incomplete
  Day 30: Close application if abandoned

Collection Flow (ongoing):
  Invoice due → reminder D-3
  Invoice overdue → escalate D+1
  D+7 → legal notice trigger
  D+30 → write-off workflow
```

Features:
- Durable state across days/weeks (persisted in DB)
- Human-in-the-loop approval nodes
- SLA tracking & breach alerts
- Visual timeline showing where each instance is in the process

ERPNext has workflow states but zero orchestration intelligence. FlowBridge fills this entirely.

### 3. Human-in-the-Loop Nodes
Flows that pause and wait for human input:

**Approval node types:**
- Email approval — click Approve/Reject in email (no login required)
- WhatsApp approval — reply "1" to approve, "2" to reject
- ERPNext form review — pop up a custom review form
- Multi-approver consensus — need 2 of 3 managers to approve
- Timeout handling — auto-approve/reject after X hours

**Real use case:**
```
Vendor invoice > ₹5L arrives via email
→ FlowBridge extracts data (AI)
→ Matches to PO in ERPNext
→ WhatsApp to CFO: "Approve ₹6.2L invoice from Tata Steel? Reply 1/2"
→ CFO replies "1" from phone
→ ERPNext Purchase Invoice created & submitted
→ Payment scheduled automatically
```

### 4. Team Collaboration Features
Enterprise teams need governance over flows:

```
Team Features:
├── Role-based flow access (who can edit vs view vs run)
├── Flow approval workflow (changes need review before deploy)
├── Version history with diff view (who changed what)
├── Comments & annotations on flow nodes
├── Shared credential pools (team uses, nobody sees the key)
└── Audit log (compliance — who ran what, when)
```

### 5. OpenTelemetry Observability — Field-Level
Most platforms log at flow level. FlowBridge logs at field level:

```
Execution Log — Invoice Sync #4521
├── Flow: Razorpay → ERPNext Invoice
├── Trigger: payment.captured at 14:32:01
├── Step 1 — Find Invoice        ✅ 12ms
│   └── Matched: INV-2024-0891
├── Step 2 — Field Transform      ✅ 3ms
│   ├── amount: 15000 → grand_total: 15000  ✅
│   ├── currency: INR → currency: INR       ✅
│   └── paid_at: epoch → posting_date      ✅
├── Step 3 — Update Invoice       ❌ 8ms
│   └── FIELD ERROR: outstanding_amount
│       Expected: Float, Got: "15,000"
│       → Comma in number from Razorpay
│       → Suggested fix: Strip commas in transform
└── [Auto-fix] [Replay] [Suppress]
```

- OpenTelemetry export → Grafana, Prometheus, Datadog
- Enterprise customers plug into existing monitoring stack
- Structured logs with trace IDs across the entire Rust/Go/Python stack

### 6. Compliance & Certification Layer
For regulated industries — pharma, NBFC, healthcare:

**Data Lineage Graph:**
> "This field in ERPNext came from Razorpay via FlowBridge at 14:32:01 and was transformed by Rule #47"

**PII Detection & Masking:**
- Auto-detect Aadhaar, PAN, phone numbers in payloads
- Mask in logs, never stored in plain text

**Data Residency Controls:**
> "This flow must never send data outside India"

**DPDP Act Compliance (India):**
- Data Principal consent tracking
- Right to erasure flow automation

**Pharma 21 CFR Part 11:**
- Electronic signature on flow runs
- Audit trail tamper-proof

**SOC 2 / ISO 27001 ready:**
- Evidence export for audits

**DPDP Act compliance alone is a forcing function for every Indian business handling customer data in 2025–26. First mover here is enormous.**

### 7. Embedded iPaaS Mode — White Label
Turn every Frappe app developer into a FlowBridge reseller:

Embedded Mode:
├── Frappe app developers embed FlowBridge into THEIR apps
├── Their customers get integration studio branded as their product
├── Example:
│   └── A Frappe HRMS vendor embeds FlowBridge
│       → Their customers connect HRMS to

### 8. Flow Versioning & CI/CD Pipeline
Enterprise deployment discipline for integrations:

```
FlowBridge DevOps:
├── Git-backed flow storage (every change = a commit)
├── Environment promotion (Dev → Staging → Production)
├── Flow diff viewer (visual diff between versions)
├── Rollback in one click
├── CLI tool (flowbridge-cli) — deploy from terminal
└── GitHub Actions integration — auto-deploy on PR merge
```

### 9. OAuth2 Token Lifecycle — Fully Managed
User connects once. FlowBridge manages everything after, forever:

```
Setup:
  1. User clicks "Connect Zoho CRM"
  2. Redirect to Zoho OAuth consent screen
  3. Zoho redirects back with authorization code
  4. FlowBridge exchanges code for access + refresh tokens
  5. Stores AES-256 encrypted in credential vault

Auto-refresh (transparent):
  → Access token expires (e.g., 1 hour for most OAuth2 providers)
  → Any connector call returns 401
  → FlowBridge intercepts — uses refresh token to get new access token
  → Retries original request automatically
  → User and flow never see the failure

Token security:
  → Tokens never appear in logs, UI, or error messages
  → Per-flow credential scoping (Flow A cannot use Flow B's tokens)
  → Token rotation on suspicious activity
  → Expiry alert if refresh token itself expires (requires re-auth)
```

### 10. Outbound Rate Limiter — Per Connector
Protect against hitting external API rate limits (Rust token bucket algorithm):

```
RATE LIMITING (outbound)
├── Max calls:    300  per  minute
├── Burst limit:  50   simultaneous
│
├── On limit hit:
│   ○ Auto-queue and wait (recommended)
│   ○ Fail immediately
│   ○ Read Retry-After header from 429 response
│
└── Per-consumer (for multi-tenant deployments)

Current usage:  187/300 req this minute  [████████░░] 62%
```

Token bucket in Rust — lock-free data structures, zero overhead on hot path.

### 11. Advanced Retry Strategies — Per HTTP Status Code
Fine-grained retry control per connector endpoint:

```
On 429 (Rate Limited):   read Retry-After header → wait exact duration → retry
On 500 / 502 / 503:      exponential backoff — 1s → 2s → 4s → 8s (max 3 retries)
On 401 (Unauthorized):   refresh OAuth2 token → retry ONCE → fail if still 401
On 404 (Not Found):      do NOT retry — fail immediately (data issue, not transient)
On timeout:              retry after 5s → 10s → give up (max 2 retries)
On network error:        retry after 10s → 20s → 40s (max 5 retries)

After all retries exhausted:
  ○ Move to dead letter queue
  ○ Trigger error handler node in flow
  ○ Alert via WhatsApp / email
  ○ Auto-suspend connector if failure rate > 50% in 10 minutes
```

Circuit breaker: if a connector endpoint fails 10 times in 5 minutes, auto-pause it for 30 minutes and notify.

### 12. Response Caching — Per Endpoint
For slow or expensive APIs (GST number lookup, IFSC lookup, PIN code → city, holiday API):

```
CACHING
├── Endpoint:  GET /ifsc/{ifsc_code}
│
├── Cache:     ✅ Enable
├── TTL:       30 days
├── Key by:    ifsc_code  (path param)
│
└── Storage:   Redis
    → First call hits API, stores in Redis
    → Next 10,000 calls for same IFSC → instant, free

Cache hit rate:  94.7%
API calls saved: 4,823 this month  (₹1,200 saved in API costs)
```

Cache invalidation: manual flush, TTL expiry, or on specific response codes.

### 13. API Health Monitor — Per Connector
Real-time visibility into every external API your flows depend on:

```
CONNECTOR HEALTH  (last 24 hours)

Shopify API
  Status:       ● Healthy
  Uptime:       99.2%
  Avg latency:  340ms
  P99 latency:  1,200ms
  Errors:       14  (11× 429,  3× 503)
  Last error:   14 minutes ago

GST Portal API
  Status:       ● Degraded  ← proactive alert sent
  Uptime:       87.4%
  Avg latency:  4,200ms  (3× slower than baseline)
  Errors:       47 errors in last 2 hours
  [View incidents]  [Pause flows using this connector]
```

Proactive alert: "GST Portal API has been down 20 minutes — 47 e-invoices are queued and will retry automatically."

Metrics stored in ClickHouse/TimescaleDB — same analytics store as flow metrics (Phase 3).

### 14. Advanced Inbound Webhook Security
Extend Phase 1's basic HMAC verification with enterprise-grade security:

**Signature algorithms:**
```
○ HMAC-SHA256    ← Shopify, Razorpay, Stripe (Phase 1)
○ HMAC-SHA1      ← WooCommerce, GitHub (Phase 1)
○ RSA-SHA256     ← PayPal, some fintech APIs
○ Ed25519        ← Modern APIs (Svix, Clerk, etc.)
○ None           ← public endpoints
```

**Replay protection:**
```
✅ Reject requests older than 5 minutes
   Read timestamp from: X-Webhook-Timestamp
✅ Nonce deduplication
   Read nonce from: X-Webhook-Id
   Store in Redis for 10 minutes — reject duplicates
   → Prevents replay attacks on payment webhooks
```

**IP filtering:**
```
Allow only from: 13.235.0.0/16  (Shopify IP range)
                 54.187.174.169
                 [Import from provider's published IP list]
```

**Inbound rate limiting:**
```
Max 100 webhook calls/minute from same IP
On breach: return 429, log, alert
```

### 15. Chaos Mode — Advanced Sandbox Testing
Test your retry and error-handling logic without a real failure:

```
CHAOS MODE (activate per connector in sandbox)
├── Randomly inject 429 rate limit responses  (20% of calls)
├── Randomly inject 500 server errors          (10% of calls)
├── Randomly inject network timeouts           (5% of calls)
├── Inject slow responses                      (P99 = 8 seconds)
└── Inject malformed JSON response             (2% of calls)

→ Verify your retry config and error handlers work
→ Verify dead letter queue catches unrecoverable failures
→ Verify circuit breaker trips at the right threshold
```

### 16. Mobile App — Operations Command Center
Operations managers and CFOs never touch ERPNext desktop — but they'll use this daily:

```
Mobile App (React Native):
├── 📊 Flow health dashboard (Green/Red at a glance)
├── 🔔 Smart alerts ("Shopify sync failed — 47 orders pending")
├── ✅ Mobile approvals (approve/reject from notification)
├── 🔁 One-tap retry (fix failed flows without laptop)
├── 📈 Live metrics (orders syncing, payments landing)
└── 🚨 Incident response (who's working on the outage?)
```

---

| Component | Language | What to Build |
|---|---|---|
| Multi-site orchestrator | Python/Go | Site federation, cross-site flow executor |

| OAuth2 token manager | Rust | Token storage, auto-refresh on 401, rotation, expiry alert |
| Outbound rate limiter | Rust | Token bucket per connector, lock-free, burst support |
| Advanced retry engine | Go | Per-status-code strategy, exponential backoff, circuit breaker |
| Response cache | Rust + Redis | TTL cache keyed by path params, hit rate metrics |
| Connector health monitor | Go + ClickHouse | Uptime, latency P50/P99, error breakdown, incident log |
| Advanced webhook security | Rust | RSA, Ed25519 verify, nonce dedup in Redis, IP filter |
| Chaos mode engine | Go | Fault injection per connector in sandbox mode |

### 2. Multi-Protocol Connectivity (All in Rust)
FlowBridge speaks every protocol, not just HTTP:

```
Protocol Support:
├── HTTP/REST          ✅ (Phase 1)
├── GraphQL            ✅ (Phase 4)
├── WebSocket          ✅ (Phase 4)
├── gRPC               ✅ (Phase 4)
├── MQTT               🆕 → IoT devices, sensors
├── AMQP/RabbitMQ      🆕 → Enterprise message buses
├── Kafka              🆕 → High-volume event streams
├── SFTP/FTP           🆕 → Legacy file transfers
├── TCP Socket         🆕 → Tally, legacy ERPs
├── Email (IMAP/SMTP)  🆕 → Email as trigger/action
└── Serial/RS232       🆕 → Factory floor machines
```

**ERPNext IoT use cases:**
- Weighbridge → auto-create stock entry
- Temperature sensor → quality alert
- POS terminal → real-time sales entry
- Factory machine → production tracking

**This opens the manufacturing and IoT vertical entirely.**

### 3. Legacy System Bridge
Huge India market — your SAP background is a direct competitive advantage:

```
Legacy Connectors:
├── Tally Prime (XML/TCP socket protocol)
├── Busy Accounting (file-based sync)
├── SAP RFC/BAPI connector
├── SFTP/FTP file watchers
├── Email parsing (read structured emails as triggers)
├── Excel/CSV file drop triggers
└── Custom TCP socket (any legacy ERP with socket API)
```

### 4. Industry Packs
Ready-to-deploy templates for zero-configuration 80% use cases:

```
Industry Packs (one-click install):
├── 🏭 Manufacturing  → BOM sync, quality alerts, dispatch tracking
├── 💊 Pharma        → Batch traceability, expiry alerts, CDSCO compliance
├── 🛒 Retail        → Shopify/WooCommerce order sync, returns, GST
├── 🏗️ Construction  → Subcontractor billing, site progress webhooks
├── 🏦 NBFC/Finance  → Loan disbursement, repayment webhooks, KYC flows
└── 🚚 Logistics     → Shipment tracking, POD capture, rate card sync
```

Each pack contains:
- 5–15 pre-built flows for the industry
- Industry-specific connectors pre-configured
- Sample ERPNext data to test with
- Setup guide for the vertical

### 5. Open Connector SDK (Go)
Third-party developers can contribute connectors using the standard interface:

```go
type Connector interface {
    // Metadata
    Name()       string
    Version()    string
    AuthType()   AuthType  // OAuth2, APIKey, Basic, HMAC

    // Triggers (inbound)
    Triggers()   []TriggerDef
    HandleTrigger(ctx context.Context, payload []byte) (*Event, error)

    // Actions (outbound)
    Actions()    []ActionDef
    Execute(ctx context.Context, action string, params map[string]any) (*Result, error)

    // Testing
    TestConnection(ctx context.Context, creds Credentials) error
}
```

- Open source SDK published on GitHub
- Connector submission + review process
- Certified connector badge
- Connector published to Marketplace automatically on approval
- SDK documentation + example connectors

Turn the Frappe partner ecosystem into FlowBridge's sales force:

Certified Partner Program:
├── 🥉 Silver Partner
│   → Build 3 connectors, get listed

├── 🥈 Gold Partner

│
├── 🥇 Platinum Partner

│   └── White-label rights + dedicated PM
│
└── Connector Bounty Program
    → "Build GST e-invoice connector → Win ₹50,000"
    → Community builds what we can't
```

### 7. Connector Bounty Program
Crowdsource connector development:
- Public bounty board (GitHub issues with bounty labels)
- ₹10,000–₹1,00,000 per connector depending on complexity
- Community submits PRs against Connector SDK
- FlowBridge reviews, certifies, publishes
- Bounty paid on certification

### 8. Connector Health Status Page — Public & Per-Site
At ecosystem scale, connector reliability becomes a shared concern:

**Public status page** (`status.flowbridge.io`):
```
FlowBridge Connector Status

Shopify API       ● Operational
Razorpay API      ● Operational
GST Portal (NIC)  ⚠ Degraded       ← last updated 5 min ago
E-way Bill API    ● Operational
WhatsApp (Meta)   ● Operational
Tally TCP         ● Operational

[Subscribe to alerts for connectors you use]
```

**Per-site aggregated health** (from Phase 6 connector health monitor):
- When FlowBridge detects that 100+ sites are seeing errors on the same connector → auto-post to public status page
- Reduces false alerts (site-local config issue vs actual API outage)
- Community-reported incidents: any site admin can "Confirm this issue" on the status page

### 9. Connector SDK — HTTP Connector as First-Class Export
The Universal Connector (Phase 1) is already built. Phase 7 formalizes it as the open SDK:

**`connector.json` as the universal format:**
- A Universal Connector exported from any site is a valid Connector SDK submission
- No Go code required to contribute a connector — JSON + field mapping is enough for REST APIs
- Go code only needed for non-HTTP protocols (MQTT, Kafka, SFTP, TCP)
- Submission tool validates: auth config, endpoint schemas, sample payloads, no hardcoded secrets

**Connector categories for SDK submissions:**
```
Category A — JSON connector.json only (REST/GraphQL/SOAP via Universal Connector)
Category B — Go connector (MQTT, Kafka, TCP, SFTP, AMQP, email parser)
Category C — Rust connector (high-throughput, binary protocols, crypto-heavy)
```

### 10. Deployment Scaling

**Single Server (Small Business):**
```
├── Frappe/ERPNext (Python) — existing
├── FlowBridge API Gateway (Rust binary) — 20MB
├── FlowBridge Executor (Go binary) — 15MB
└── Redis (event queue)
```

**Enterprise / High Volume:**
```
├── Frappe cluster (existing)
├── Rust Gateway — horizontally scaled behind Nginx
├── Go Executor pool — auto-scale based on queue depth
├── Redis Cluster — durable event persistence
└── Prometheus + Grafana — flow metrics
```

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_flowbridge/docs/phase8.md
# Phase 8 — FlowBridge Cloud

**Type:** Separate cloud infrastructure (NOT a Frappe app)
**Duration:** 4 months
**Goal:** Zero-install managed cloud execution of FlowBridge — removes the self-hosting barrier for 70% of prospects
**Depends on:** Phase 1 complete (core engine must exist before cloud-wrapping it)

---

## The Problem It Solves

Every Phase 1–7 feature requires the customer to:
1. Have a Frappe/ERPNext server with enough resources
2. Install Rust binary + Go binary + Redis
3. Manage upgrades, backups, monitoring themselves

Most small ERPNext businesses (the biggest market segment) can't or won't do this.

**FlowBridge Cloud flips the model:**
```
Self-Hosted (Phases 1–7):
  Customer server → runs Rust gateway + Go executor + Redis

Cloud (Phase 8):
  FlowBridge's infrastructure → executes all flows
  Customer's ERPNext site → just a data source/destination
```

---

## Architecture

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              FlowBridge Cloud Platform                  │
│                                                         │
│  ┌─────────────┐  ┌──────────────┐  ┌───────────────┐  │
│  │ Multi-Tenant│  │  Tenant-Aware│  │  Cloud        │  │
│  │ Rust Gateway│  │  Go Executor │  │  Credential   │  │
│  │ (Axum)      │  │  Pool        │  │  Vault (HSM)  │  │
│  └─────────────┘  └──────────────┘  └───────────────┘  │
│         │                │                              │
│  ┌──────┴──────┐  ┌──────┴──────┐                      │
│  │ Redis       │  │ ClickHouse  │                      │
│  │ Cluster     │  │ (metrics)   │                      │
│  └─────────────┘  └─────────────┘                      │
│                                                         │
│  Tenant isolation: goroutine pools + namespace keys     │
│  Data residency zones: IN / EU / US                     │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         │ outbound API calls to customer's systems
         ▼
Customer's ERPNext site / Shopify / Razorpay / etc.
```

---

## Deliverables

### 1. Multi-Tenant Execution Engine
- Rust/Axum gateway with tenant namespace isolation
- One tenant's traffic cannot impact another's latency or throughput
- Per-tenant goroutine pool in Go executor (configurable max concurrency)
- Resource quotas per plan tier (flow runs/month, concurrent flows, API calls)
- Tenant onboarding in < 5 minutes (no server setup)

### 2. Cloud Credential Vault (HSM-Backed)
- Hardware Security Module (HSM) for key storage — not just AES-256 on a single server
- Customer credentials never stored in plaintext anywhere
- Envelope encryption: tenant key → wrapped by HSM master key
- SOC 2 Type II compliant credential storage
- Credential access audit log per tenant

### 3. Zero-Install Onboarding
```
Step 1: Sign up at cloud.flowbridge.io
Step 2: Enter your ERPNext site URL + API key
Step 3: FlowBridge Cloud connects and reads your DocType list
Step 4: Build your first flow
→ Live in under 10 minutes. No server. No install.
```

- ERPNext site agent (lightweight Python script installed on customer's Frappe — 50 lines)
- Agent handles inbound calls FROM FlowBridge Cloud to ERPNext
- All execution happens in FlowBridge Cloud, not on customer's server

### 4. Data Residency Zones
```
India zone   (Mumbai)     ← default for Indian customers
EU zone      (Frankfurt)  ← GDPR compliance
US zone      (Virginia)   ← US customers
BYOI mode                 ← bring-your-own-infrastructure
  → FlowBridge Cloud execution engine deployed IN customer's VPC
  → Data never leaves their network, cloud performance guaranteed
```

### 5. SaaS Billing & Plan Management
```
Cloud Starter    FREE
  ├── 3 flows
  ├── 500 runs/month
  └── 1 connector

Cloud Pro        ₹499/mo
  ├── Unlimited flows
  ├── 10,000 runs/month
  └── All connectors

Cloud Business   ₹1,999/mo
  ├── Unlimited runs
  ├── Team access (5 seats)
  ├── Priority execution queue
  └── Phone support

Cloud Enterprise  ₹9,999/mo
  ├── Dedicated execution pool
  ├── BYOI option
  ├── SLA 99.9% uptime
  └── Dedicated support engineer
```

- Razorpay subscription billing
- Usage metering: count flow runs, API calls, data volume per tenant
- Overage alerts + hard/soft limits per plan
- Plan upgrade/downgrade self-service

### 6. Bring-Your-Own-Infrastructure (BYOI) Mode
For enterprises that cannot send data to FlowBridge's servers:
- FlowBridge provides a Docker Compose / Helm chart
- Customer deploys it in their own AWS/GCP/Azure VPC
- Licensing key activates the cloud feature set
- FlowBridge manages updates remotely (customer approves before applying)
- Telemetry: only metrics (no payload data) sent to FlowBridge for billing

### 7. Cloud Dashboard
```
cloud.flowbridge.io → Tenant Dashboard

My Sites:
├── site1.erpnext.com     [Connected ✅]  47 flows active
├── site2.erpnext.com     [Connected ✅]  12 flows active
└── site3.erpnext.com     [Disconnected ⚠️]  [Reconnect]

This month:
├── Flow runs:     8,234  /  10,000 limit  (82%)
├── API calls:     41,200
├── Data moved:    1.2 GB
└── Uptime:        99.97%

Execution zone:  India (Mumbai)  [Change]
Plan:            Cloud Business  [Upgrade]
```

### 8. Migration Tool (Self-Hosted → Cloud)
For existing self-hosted FlowBridge users migrating to Cloud:
- Export all flows, connectors, credentials (encrypted) from self-hosted
- Import into FlowBridge Cloud tenant
- Re-enter credential secrets (security: never migrated in plaintext)
- Test each flow in cloud before switching DNS/webhooks
- Cutover with zero downtime

---

## Architecture Work

| Component | Language | What to Build |
|---|---|---|
| Multi-tenant gateway | Rust/Axum | Tenant routing, namespace isolation, quota enforcement |
| Tenant executor pool | Go | Per-tenant goroutine pool, resource limits, priority queue |
| HSM credential vault | Rust | Envelope encryption, HSM integration (AWS CloudHSM / Azure Dedicated HSM) |
| Onboarding API | Python/FastAPI | Tenant registration, site connection, DocType discovery |
| ERPNext site agent | Python | Lightweight script on customer Frappe, handles cloud→ERPNext calls |
| Billing engine | Python | Razorpay subscriptions, usage metering, overage tracking |
| Cloud dashboard | React (standalone) | Tenant management, usage, billing, zone selection |
| BYOI packaging | Docker/Helm | Self-contained deployment package, remote update manager |
| Migration tool | Python + Go | Export/import flows + connectors, credential re-entry wizard |

---

| Metric | Target |
|---|---|

# Phase 9 — Conversational Interface

**App name:** `flowbridge_messenger`
**Type:** Separate Frappe app (installs on customer's site)
**Duration:** 2 months
**Goal:** Let operators manage, monitor, and control FlowBridge entirely via WhatsApp and Telegram — no laptop required
**Depends on:** Phase 1 (core FlowBridge), Phase 3 (AI engine for NLP)

---

## The Problem It Solves

CFOs, operations managers, and business owners in India live on WhatsApp.
They will never open a Frappe dashboard.

FlowBridge Messenger puts everything they need — flow health, alerts, approvals, retries — directly into the messaging apps they already use, 24/7 from their phone.

---

## What Users Can Do via WhatsApp / Telegram

### Monitoring
```
User: "Status"
Bot:  📊 FlowBridge Status — Right Now

      ✅ 43 flows running
      ❌  3 flows failed (last hour)
      ⏸️  2 flows paused
      🕐  Last run: 2 minutes ago

      [Failed Flows] [Retry All] [Full Report]
```

### Failed Flow Details + Retry
```
User: [taps "Failed Flows"]
Bot:  ❌ Failed Flows (3)

      1. Shopify Sync — Rate limit (12 orders pending)
      2. Razorpay Recon — Invoice INV-2024-0891 not found
      3. GST e-Invoice — Portal timeout

      [Retry All] [Retry #1] [Retry #2] [Retry #3]

User: [taps "Retry All"]
Bot:  🔄 Retrying 3 flows...

      ✅ Shopify Sync — 12 orders synced
      ✅ Razorpay Recon — Matched and reconciled
      ❌ GST e-Invoice — Still failing (portal down)
         [Pause for 2h] [Alert CA]
```

### Flow Control
```
User: "Pause GST flow until tomorrow 9am"
Bot:  ✅ GST e-Invoice flow paused.
      Will resume: Tomorrow, 26 Apr 9:00 AM IST
      [Cancel Pause]

User: "How many orders synced today?"
Bot:  📦 Today's Shopify Sync

      Orders synced:   234
      Revenue:         ₹4,23,000
      Failed:          3 (auto-retried ✅)
      Last sync:       8 minutes ago
```

### Human-in-Loop Approvals (Extends Phase 6)
```
Bot:  🔔 Approval Required

      Vendor Invoice from Tata Steel
      Amount:   ₹6,23,450
      PO:       PO-2024-0234  ✅ matched
      Due:      30 Apr 2026

      [✅ Approve] [❌ Reject] [👁 View Details]

User: [taps ✅ Approve]
Bot:  ✅ Approved by you at 2:34 PM
      Purchase Invoice PI-2024-0891 created in ERPNext
      Payment scheduled: 28 Apr 2026
```

### Build Simple Flows via Chat
```
User: "When Razorpay payment received, update Sales Invoice"
Bot:  Got it. Building flow...

      [Razorpay Webhook] → [Find Sales Invoice] → [Mark Paid]

      Looks right?
      [✅ Yes, activate] [✏️ Edit] [❌ Cancel]

User: [taps ✅ Yes, activate]
Bot:  ✅ Flow activated. Running now.
      I'll notify you if anything fails.
```

### Daily Digest (Proactive)
```
Bot sends every morning at 8:00 AM:

  Good morning! 📊 Yesterday summary:

  💰 Revenue synced:    ₹8.4L (↑12% vs last week)
  📦 Orders:            234  (↑8%)
  🚚 Deliveries:        198  (98.3% SLA)
  ❌ Failed flows:       3  → auto-retried ✅
  🔔 Needs attention:   GST filing due in 3 days

  [View Dashboard] [Set Alerts] [Help]
```

## Deliverables

### 1. WhatsApp Bot (Meta Business API)
- Connect via Meta WhatsApp Business API (uses Universal Connector from Phase 1)
- Interactive message buttons (up to 3 quick reply buttons per message)
- List messages (for longer option sets like flow lists)
- Template messages for proactive alerts (pre-approved by Meta)
- Message status tracking (sent → delivered → read)
- Per-user auth: WhatsApp number → linked to Frappe user account

### 2. Telegram Bot
- Full Telegram Bot API integration
- Inline keyboards (buttons inside messages)
- No Meta approval needed — faster to launch than WhatsApp
- Ideal for developers and technical users
- Group chat support (post alerts to an ops team group)

### 3. Conversation State Manager (Redis)
- Tracks conversation context per user per channel
- Multi-turn conversations ("retry #2" knows what #2 was from previous message)
- Session timeout: 30 minutes of inactivity resets context
- Concurrent users: each user has isolated conversation state

### 4. NLP Intent Parser (Default AI Engine)
- Uses spaCy from Phase AI Engine (no LLM needed for most commands)
- Intent classification:
  ```
  "show failed flows"    → INTENT: get_failed_flows
  "retry shopify"        → INTENT: retry_flow, ENTITY: "shopify"
  "pause until 9am"      → INTENT: pause_flow, ENTITY: time("9am")
  "how many orders today"→ INTENT: query_metric, ENTITY: "orders", TIME: "today"
  ```
- For complex / unrecognized intents → LLM provider (Claude/OpenAI via Phase AI Engine)
- Confidence threshold: if  5 failures in 1 hour)
├── Connector down (> 10 consecutive failures on same connector)
├── Daily digest (8 AM, configurable time)
├── Weekly summary (Monday 9 AM)
├── Human-in-loop approval request (immediate)
├── Credential expiry warning (7 days before)
└── GST/compliance deadline reminders (configurable)

Per-user alert preferences:
├── Which flow failures to notify (all / only high-priority / none)
├── Quiet hours (no alerts 10 PM – 7 AM)
└── Channel preference (WhatsApp / Telegram / both)
```

### 7. Voice Note Support (WhatsApp)
- User sends voice note: "retry all failed flows and tell me what happened"
- FlowBridge transcribes using Whisper (local, via Default AI Engine)
- Parsed as text command → executed → responds with text + summary
- No cloud STT service needed (privacy preserved)

### 8. Multi-User & Role Awareness
```
CFO (WhatsApp): sees financial flows, approval requests, revenue metrics
Ops Manager:    sees all flows, retry controls, connector health
Developer:      sees all flows + technical error details + raw payload
Read-Only:      monitoring and digest only, no control actions

→ Permissions inherited from Frappe user roles
→ "Approve invoice" button only appears for users with approval permission
```

### 9. Settings & Configuration (in Frappe UI)
- Connect WhatsApp / Telegram credentials (via Universal Connector credential vault)
- Link Frappe users to phone numbers
- Configure alert preferences per user
- Customise daily digest content and schedule
- Set quiet hours
- Test connection ("Send me a test message")

---

## Architecture Work

| Component | Language | What to Build |
|---|---|---|
| WhatsApp connector | Python/Frappe | Meta API integration, template management, webhook receiver |
| Telegram connector | Python/Frappe | Bot API integration, inline keyboard builder |
| Conversation state | Python + Redis | Per-user session, context, timeout |
| NLP intent parser | Python (spaCy) | Intent classifier, entity extractor, confidence scorer |
| Action renderer | Python | State → message formatter, button builder |
| Alert engine | Python + Go | Trigger rules, quiet hours, digest scheduler |
| Voice transcription | Python (Whisper ONNX) | Local STT, no external API |
| Frappe DocTypes | Python | MessengerConfig, UserChannel, AlertRule, ConversationLog |
| Settings UI | React | Connect channels, user mapping, alert preferences |
