# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_flowbridge/docs/phase_ai_engine.md
# Phase AI Engine — Multi-Provider AI Architecture

**Duration:** Parallel to Phase 3 (Intelligence)
**Goal:** Build a pluggable AI engine layer with 5 providers — Claude, OpenAI, Gemini, Ollama, and a Default engine that requires zero LLMs
**Layer:** Intelligence (AI & Insights)
**Depends on:** Phase 1

---

## The Core Design Principle

FlowBridge AI is **provider-agnostic**. Every AI feature routes through a single `AIEngine` interface. The user picks the provider once in settings — all features work regardless of which one they choose.

```
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                  FlowBridge AI Router                 │
├───────────┬──────────┬─────────┬────────┬─────────────┤
│  Claude   │  OpenAI  │ Gemini  │ Ollama │   Default   │
│ Anthropic │   GPT    │ Google  │ Local  │  No LLM ✨  │
└───────────┴──────────┴─────────┴────────┴─────────────┘
         │
         ▼
  Same AI features for all providers:
  Field Mapping · Flow Builder · Agents
  Anomaly Detection · Copilot · Chatbot
```

**The Default Engine is the most important one** — it works out of the box, with zero API keys, zero external calls, zero cost. Businesses that can't or won't use cloud LLMs still get AI-powered FlowBridge.

---

## The 5 Providers

### 1. Claude (Anthropic)
```
Model options:
├── claude-opus-4-6     → Best reasoning, complex flows & agents
├── claude-sonnet-4-6   → Balanced: fast + smart (recommended default)
└── claude-haiku-4-5    → Fastest, cheapest, high-volume tasks

Best for:
├── AI Flow Builder (complex multi-step reasoning)
├── ERPNext AI Agents (tool use + reasoning)
├── FlowBridge Copilot (context-aware suggestions)
└── RAG pipelines (long context window)

Config:
├── API Key (ANTHROPIC_API_KEY)
├── Model selection
└── Max tokens, temperature per feature
```

### 2. OpenAI
```
Model options:
├── gpt-4o             → Top reasoning
├── gpt-4o-mini        → Fast + cheap (recommended default)
└── o1-mini            → Deep reasoning tasks

Best for:
├── AI Flow Builder
├── Function calling / tool use (mature API)
├── Embeddings (text-embedding-3-small for field mapping)
└── Fine-tuning (custom FlowBridge model later)

Config:
├── API Key (OPENAI_API_KEY)
├── Model selection
├── Embeddings model selection (separate from chat model)
└── Org ID (optional)
```

### 3. Gemini (Google)
```
Model options:
├── gemini-2.0-flash    → Fast + cheap (recommended default)
├── gemini-2.0-pro      → Best reasoning
└── gemini-1.5-flash    → Legacy, long context

Best for:
├── AI Flow Builder
├── Long document processing (large context window)
├── Multimodal (screenshot → flow suggestion)
└── Indian language support (Hindi field names etc.)

Config:
├── API Key (GOOGLE_API_KEY) or Service Account
├── Model selection
└── Region (important for data residency compliance)
```

### 4. Ollama (Local LLM)
```
Model options (self-hosted):
├── llama3.2:3b         → Fast, low memory (recommended for dev)
├── llama3.2:8b         → Better quality, needs 8GB VRAM
├── llama3.1:70b        → Production quality, needs GPU server
├── mistral:7b          → Good for code & structured output
├── qwen2.5:7b          → Excellent multilingual (Hindi support)
├── deepseek-r1:8b      → Strong reasoning
└── nomic-embed-text    → Embeddings without external API

Best for:
├── Air-gapped / on-premise deployments
├── Data residency requirements (no data leaves server)
├── DPDP / HIPAA compliant setups
└── Cost-sensitive high-volume workloads

Config:
├── Ollama server URL (default: http://localhost:11434)
├── Model name
└── Context length override
```

### 5. Default Engine (No LLM) ✨
```
Technology stack (all self-hosted, no API key needed):
├── Sentence Transformers  → Semantic understanding
├── FAISS                  → Vector similarity search
├── spaCy                  → NLP / entity extraction
├── scikit-learn           → Classification & prediction
├── RapidFuzz              → Fuzzy string matching
├── ONNX Runtime           → Run quantized ML models
└── Statistical methods    → Anomaly detection

Zero cost. Zero external calls. Works offline. Always available.
```

---

## The Default Engine — Deep Dive

The Default Engine is not a dumbed-down fallback. It is purpose-built for the **specific, narrow AI tasks** FlowBridge needs — and for those tasks it's actually faster and more predictable than an LLM.

### What it uses (and why it's not an LLM)

| Technology | What it is | What FlowBridge uses it for |
|---|---|---|
| **Sentence Transformers** (e.g. `all-MiniLM-L6-v2`) | Small BERT-based embedding model, ~22MB | Semantic field mapping — understands that `order.customer.name` → `customer_name` even with different wording |
| **FAISS** (Facebook AI Similarity Search) | Vector index library | Fast nearest-neighbor search over field embeddings, flow templates, connector schemas |
| **spaCy** (`en_core_web_sm`) | Industrial NLP library, ~12MB | Entity extraction from flow descriptions, connector name detection, intent parsing |
| **scikit-learn** | Classic ML library | Flow pattern classification, error categorization, anomaly detection (IsolationForest) |
| **RapidFuzz** | Fuzzy string matching (C++ under Python) | Fuzzy field name matching, connector action lookup, DocType name resolution |
| **ONNX Runtime** | Run any model as a portable binary | Run quantized NLP models without Python ML stack on Rust/Go services |
| **Z-score / IsolationForest** | Statistical anomaly detection | Detect flow failure rate spikes, unusual execution times, data volume drops |
| **TF-IDF + cosine similarity** | Classic IR technique | Suggest flows from Marketplace based on user's existing connectors |
| **Rule engine (custom)** | Decision tree over connector metadata | Flow builder for simple patterns (trigger X → action Y) without any ML at all |

### Feature-by-feature: Default Engine vs LLM

| Feature | What LLM does | What Default Engine does |
|---|---|---|
| **Field Mapper** | GPT understands semantics: `order_total` → `grand_total` | `sentence-transformers` embeds both field names; FAISS finds nearest match. Same result, 100x faster |
| **Flow Builder (simple)** | GPT generates node graph from description | Rule engine parses trigger/action intent with spaCy; matches to connector catalog with FAISS. Works for 70% of cases |
| **Flow Builder (complex)** | GPT handles multi-step reasoning | Falls back to template suggestions — "here are 3 similar flows, pick one" |
| **Anomaly Detection** | GPT analyzes logs and explains what's wrong | IsolationForest on execution time + error rate metrics. Flags anomalies. No natural language explanation |
| **Error Categorization** | GPT reads error message and explains it | spaCy + rule engine categorizes by known error patterns (HTTP 429 → rate limit, 401 → auth, etc.) |
| **Copilot Suggestions** | GPT gives contextual advice | TF-IDF similarity over flow history + known failure patterns. "You have similar flows to 847 other sites" |
| **Connector Suggest** | GPT understands what you're trying to do | FAISS over connector descriptions finds top-3 relevant connectors |
| **Chatbot** | Full LLM conversation | FAQ bot over ERPNext docs via vector search — answers factual questions, not reasoning |

### What the Default Engine cannot do (LLM only)

- Complex multi-step reasoning (e.g., "generate a 10-step flow from scratch")
- Freeform natural language input that falls outside known patterns
- ERPNext AI Agents (require tool use + reasoning)
- Contextual RAG over arbitrary documents
- Explaining errors in plain English (beyond pattern-matched messages)

These features are **gracefully degraded** — UI shows them as "requires AI provider" when Default Engine is active.

---

## AI Engine Configuration (User-Facing)

```
Settings → AI Engine

Active Provider: [ Default ▼ ]
  ○ Default (No API Key — works offline)
  ○ Claude (Anthropic)
  ○ OpenAI
  ○ Gemini
  ○ Ollama (Self-hosted)

──────────────────────────────────────
Per-Feature Override:

  Field Mapper:        [ Default  ▼ ] ← fast, no cost
  Flow Builder:        [ Claude   ▼ ] ← best reasoning
  AI Agents:           [ Claude   ▼ ] ← requires LLM
  Copilot:             [ Default  ▼ ] ← pattern-based is enough
  Anomaly Detection:   [ Default  ▼ ] ← statistical is better
  Chatbot:             [ Ollama   ▼ ] ← local, private

──────────────────────────────────────
API Keys (encrypted in Credential Vault):
  Anthropic:  [••••••••••••••] [Test ✅]
  OpenAI:     [not configured]
  Gemini:     [not configured]
  Ollama URL: [http://localhost:11434] [Test ✅]
```

**Per-feature overrides** let sophisticated users run the Default Engine for high-volume cheap tasks (field mapping, anomaly detection) and Claude only for reasoning-heavy tasks (agents, complex flow builder).

---

## Architecture

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    AI Engine Layer                      │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                         │
│   FlowBridge Feature                                    │
│   (Field Mapper, Flow Builder, Agent, Copilot)          │
│         │                                               │
│         ▼                                               │
│   ┌──────────────┐                                      │
│   │  AI Router   │  ← reads per-feature config         │
│   │  (Python)    │    picks provider                    │
│   └──────┬───────┘                                      │
│          │                                              │
│    ┌─────┴──────────────────────────────┐              │
│    ▼       ▼         ▼        ▼         ▼              │
│ Claude  OpenAI    Gemini   Ollama    Default            │
│  API     API       API    HTTP API   (Local)            │
│                                         │               │
│                               ┌─────────┴────────┐     │
│                               │  Default Engine  │     │
│                               │  (Rust + Python) │     │
│                               ├──────────────────┤     │
│                               │ sentence-        │     │
│                               │ transformers     │     │
│                               │ FAISS            │     │
│                               │ spaCy            │     │
│                               │ scikit-learn     │     │
│                               │ RapidFuzz        │     │
│                               │ ONNX Runtime     │     │
│                               └──────────────────┘     │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### Interface definition (Python)

```python
from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any

@dataclass
class AIRequest:
    feature: str          # "field_mapper", "flow_builder", "agent", "copilot"
    prompt: str           # user input or system-generated prompt
    context: dict         # ERPNext context (doctypes, connectors, flow history)
    max_tokens: int = 1000
    temperature: float = 0.2

@dataclass
class AIResponse:
    content: str | dict   # text or structured JSON
    provider: str         # which engine was used
    tokens_used: int      # 0 for Default Engine
    cost_usd: float       # 0.0 for Default Engine
    confidence: float     # 0-1, from Default Engine scoring

class AIEngine(ABC):
    @abstractmethod
    def complete(self, request: AIRequest) -> AIResponse: ...

    @abstractmethod
    def embed(self, text: str) -> list[float]: ...

    @abstractmethod
    def is_available(self) -> bool: ...

    @abstractmethod
    def supported_features(self) -> list[str]: ...
```

### Default Engine implementation stack

```
Language split:
├── Python    → sentence-transformers, spaCy, scikit-learn, FAISS bindings
├── Rust      → ONNX Runtime (fast inference), RapidFuzz (via PyO3), FAISS wrapper
└── Go        → anomaly detection metrics collector, statistical analysis

Model files (bundled with FlowBridge, no download needed):
├── all-MiniLM-L6-v2.onnx       22MB  → field mapping embeddings
├── en_core_web_sm               12MB  → spaCy NLP model
└── flowbridge_classifier.onnx   ~5MB  → custom trained flow intent classifier
                                ──────
                        Total:  ~40MB  → ships with FlowBridge
```

| Copilot | ✅ Pattern-based | ✅ Contextual | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| Connector Suggest | ✅ TF-IDF | ✅ Semantic | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| API Key needed | ❌ None | ✅ Anthropic | ✅ OpenAI | ✅ Google | ❌ Self-hosted |
| Data leaves server | ❌ Never | ✅ To Anthropic | ✅ To OpenAI | ✅ To Google | ❌ Never |
| Cost per call | ₹0 | Per token | Per token | Per token | ₹0 |
| Works offline | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |

---

## Why the Default Engine Matters

1. **Zero barrier to entry** — FlowBridge works with AI features on day one, no API key setup required
2. **Privacy by default** — for regulated industries (pharma, NBFC), data never leaves the server even for AI features
3. **DPDP Act compliance** — no customer data sent to US cloud providers
4. **Air-gapped deployments** — factory floors, government ERPNext installations, high-security environments
5. **Cost control** — field mapping on 10,000 records/day? ₹0 with Default Engine vs ₹500–2000/day with cloud LLMs
6. **Predictability** — rule-based components give deterministic output, critical for compliance audit trails
7. **Speed** — FAISS similarity search in Rust: ~0.1ms. GPT-4o API call: ~500ms. For high-frequency features like field mapping, this is a 5000x speedup

---

## Success Metrics

- [ ] All 5 providers pass the same feature test suite (provider-agnostic behavior verified)
- [ ] Per-feature provider override works in UI
- [ ] Graceful degradation: if Claude API is down, falls back to Default without breaking the flow
- [ ] Default Engine model bundle ≤ 50MB (ships with FlowBridge, no separate download)
- [ ] LLM cost per feature tracked and visible in admin dashboard
- [ ] Ollama works with at least llama3.2 and qwen2.5 models
- [ ] AI features clearly labelled in UI: which provider is active, what it can/cannot do

# FlowBridge — Master Build Plan

## Product Overview

FlowBridge is a 7-layer integration and API platform built on Frappe/ERPNext using a polyglot Rust + Go + Python architecture.

---

## Architecture Stack

```
FRONTEND          Frappe / React UI
ORCHESTRATION     Python (Frappe DocTypes, marketplace logic, permissions)
API GATEWAY       Rust (Axum) — sub-5ms, 100k+ req/s
FLOW EXECUTOR     Go — goroutines, concurrent flow execution
CONNECTORS        Go — standardized Connector interface
HEAVY COMPUTE     Rust — AI mapping, PDF parsing, crypto, CDC
```

---

## The 7 Layers (Product Vision)

| Layer | Name | What It Does |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Studio | Build & automate integrations visually |
| 2 | API Platform | Expose ERPNext as a proper API platform |
| 3 | Data Platform | Move & transform data at scale (ETL/CDC) |
| 4 | Connectivity | Connect to anything — HTTP, IoT, legacy, protocols |
| 5 | Intelligence | AI agents, MCP server, Copilot, anomaly detection |
| 6 | Enterprise | Multi-site, compliance, embedded iPaaS, mobile |
| 7 | Marketplace | Distribute, monetize, partner ecosystem |

---

## Build Phases

| Phase | Name | Duration | Priority |
|---|---|---|---|
| [Phase 1](phase1.md) | Studio Core | 3 months | Foundation |
| [Phase 2](phase2.md) | Marketplace Alpha | 2 months | Distribution |
| [Phase 3](phase3.md) | Intelligence & AI | 3 months | Differentiation |
| [Phase AI](phase_ai_engine.md) | AI Engine (Multi-Provider) | Parallel to Phase 3 | Foundation for all AI |
| [Phase 4](phase4.md) | API Platform | 3 months | Expansion |
| [Phase 5](phase5.md) | Data Platform | 3 months | Enterprise |
| [Phase 6](phase6.md) | Enterprise & Compliance | 3 months | Scale |
| [Phase 7](phase7.md) | Ecosystem & Connectivity | Ongoing | Moat |

## Extended Platform (New Apps & Services)

| Phase | Name | Type | App Name |
|---|---|---|---|
| [Phase 8](phase8.md) | FlowBridge Cloud | Cloud infrastructure (not Frappe app) | cloud.flowbridge.io |
| [Phase 9](phase9.md) | Conversational Interface | Separate Frappe app | `flowbridge_messenger` |
| [Phase 10](phase10.md) | Developer Experience Platform | External tools (CLI, SDKs, VS Code) | PyPI / NPM / GitHub |
| [Phase 11](phase11.md) | B2B EDI & Supply Chain | Separate Frappe app | `flowbridge_edi` |
| [Phase 12](phase12.md) | Financial Automation | Separate Frappe app | `flowbridge_finance` |

| Scale | Every Frappe site in the world is a potential customer |
| Pain | Custom integrations cost ₹50K–5L to build today |
| Community | Frappe community will build the product for you |
| Performance | Rust/Go vs Zapier/n8n Python/Node — 100x faster |
| Platform | Becomes infrastructure — sticky like AWS |

| 4.24 | GraphQL outbound connector (call external GraphQL APIs as action) | [x] Done | `call_graphql_api(endpoint, query, variables, connector_name)` — loads auth from FB Credential vault; `build_graphql_connector(api_endpoint)` — runs introspection query, creates FB Connector + endpoints per Query/Mutation field; `graphql` node type wired into executor |

## Phase 16 — High-Performance API Platform

> See [phase16.md](phase16.md) for full architecture diagram, performance targets, and file layout.
> Three pillars: (A) Feature completeness, (B) Event-driven triggers, (C) Rust/Axum + Go performance layer.

### 16-A: Feature Completeness (Python)

|---|---|---|---|

## Phase 18 — Performance Hardening & Benchmarking

> **Cross-cutting reference:** [performance.md](performance.md) — SLO targets, multi-layer cache design, DataLoader pattern, compression, HTTP/2, connection pooling, full benchmark suite.
> See [phase18.md](phase18.md) for all deliverables.
>
> **Core principle:** Every generated API passes through Rust/Go first. Python/Frappe is only called for `custom_logic`, DocType hooks, and workflow transitions. The hot API path never waits for gunicorn.

### 18-A: Query Engine Optimization

| # | Deliverable | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 18.1 | DataLoader / batch query engine | [x] Done | `BatchLoader` class — one `frappe.get_all(IN [...])` per unique (doctype, link_field) per depth level; eliminates N+1 |
| 18.2 | Parallel join resolution (same depth level) | [x] Done | Independent joins at same tree level fire concurrently via `ThreadPoolExecutor` (Python) + `tokio::join!` (Rust) |
| 18.3 | Request-scope memoization | [x] Done | `_request_memo[(doctype,name)]` — prevents duplicate fetches in diamond/self-referencing graphs |
| 18.4 | Auto index suggestion | [x] Done | `?_debug=1` runs EXPLAIN; detects full table scans + filesort; surfaces `CREATE INDEX` suggestions in Desk form |
| 18.5 | Covering index hints | [x] Done | `USE INDEX (name)` hints emitted for confirmed indexes on frequently-queried (filter+order+fields) combos |
| 18.6 | Read replica routing | [x] Done | GET → read replica sqlx pool; writes → primary; 500ms consistency window post-write |
| 18.7 | ProxySQL integration | [x] Done | Query routing rules; 1000 app → 50 MariaDB connections; MySQL-protocol query cache |

### 18-B: Rust Gateway Performance

| # | Deliverable | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 18.8 | Zero-copy cache hit response | [x] Done | Redis bytes served as raw `Bytes` — no deserialize+reserialize; zero allocations |
| 18.9 | LRU in-process DashMap cache | [x] Done | 10k entry LRU;  1k rows; first rows in `; no Redis round-trip; evicts idle buckets after 5 min |

### 18-C: Go Worker Performance

| # | Deliverable | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 18.16 | Configurable goroutine pool | [x] Done | `MAX_WORKERS` env var; buffered channel (1000 capacity) absorbs write bursts |
| 18.17 | Bulk write single transaction | [x] Done | 1000 inserts in one `BEGIN...COMMIT` — 50× faster than individual transactions |
| 18.18 | Prepared statement cache (Go) | [x] Done | `db.PrepareContext` per doctype pattern; eliminates SQL parse overhead |
| 18.19 | Async flow trigger (non-blocking) | [x] Done | `go func(){}()` — flow trigger never blocks write response path |
| 18.20 | Back-pressure handling | [x] Done | Channel full → `503 Service Busy` + `Retry-After: 1`; prevents memory exhaustion |

### 18-D: Real-Time Performance

| # | Deliverable | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 18.21 | Tokio async SSE — 50k connections/node | [x] Done | Each SSE = one tokio task; 50k connections ≈ 500 MB RAM; shared Redis reader fans out via mpsc |
| 18.22 | Redis Streams for SSE persistence | [x] Done | `fb:rt:{slug}` stream MAXLEN 1000; `XREAD {Last-Event-ID}` on reconnect; no missed events |
| 18.23 | WebSocket per-message deflate | [x] Done | `permessage-deflate`; 10 KB → 2 KB; CPU  100k req/s p99  See [phase19.md](phase19.md) for full deliverables and completion criteria.

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_flowbridge/docs/universal_connector.md
# Universal Connector — Complete Reference

**The single most important feature in FlowBridge.**

> Connect ERPNext to any REST, GraphQL, SOAP, or file-based API in the world — without writing code. Build once, share to thousands via the Marketplace.

---

## What It Is

The Universal Connector is a no-code API integration builder that lets any user declare:
1. The external API endpoint (URL, method, auth)
2. How to map ERPNext fields → API request (including deeply nested objects and arrays)
3. How to map the API response → back to ERPNext fields

The path resolver, transform engine, and response mapper all run in **Rust** — no Python overhead, handles 50,000-row child tables in milliseconds.

---

## Phase Rollout

| Feature | Phase |
|---|---|
| Core endpoint declaration (URL, method, headers) | Phase 1 |
| All 9 auth types | Phase 1 |
| JSON + Form + XML template + Multipart body formats | Phase 1 |
| Nested key mapper (flat↔nested, array wildcard) | Phase 1 |
| Conditional mapping + default values + null handling | Phase 1 |
| Core transform library (math, string, date, lookup, coerce) | Phase 1 |
| Multi-step API chains | Phase 1 |
| Basic retry (fixed attempts) | Phase 1 |
| Basic inbound webhook security (HMAC-SHA256/SHA1) | Phase 1 |
| Connector sandbox (static mock + record & replay) | Phase 1 |
| Connector versioning + rollback | Phase 2 |
| Connector Marketplace (publish + install) | Phase 2 |
| AI Connector Builder (plain English → connector) | Phase 3 |
| Crypto + Regex + Array transform library (full) | Phase 3 |
| OpenAPI / Swagger import | Phase 4 |
| Postman Collection import | Phase 4 |
| SOAP / WSDL import | Phase 4 |
| GraphQL as outbound connector target | Phase 4 |
| Pagination handler (page / cursor / offset / link-header) | Phase 5 |
| Polling trigger with watermark + deduplication | Phase 5 |
| Batch API support (chunked, parallel goroutines) | Phase 5 |
| CSV response parsing | Phase 5 |
| Binary / PDF response → ERPNext Files | Phase 5 |
| OAuth2 full token lifecycle (auto-refresh) | Phase 6 |
| Outbound rate limiter per connector (token bucket) | Phase 6 |
| Advanced retry per HTTP status code + circuit breaker | Phase 6 |
| Response caching (Redis, configurable TTL) | Phase 6 |
| Connector health monitor (uptime, latency, error rate) | Phase 6 |
| Advanced inbound webhook security (RSA, Ed25519, replay protection, IP filter) | Phase 6 |
| Chaos mode (fault injection for sandbox testing) | Phase 6 |
| Public connector status page | Phase 7 |
| Connector SDK (`connector.json` format, no-code submissions) | Phase 7 |

---

## User Interface Flow

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  NEW CONNECTOR                                          │
│                                                         │
│  Name:     My Logistics API                             │
│  Base URL: https://api.mylogistics.com/v2               │
│  Auth:     Bearer Token  ▼                              │
│                                                         │
│  IMPORT FROM  [OpenAPI URL]  [Postman]  [WSDL]         │
│  or build manually ↓                                    │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  ENDPOINTS                                    [+ Add]   │
│  ├── POST /shipments              Create Shipment       │
│  ├── GET  /shipments/{id}/track   Get Tracking          │
│  └── PUT  /shipments/{id}/cancel  Cancel Shipment       │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  [Test Connection ✅]  [Sandbox]  [Export]  [Publish]  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

## Endpoint Declaration

```
Endpoint Name:  Create Shipment
Method:         POST ▼
Path:           /shipments

Headers:
  Content-Type:      application/json
  X-Client-ID:       {{credential.client_id}}
  X-Timestamp:       {{now | to_unix_timestamp}}

Auth:           Bearer Token (from credential vault)
```

### Dynamic URL Path & Query Params

```
Path template:   /orders/{order_id}/items/{item_id}
Path params:
  order_id   →  {{sales_order.name}}
  item_id    →  {{item.row_id}}

Query params:
  include    →  "customer,items"          (static)
  currency   →  {{company.default_currency}}  (dynamic)
  from_date  →  {{today | date_format: "YYYY-MM-DD"}}
  status     →  {{filter.status}}  IF NOT NULL  (conditional)
```

---

## Request Body Formats

### JSON (default)
```json
{
  "shipment": {
    "reference": "{{sales_order.name}}",
    "receiver": {
      "name": "{{customer.customer_name}}",
      "phone": "{{customer.mobile_no}}",
      "address": {
        "line1": "{{address.address_line1}}",
        "city":  "{{address.city}}",
        "pin":   "{{address.pincode}}"
      }
    },
    "items": [
      {
        "sku":    "{{items[*].item_code}}",
        "qty":    "{{items[*].qty}}",
        "weight": "{{items[*].weight_per_unit}}"
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

### Form URL-Encoded (payment gateways)
```
amount=5000&currency=INR&order_id={{name}}&customer={{customer_name}}
```

### XML Template (Tally, GST, government APIs)
```xml

  
    Import Data
  
  
    
      {{posting_date | date_format: "YYYYMMDD"}}
      Sales
      {{customer_name}}
      {{#each items}}
      
        {{item_name}}
        {{qty}}
        {{rate}}
      
      {{/each}}
    
  

```

### Multipart / form-data (file upload APIs)
```
Part 1: invoice_data (application/json)   →  {{payload as json}}
Part 2: pdf_file (application/pdf)        →  {{file.content}}  from ERPNext File
Part 3: signature (text/plain)            →  {{hmac_sha256(payload, secret)}}
```

### GraphQL (outbound)
```graphql
query GetOrder($id: ID!) {
  order(id: $id) {
    id
    status
    lineItems { sku qty unitPrice }
  }
}
# Variables:
{ "id": "{{sales_order.shopify_order_id}}" }
```

---

## Nested Key Mapper

### Flat → Nested (ERPNext → API request)

| ERPNext Field | API Path | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `customer_name` | `shipment.receiver.name` | 2 levels deep |
| `mobile_no` | `shipment.receiver.contact.phone` | 3 levels deep |
| `address_line1` | `shipment.receiver.address.line1` | 3 levels deep |
| `pincode` | `shipment.receiver.address.postal_code` | rename + nest |
| `items[*].item_code` | `order.line_items[*].sku` | array wildcard |
| `items[*].qty` | `order.line_items[*].quantity` | array wildcard |
| `items[*].rate` | `order.line_items[*].unit_price` | array wildcard |

### Nested → Flat (API response → ERPNext)

| API Response Path | ERPNext Field | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `shipment_id` | `custom_tracking_id` | top-level |
| `data.order.status.code` | `custom_status` | 3 levels deep |
| `data.pricing.total` | `grand_total` | rename |
| `data.items[*].sku` | `items[*].item_code` | array back |
| `errors[0].message` | `flow_run.error_message` | index access |

### XML / XPath Response
```
XPath: //ShipmentResponse/TrackingNumber/text()
→  delivery_note.tracking_number

XPath: //Items/Item/Rate/text()   (repeating nodes)
→  items[*].rate
```

### Conditional Mapping
```
gst_number       →  tax_id           ONLY IF  country == "India"
discount_amount  →  discount         ONLY IF  discount_amount > 0
items[*].batch   →  items[*].batch_no ONLY IF  has_batch_no == true
shipping_address →  address_display  IF NOT NULL  ELSE  billing_address
```

---

## Transform Library

### Math
```
multiply(n)          items[*].rate multiply(1.18)  ← add 18% GST
divide(n)            amount divide(100)            ← paise to rupees
round(n)             grand_total round(2)
abs()
sum(items[*].amount)
```

### String
```
uppercase / lowercase / titlecase
trim
concat(first_name, " ", last_name)
substr(0, 10)
pad_left("0", 6)                   "42" → "000042"
strip_html
```

### Date
```
date_format("DD-MM-YYYY")
date_format("YYYYMMDD")            ← Tally format
date_format("YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm:ssZ")  ← ISO 8601
add_days(30)
to_unix_timestamp
from_unix_timestamp
timezone_convert("Asia/Kolkata")
```

### Type Coercion
```
to_string / to_integer / to_float / to_boolean
parse_json(field)                  ← string field that contains JSON
stringify_json(object)
```

### Conditional
```
if(qty > 0, qty, 0)
coalesce(field1, field2, "default")
nullif(value, "N/A")               ← treat "N/A" as null
```

### Lookup Table
```json
{
  "DELIVERED": "Delivered",
  "IN_TRANSIT": "In Transit",
  "RTO": "Returned to Origin",
  "default": "Unknown"
}
```

### Crypto (Phase 3 — Rust)
```
hmac_sha256(payload, secret)       ← sign API request body
hmac_sha1(payload, secret)
base64_encode(value)
base64_decode(value)
md5(value)
aes_decrypt(value, key)
sha256(value)
```

### Regex (Phase 3 — Rust)
```
regex_extract("INV-(\d+)", 1)      "INV-2045" → "2045"
regex_replace("-", "_")
regex_match("^[A-Z]{5}\d{4}[A-Z]$")  ← boolean validate PAN
```

### Array (Phase 3 — Rust)
```
join(items[*].code, ",")           ← array → comma string
first(items)
last(items)
filter(items, qty > 0)
count(items)
pluck(items, "item_code")          ← extract one field from every row
sum_field(items, "amount")
sort(items, "rate", "desc")
```

---

## Authentication Types

| Type | What user fills in | Stored |
|---|---|---|
| Bearer Token | Token value | AES-256 encrypted vault |
| API Key Header | Header name + key | AES-256 encrypted vault |
| API Key Query Param | Param name + key | AES-256 encrypted vault |
| Basic Auth | Username + password | AES-256 encrypted vault |
| OAuth2 Client Credentials | Client ID, Secret, Token URL | Tokens auto-managed |
| OAuth2 Authorization Code | Client ID, Secret, Auth URL, Token URL | Tokens auto-managed |
| HMAC Signature | Secret + algorithm + header name | AES-256 encrypted vault |
| Custom Headers | Any key-value pairs | AES-256 encrypted vault |
| No Auth | — | — |

**OAuth2 lifecycle (Phase 6):** FlowBridge handles token exchange, storage, auto-refresh on 401, and rotation automatically. User clicks "Connect" once — never needs to re-authenticate unless the refresh token itself expires.

---

## Response Handling

### Success / Error Codes
```
Success codes:  200, 201, 202  (customizable per endpoint)
Error codes:    4xx → route to error handler node
                5xx → retry engine
```

### Response Formats
```
JSON      →  standard nested key mapper
XML       →  XPath extraction
CSV       →  row streaming, column-to-field mapping (Phase 5)
PDF/Binary →  save to ERPNext Files doctype (Phase 5)
Plain text →  raw string into a single field
```

### Error Message Extraction
```
Error message path:  error.details[0].message
Error code path:     error.code
→ Available as {{flow_run.error_message}} in error handler node
```

---

## Multi-Step API Chains

For integrations that need sequential calls where each depends on the previous:

```
Step 1: GET /customers?email={{customer_email}}
        Extract: response[0].external_id  → {{ext_customer_id}}

Step 2: POST /orders
        Body: { "customer_id": "{{ext_customer_id}}", ... }
        Extract: response.order_id  → {{ext_order_id}}

Step 3: POST /orders/{{ext_order_id}}/fulfillments
        Body: { "items": [...] }
        Extract: response.fulfillment_id  → {{flow_run.fulfillment_id}}
```

Declared as a single connector ACTION — executor runs steps sequentially, context passes forward. User sees one node in the flow designer.

---

## Pagination (Phase 5)

```
Strategy:    Cursor-based  ▼

Data field:  response.orders
Next cursor: response.next_cursor    ← path in response that holds next page token
Page param:  page_info               ← query param name to pass cursor in
Stop when:   response.next_cursor IS NULL

First call:  GET /orders?limit=250
Next calls:  GET /orders?limit=250&page_info={{cursor}}
Stop:        when next_cursor is absent
```

Strategies: Page number · Cursor · Offset/Limit · RFC 5988 Link header

---

## Polling Trigger (Phase 5)

For APIs with no webhook support:

```
Endpoint:    GET /api/orders?status=pending
Every:       5 minutes

Watermark:   updated_at
→ Adds: ?updated_after={{last_seen_ts}} to each poll

Deduplication track field:  order_id
→ Only fires flow for NEW items not seen in previous polls
```

---

## Batch Mode (Phase 5)

```
Source:      ERPNext Items (4,328 records)
Strategy:    Parallel  — 10 batches of 100, 10 goroutines concurrently
Endpoint:    POST /items/bulk
On failure:  Retry failed batches only
```

---

## Inbound Webhook Listener

Every connector generates a unique listener URL:
```
https://yoursite.com/flowbridge/wh/{connector_slug}/{flow_id}
```

Security (Phase 1: HMAC · Phase 6: RSA, Ed25519, replay protection, IP filter):
```
Signature algorithm:  HMAC-SHA256
Header:               X-Webhook-Signature
Secret:               (from credential vault)

Replay protection (Phase 6):
  Reject requests older than 5 minutes
  Track nonce in Redis for 10 minutes → reject duplicates

IP allowlist (Phase 6):
  13.235.0.0/16  (Shopify)
  54.187.174.169
```

ACK strategy: Rust/Axum returns `200 OK` in < 5ms, queues to Go executor — never keeps the caller waiting.

---

## Reliability (Phase 6)

### Outbound Rate Limiter (Rust token bucket)
```
Max calls:    300/minute
Burst:        50 simultaneous
On 429:       read Retry-After header → wait → retry
```

### Retry Strategy (per status code)
```
429  →  read Retry-After → wait exact duration → retry
500+ →  exponential backoff: 1s → 2s → 4s → 8s (max 3 retries)
401  →  refresh OAuth2 token → retry once
404  →  do NOT retry (data issue)
timeout → retry: 5s → 10s (max 2)
```

### Circuit Breaker
```
10 failures in 5 minutes → auto-pause connector for 30 minutes → alert
```

### Response Caching (Redis)
```
Endpoint:  GET /ifsc/{code}
TTL:       30 days
Key by:    code (path param)
→ First call hits API, all subsequent serve from Redis
```

---

## Sandbox & Testing

```
Static mock       →  paste expected response JSON, flow runs against it
Record & replay   →  capture one real API call, replay for all test runs
Chaos mode (P6)   →  inject 429s, 500s, timeouts, slow responses to test retry logic
```

---

## Connector Package Format (Marketplace)

```
connector_package/
├── connector.json        ← all endpoint definitions, auth type
├── auth_schema.json      ← what credentials the installer must provide
├── sample_payloads/
│   ├── create_shipment.request.json
│   ├── create_shipment.response.json
│   └── get_tracking.response.json
├── README.md             ← setup instructions
└── changelog.md
```

A `connector.json` built with the Universal Connector UI is a valid Connector SDK submission — no code required for REST/GraphQL/SOAP connectors.

---

## Architecture

```
User fills in connector definition
         │
         ▼
ConnectorEndpoint DocType  ←  Python/Frappe stores config
         │
         ▼
Go Executor picks up flow run
  → Reads endpoint definition
  → Calls Path Resolver (Rust): builds nested request payload
  → Calls Transform Engine (Rust): applies all field transforms
  → Calls HTTP client (Go): sends request with correct auth
  → Rust/Axum Gateway: rate limits, logs, routes inbound webhooks
         │
         ▼
Response received
  → Response Mapper (Rust): extracts fields via dot-notation / XPath
  → Writes back to ERPNext via Frappe Python API
```

**Why Rust for mapping + transforms:**
- 50,000-row child table (SAP BAPI response) mapped in < 500ms
- Crypto operations (HMAC, AES) in microseconds
- XPath on large XML responses without memory allocation spikes
- Zero-copy path resolution on large nested JSON payloads

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_flowbridge/docs/usecases.md
# FlowBridge — Use Cases

Real-world integration and automation scenarios organized by industry and function.

---

## E-Commerce & Retail

### Shopify → ERPNext Order Sync

**Problem:** Orders placed on Shopify need to appear in ERPNext as Sales Orders within seconds, without manual data entry.

**Solution:**
1. Webhook Trigger on Shopify `orders/paid` event
2. HTTP Action node calls the Shopify connector's `get_order` endpoint to fetch full order details
3. Loop node iterates over line items
4. Create Doc node creates a Sales Invoice in ERPNext with mapped fields
5. HTTP Action sends an order confirmation via WhatsApp Business API

**Result:** Orders appear in ERPNext within 3 seconds of payment. Zero manual entry. WhatsApp confirmation sent automatically.

**Install:** Available as a pre-built Marketplace flow — search "Shopify ERPNext Sync".

---

### WooCommerce Inventory Sync

**Problem:** Stock levels in ERPNext and WooCommerce drift out of sync, causing overselling.

**Solution:**
- DocType Event Trigger on `Stock Entry` (submit)
- FlowBridge reads the updated item's warehouse stock
- HTTP Action calls the WooCommerce REST API to update the product's `stock_quantity`
- Condition node skips items not listed in WooCommerce (no `woocommerce_product_id` set)

**Result:** WooCommerce stock is updated within 5 seconds of every ERPNext warehouse transaction.

---

### Razorpay Payment → ERPNext Payment Entry

**Problem:** Payments collected on the Razorpay payment page need to be reconciled with Sales Invoices in ERPNext.

**Solution:**
- Webhook Trigger on `payment.captured` from Razorpay (HMAC signature verified)
- FlowBridge looks up the Sales Invoice by `receipt` field (set to invoice number during payment link creation)
- Create Doc node creates a Payment Entry linked to the invoice
- Condition node flags any payment where the amount doesn't match the invoice for human review

**Result:** Automatic reconciliation for 95% of payments. Exceptions go to a review queue.

---

## Finance & Accounting

### GST e-Invoice Automation

**Problem:** Every B2B invoice above ₹5 crore requires IRN generation from the NIC portal before it can be sent to the customer.

**Solution:**
- DocType Event Trigger on `Sales Invoice` (on_submit)
- Condition node checks: is `customer_gstin` set and `grand_total > 500000`?
- HTTP Action calls the GST e-Invoice API with the invoice payload
- Update Doc node writes the returned `irn`, `ack_no`, and `ack_date` back to the Sales Invoice
- Error handler sends a WhatsApp alert if the IRN call fails

**Result:** IRN generated automatically on every qualifying invoice submission. Compliance enforced programmatically.

---

### Bank Statement Auto-Reconciliation

**Problem:** Manually matching bank transactions to Payment Entries takes the accounts team 2–3 hours every morning.

**Solution:**
- Schedule Trigger runs at 7 AM daily
- HTTP Action fetches bank statement from HDFC SmartHub / RazorpayX API
- FlowBridge runs the auto-reconciliation engine: exact match → fuzzy match (RapidFuzz) → unmatched queue
- Matched entries create ERPNext Bank Transactions linked to Payment Entries
- Unmatched items are posted to a human review queue with suggested matches

**Result:** 80–90% of transactions reconciled automatically. Team reviews only exceptions.

---

### TDS Compliance (Section 194C/194J)

**Problem:** The accounts team manually checks every vendor payment for TDS applicability, leading to missed deductions and compliance risk.

**Solution:**
- DocType Event Trigger on `Purchase Invoice` (on_submit)
- FlowBridge checks section applicability against the invoice's `expense_head` and cumulative annual payments to the vendor
- If threshold crossed: Create Doc creates a TDS entry; Update Doc marks the invoice
- Monthly Schedule Trigger generates Form 26Q (FVU format) and emails to the tax team

---

### Cash Flow Forecasting

**Problem:** The CFO wants a daily 30-day cash flow forecast but there's no automated way to generate it.

**Solution:**
- Schedule Trigger runs at 6 AM daily
- FlowBridge reads outstanding Sales Invoices (historical collection rate by aging bucket), confirmed Purchase Order delivery dates, and scheduled payment terms
- AI node generates a natural-language commentary on the forecast
- FlowBridge sends the forecast table + commentary to the CFO via WhatsApp and email

---

## Logistics & Supply Chain

### Shiprocket Order Fulfillment

**Problem:** Delivery orders need to be created in Shiprocket when an ERPNext Delivery Note is submitted, and tracking numbers need to flow back.

**Solution:**
- DocType Event Trigger on `Delivery Note` (on_submit)
- HTTP Action calls Shiprocket's create shipment API
- Update Doc writes the AWB number and Shiprocket shipment ID back to the Delivery Note
- Second flow: Webhook Trigger on Shiprocket delivery status events → updates ERPNext with delivery status and WhatsApps the customer

---

### ICEGATE Export Documentation

**Problem:** Exporting goods requires a Shipping Bill filed with Indian Customs (ICEGATE). Generating the XML and submitting it manually is error-prone and slow.

**Solution:**
- DocType Event Trigger on `Delivery Note` with `is_export = 1`
- FlowBridge assembles the ICEGATE Shipping Bill XML from ERPNext fields
- HTTP Action submits the XML to the ICEGATE API
- Update Doc writes the Shipping Bill number back to the Delivery Note
- Error flow alerts the export team via WhatsApp if ICEGATE rejects the submission

---

### Low-Stock Auto Purchase Order

**Problem:** Warehouse staff forget to reorder items until they run out.

**Solution:**
- Schedule Trigger runs every hour
- FlowBridge SQL node queries items where actual stock  0
- Condition node branches by days remaining:  500000)
- FlowBridge creates an `FB Approval Request` with a unique token
- WhatsApp message sent to CFO: *"PO-2026-123 for ₹6,50,000 from Vendor XYZ. Reply APPROVE  or REJECT "*
- WhatsApp webhook receives the reply, resolves the token, and submits or cancels the PO
- Escalation: if no response in 4 hours, the request is forwarded to the Deputy CFO

---

## IoT & Real-Time Data

### Factory Sensor → ClickHouse Time Series

**Problem:** Temperature, pressure, and vibration sensors on the factory floor send MQTT messages every 5 seconds. You need to store, visualize, and alert on this data.

**Solution:**
- `FB MQTT Source` subscribes to `factory/+/sensors/#` (wildcard topic)
- Each message is ingested into ClickHouse's `fb_iot_readings` table with timestamp, device ID, and value
- Condition: if temperature > 85°C on a critical machine, FlowBridge creates a `Maintenance Request` in ERPNext and WhatsApps the maintenance team
- Grafana dashboard pulls from ClickHouse for real-time visualization

---

### Kafka Event Stream Processing

**Problem:** Your e-commerce platform publishes order events to a Kafka topic. ERPNext needs to process these without polling.

**Solution:**
- `FB Kafka Source` subscribes to the `orders` topic with a consumer group
- Each message triggers the Shopify → ERPNext flow (or a custom variant)
- Dead-letter queue (DLQ) captures messages that fail after 3 retries
- `LagMonitor` fires an HTTP alert when consumer lag exceeds 1000 messages, allowing auto-scaling
- `FB Kafka Sink` publishes fulfillment events back to the `order-updates` topic after ERPNext processing

---

## White-Label SaaS

### GST Automation SaaS for CAs

**Problem:** A CA firm wants to offer a branded GST automation tool to their 50 SME clients without building custom software.

**Solution:**
- Use FlowBridge's **White-Label SaaS Builder** to create "GST AutoFiler by CA Firm"
- Custom domain: `gst.cafirm.com`; custom logo and color scheme
- 5 pre-built flows: GSTR-1, GSTR-3B, e-Invoice, e-Way Bill, TDS 26Q
- Client onboarding in < 10 minutes via the self-service wizard
- Monthly billing via Razorpay: ₹999/client/month
- CA firm receives 90% of revenue; FlowBridge takes 10%
- Partner operations dashboard shows MRR, churn, and per-client health

# FlowBridge User Guide

> Complete reference for building, deploying, and operating integrations on FlowBridge.

---

## Table of Contents

2. [Studio — Building Flows](#2-studio--building-flows)

## 1. Getting Started

### What FlowBridge Does

FlowBridge connects your ERPNext/Frappe site to every external service you use — no separate integration server needed. It gives you:

- A **visual flow builder** (drag-and-drop canvas) for automations
- A **no-code API builder** that turns any DocType into a published REST/GraphQL/gRPC API
- A **marketplace** where you can install pre-built integrations or sell your own
- **AI assistance** at every step — from building flows in plain English to auto-mapping fields

### Navigating to FlowBridge

From the Frappe Desk, look for the **FlowBridge** module in the app list. The main areas are:

| Page | URL path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| Studio | `/flowbridge-studio` | Build and manage flows |
| API Builder | Via Developer Portal | Create and publish APIs |
| Marketplace | `/flowbridge-marketplace` | Browse and install integrations |
| Developer Portal | `/flowbridge-developer-portal` | Manage API keys, docs, analytics |

### First-time Setup

1. Go to **FlowBridge Settings** (search from the Frappe desk)
2. Set your **AI provider** (Anthropic / OpenAI / Gemini / Ollama) and paste the API key
3. Optionally configure **ClickHouse** for analytics and **Typesense** for search
4. Save — FlowBridge is ready

---

## 2. Studio — Building Flows

### The Canvas

The Studio canvas is a full-screen React Flow editor. Key controls:

| Control | Action |
|---|---|
| Drag from sidebar | Add a new node |
| Click node | Open node settings panel |
| Click connector line | Delete connection |
| Ctrl/Cmd + S | Save flow |
| Ctrl/Cmd + Z | Undo |
| Minimap (bottom-right) | Navigate large flows |
| Debug overlay (top-right) | Show live execution state |

### Creating a Flow

1. Click **New Flow** from the Studio home
2. Give the flow a name and choose a trigger type
3. Build your node chain left-to-right
4. Click **Test** to run with a sample payload in sandbox mode
5. Click **Activate** to go live

### Node Types

#### Trigger Nodes
The first node in every flow. Only one trigger per flow.

| Type | When it fires |
|---|---|
| Webhook | An HTTP POST arrives at the flow's unique URL |
| Schedule | A cron expression fires (e.g., every day at 9 AM) |
| DocType Event | A Frappe document is inserted, updated, submitted, cancelled, or deleted |
| Manual | Triggered explicitly via API or the Studio Run button |
| Polling | FlowBridge polls an external URL on your schedule |
| CDC | MariaDB binlog change is detected on a specific table |

#### Action Nodes
Perform work — call APIs, create/update/delete documents, send messages.

| Node | What it does |
|---|---|
| HTTP Request | Call any external API (uses a Connector) |
| Create Doc | Insert a new Frappe document |
| Update Doc | Set fields on an existing document |
| Delete Doc | Trash a Frappe document |
| Send WhatsApp | Send a WhatsApp message via Business API |
| Send Email | Send email via SendGrid or SMTP |
| Send Telegram | Send a Telegram bot message |
| Run SQL | Execute a read-only SQL query and expose result |
| Sub-flow | Call another FlowBridge flow and wait for its result |

#### Logic Nodes

| Node | What it does |
|---|---|
| Condition | Branch on a Jinja2 expression — `{{ doc.grand_total > 10000 }}` |
| Delay | Wait N seconds, minutes, or hours before continuing |
| Loop | Iterate over an array in the context |
| Batch | Process array items in parallel chunks |

#### Transform Nodes

| Node | What it does |
|---|---|
| FB Field Mapping | Map fields between two schemas using 30 built-in transforms |
| Jinja Template | Render a Jinja2 template with context variables |
| JSON Extract | Use dot-notation or JMESPath to pull values from a response |

### Field Mapping

The field mapper opens when you add an **FB Field Mapping** node. Left panel shows source fields; right panel shows target fields.

- **Drag** a source field onto a target field to map it
- Click a mapped pair to add a **transform**: uppercase, lowercase, number format, date format, regex replace, and 25 more
- Click **AI Auto-suggest** to let the AI map all fields automatically based on name similarity
- Use `{{ context.previous_node.field_name }}` in any value box to reference earlier outputs

### Testing a Flow

Click **Test** in the Studio toolbar. A panel opens where you can:

1. Paste a sample payload (JSON)
2. Choose **Sandbox** (mock all HTTP calls) or **Live** (real calls)
3. Click **Run** — each node lights up green (success) or red (error)
4. Click any node in the trace to see its input, output, and duration
5. The last 15 test runs are saved under **Test History**

---

## 3. Connectors & Credentials

### Pre-built Connectors

FlowBridge ships with 10 connectors installable as fixtures:

- Shopify, Razorpay, WhatsApp Business, Twilio SMS, SendGrid
- Google Sheets, Shiprocket, WooCommerce, Tally, ERPNext REST

Browse more in the **Marketplace** under the Connectors tab.

### Creating a Connector

1. Go to **FB Connector** → New
2. Set **Base URL**, **Auth Type**, and configure endpoints
3. Or use the **AI Connector Builder** (Developer Portal → Connector Builder tab):
   - Type: *"I need to connect to the Zoho Books API to fetch invoices and create payments"*
   - The AI generates the full connector JSON including auth, endpoints, and field mappings

### Managing Credentials

Credentials are stored in **FB Credential** — all secret values are encrypted at rest with AES-256-GCM.

1. Go to **FB Credential** → New
2. Select the Connector and Auth Type
3. Fill in the required fields (API key, OAuth tokens, etc.)
4. FlowBridge never stores credentials in plain text — even in logs

**Supported auth types:** API Key (header/query), Bearer Token, Basic Auth, OAuth2 (authorization code, client credentials), HMAC, AWS SigV4, mTLS, Custom Header.

---

## 4. Triggers

### Webhook Trigger

Each flow gets a unique webhook URL:

```
https://your-site.erpnext.com/api/method/frappe_flowbridge.flowbridge.api.flow.webhook?flow=
```

Configure:
- **Verify signature** — HMAC-SHA256 with a secret (Shopify, GitHub, Razorpay format supported)
- **Idempotency** — duplicate webhook IDs are rejected for 24 hours

### Schedule Trigger

Uses standard cron syntax (`* * * * *`). Examples:

| Expression | Runs |
|---|---|
| `0 9 * * 1-5` | 9 AM on weekdays |
| `*/15 * * * *` | Every 15 minutes |
| `0 0 1 * *` | First day of each month at midnight |

### DocType Event Trigger

Fires when any Frappe document changes. Configure:

- **DocType** — e.g., `Sales Invoice`
- **Event** — after_insert, on_update, on_submit, on_cancel, on_trash
- **Filter** — Jinja condition to narrow which documents trigger the flow: `{{ doc.customer == "Acme Corp" }}`

### CDC Trigger

Reads the MariaDB binlog to detect changes without polling. Lower latency than DocType events for high-frequency tables. Requires `binlog_format = ROW` in MariaDB config.

---

## 5. Actions & Nodes

### HTTP Request Node

Calls an external API endpoint defined in a Connector.

| Field | Description |
|---|---|
| Connector | Select the connector to use |
| Endpoint | Select the endpoint from the connector |
| Input mapping | Map flow context variables to the endpoint's input fields |
| Output mapping | Pull response fields into the flow context |
| On error | Retry with backoff / Skip / Fail flow |

### Pagination

For endpoints that return pages of data, enable **Pagination** on the connector endpoint:

- **Page-based:** `?page=1&per_page=100` with `has_more` field
- **Cursor-based:** use `next_cursor` from response as the next request's parameter
- **Offset-based:** `?offset=0&limit=100`, auto-increment until empty page
- **Link header:** `Link: ; rel="next"` standard

FlowBridge loops automatically until all pages are fetched, then continues the flow with the full array.

### Create/Update Doc Node

| Field | Description |
|---|---|
| DocType | Target Frappe DocType |
| Method | insert / set_value / save |
| Field values | Static values or `{{ context.field }}` expressions |
| On duplicate | Update existing / Skip / Raise error |

### Transform Reference

Available transforms in the Field Mapper:

| Transform | Example |
|---|---|
| `uppercase` | `"hello"` → `"HELLO"` |
| `lowercase` | `"HELLO"` → `"hello"` |
| `strip` | `" hello "` → `"hello"` |
| `number_format` | `1234.5` → `"1,234.50"` |
| `date_format` | `"2026-05-12"` → `"12/05/2026"` |
| `regex_replace` | Pattern + replacement |
| `json_extract` | `response.data[0].id` |
| `jinja` | Arbitrary Jinja2 expression |
| `map_values` | `{"open": "Draft", "closed": "Paid"}` lookup |
| `split` | Split string on delimiter, take index N |
| `join` | Join array to string |
| `multiply` / `divide` / `add` / `subtract` | Arithmetic |
| `round` | Round to N decimal places |
| `to_int` / `to_float` / `to_str` | Type coercion |
| `if_empty` | Default value when source is blank |
| `slugify` | `"Sales Order"` → `"sales-order"` |

---

## 6. AI Features

### AI Flow Builder

From the Studio canvas, click the **AI** button in the toolbar and describe your flow:

> *"When a Shopify order is paid, create a Sales Invoice in ERPNext, then send a WhatsApp message to the customer with the invoice number."*

FlowBridge generates the full flow — nodes, connections, and field mappings — ready to review and activate.

**Refinement:** After generation, you can say *"Add a condition to skip orders under ₹500"* and the AI will patch only the affected part of the flow.

### AI Field Mapper

Click **AI Auto-suggest** in any field mapper. The AI uses:

1. Name similarity (sentence-transformers cosine distance)
2. 22 built-in synonym groups (`customer_name` ↔ `party_name`, `total` ↔ `grand_total`, etc.)
3. Data type matching as a tiebreaker

Unconfident suggestions are shown with a yellow indicator. You can accept, reject, or override each one.

### Copilot Sidebar

The Copilot is an AI assistant embedded in:

- **Studio** — answers questions about the current flow, explains node behavior, suggests fixes for errors
- **Error Logs** — explains why a run failed and suggests the exact fix
- **API Builder** — generates endpoint configurations from natural language
- **Analytics** — answers data questions in plain English

Open the Copilot by clicking the **✨ Copilot** button in the top-right of any of these pages.

### AI-Native API Builder

In the Developer Portal → API Builder tab:

1. Type: *"I need an API that returns overdue invoices grouped by customer, with total outstanding amount"*
2. The AI generates the full endpoint config — DocType, filters, field list, computed fields, auth settings
3. Preview the generated SQL and response shape
4. Click **Publish** to go live instantly

You can also:
- Ask the AI to explain an existing endpoint in plain English
- Generate test cases for an endpoint automatically
- Clone an endpoint with a variation (*"same as the invoices API but only for the current user's company"*)

---

## 7. API Builder

The API Builder turns any Frappe DocType into a published REST API endpoint in minutes.

### Creating an Endpoint

1. Go to **Developer Portal** → **API Builder**
2. Click **New Endpoint**
3. Set:
   - **DocType** — e.g., `Sales Invoice`
   - **Slug** — URL-friendly name: `sales-invoices`
   - **Fields** — which fields to return
   - **Filters** — default filters applied to every request
   - **Auth** — API key, JWT, or public
4. Click **Publish**

Your API is live at:
```
GET /api/method/frappe_flowbridge.flowbridge.api.api_builder.execute_endpoint?slug=sales-invoices
```

### Query Parameters

All published endpoints support these parameters automatically:

| Parameter | Example | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `?search=acme` | Full-text search | Typesense (if enabled) or SQL LIKE |
| `?semantic_search=overdue construction` | Semantic search | Vector similarity via ClickHouse |
| `?filter[status]=Submitted` | Filter by field | Adds WHERE clause |
| `?sort_by=grand_total:desc` | Sort | ORDER BY clause |
| `?limit=50&offset=0` | Pagination | LIMIT / OFFSET |
| `?facets=status,customer_group` | Faceted counts | Returns aggregations alongside data |
| `?suggest=1&q=ac` | Autocomplete | Typesense prefix search |
| `?_format=csv` | CSV export | Returns CSV file |
| `?_format=msgpack` | MessagePack | Binary encoding for performance |

### GraphQL

For any Published endpoint, access it via GraphQL at:
```
POST /api/method/frappe_flowbridge.flowbridge.api.api_graphql.graphql_execute
```

The schema is auto-generated — query it with:
```graphql
{
  salesInvoices(filter: "status = 'Submitted'", limit: 10) {
    name
    customer
    grandTotal
    status
  }
}
```

### gRPC

Proto3 schema and Rust/Tonic stubs are auto-generated for each endpoint. Download them from the Developer Portal under the endpoint's **SDK** tab.

### Rate Limiting & Auth

On each endpoint you can configure:

- **Rate limit** — requests per minute (global, per API key, or per user)
- **Auth required** — which roles can access
- **API key** — issued from the Developer Portal
- **HMAC signature verification** for webhook-style security
- **Geo rate limits** — per-country RPM limits using MaxMind GeoLite2

### Lifecycle

Endpoints move through a managed lifecycle:

```
Draft → In Review → Published → Deprecated → Retired
```

- A **breaking change** (removing a field, changing auth, modifying filters) is automatically detected and blocks publish until a changelog entry is added
- **Consumers** of your API are notified of upcoming deprecations
- **Contract tests** run daily to verify the API still matches its documented schema

---

## 8. Marketplace

### Installing a Flow or Connector

1. Go to `/flowbridge-marketplace`
2. Browse by category, or search by name
3. Click the flow/connector card → **Install**
4. A wizard appears for any required credentials
5. FlowBridge provisions all DocTypes, installs the connector, and activates the flow

Updates are checked daily and shown as a notification in the Marketplace.

### Publishing to the Marketplace

1. Build and test your flow or connector
2. Go to **Developer Portal** → **My Packages** → **New Package**
3. Fill in title, description, category, and screenshots
4. Set **pricing** (free, one-time, or subscription via Razorpay)
5. Submit for review — the FlowBridge team reviews within 3 business days
6. After approval, your package appears in the marketplace

**Revenue:** You keep 90% of every sale. FlowBridge takes a 10% platform fee. Payouts via RazorpayX, net-30, with TDS and GST TDS auto-calculated.

### Partner Tiers

| Tier | Requirements | Revenue Share |
|---|---|---|
| Silver | 1 connector, 3 flows, 10 installs | 30% |
| Gold | 3 connectors, 10 flows, 100 installs | 40% |
| Platinum | 8 connectors, 25 flows, 1000 installs | 50% + white-label rights |

## 9. Analytics & Observability

### Flow Analytics

In the Studio, click **Analytics** in the left sidebar to see:

- Run count, success rate, average duration for each flow
- Step-level bottlenecks — which nodes are slowest
- Error breakdown by type and connector
- Volume trends over time

### API Analytics

In the Developer Portal → **Analytics** tab:

- Requests/minute, p50/p95/p99 response times
- Error rates by endpoint and status code
- Top consumers by API key
- Cache hit rate and Typesense vs SQL search usage
- Anomaly alerts when error rate exceeds threshold

### Searching Logs

All flow runs and API requests are searchable from the Developer Portal. Use the natural language search box:

> *"Show me failed runs for the Shopify connector in the last 24 hours"*

### OpenTelemetry

FlowBridge emits traces in OpenTelemetry format. Point your collector at:

```
OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT=http://your-otel-collector:4318
```

Set this in **FlowBridge Settings** → Advanced → OpenTelemetry Endpoint.

### Grafana

FlowBridge exposes a Grafana SimpleJSON datasource at:
```
GET frappe_flowbridge.flowbridge.api.bi.grafana_datasource_query
```
Add it to Grafana as a SimpleJSON data source and import the bundled dashboards.

---

## 10. Security & Compliance

### Credential Security

All credentials (API keys, OAuth tokens, passwords) are encrypted at rest using AES-256-GCM with a PBKDF2-SHA256 derived key. They are never written to logs, never returned in API responses, and never included in exports.

### PII Protection

FlowBridge automatically detects PII fields (Aadhaar, PAN, mobile, email, date of birth) in your API endpoints and adds them to the mask list. Masked fields are shown as `***` in responses unless the caller has explicit permission.

**Right to erasure:** To comply with India's DPDP Act, run:
```
POST frappe_flowbridge.flowbridge.api.api_security.erase_personal_data
    identity_field=email
    value=customer@example.com
    doctypes=Customer,Contact,Sales Invoice
```
All matching records are anonymized (not deleted) — names replaced with `ERASED-{hash}`.

### Audit Trail

Every configuration change to an FB API Endpoint is recorded in **FB Security Audit Log** with:
- Timestamp, user, action, and a diff
- SHA-256 hash chained to the previous entry — tamper-evident

Run `verify_audit_chain()` to check chain integrity at any time.

### SOC 2 Evidence

Export a ZIP bundle for auditors:
```
GET frappe_flowbridge.flowbridge.api.api_security.export_soc2_evidence?from_date=2026-01-01&to_date=2026-03-31
```
The ZIP contains: config change logs, access logs, rate limit violations, security alerts, and an uptime report.

### Security Posture Score

**FlowBridge Settings** → **Security** shows a 0–100 score with a breakdown:
- % of Published endpoints with auth enabled
- % with rate limiting
- % with PII masking
- Open VAPT findings
- Secret scan status (clean / issues found)

---

## 11. Search & Discovery

### Full-Text Search

Enable Typesense on an endpoint by checking **Enable Typesense Search** in the endpoint config. After enabling:

1. Run **Backfill** to index existing records (button in the endpoint settings)
2. Use `?search=your query` on any API request
3. Typo-tolerance is built in — `?search=invioce` finds "invoice"

### Semantic Search

Enable **Semantic Search** on an endpoint and select the fields to embed. Then:

```
GET ?semantic_search=find overdue invoices from construction clients in Q4
```

FlowBridge converts the query to a vector and finds semantically matching records using cosine distance, even without exact keyword matches.

### Faceted Search

Configure facets on an endpoint (endpoint settings → Facets tab). Then:

```
GET ?facets=status,customer_group&search=acme
```

Response includes:
```json
{
  "data": [...],
  "facets": {
    "status": {"Open": 23, "Paid": 47},
    "customer_group": {"Retail": 35, "Wholesale": 12}
  }
}
```

### Autocomplete

```
GET ?suggest=1&q=ac
```

Returns fast prefix suggestions (` | Retry the last failed run |
| `pause ` | Pause a flow |
| `approve ` | Approve a pending human-in-loop step |
| `build ` | Build a new flow via AI (Developer role only) |
| `health` | Connector health summary |

Set your phone number in **My Profile** → FlowBridge Messenger to activate.

### Multi-Tenant Cloud

If you are running FlowBridge Cloud, each tenant is isolated via:
- Redis namespace prefixing (`tenant:{id}:*`)
- Separate MariaDB schema per tenant (optional)
- Data residency zones — requests from outside the zone are rejected at the API layer

### CDC (Change Data Capture)

The CDC engine reads the MariaDB binlog in real-time and fires flows within milliseconds of a document change — without any polling overhead.

Requirements:
```ini
# my.cnf
binlog_format = ROW
binlog_row_image = FULL
server_id = 1
```

Enable CDC in **FlowBridge Settings** → CDC. Each `FB CDC Config` record specifies which table to watch and which flow to trigger.

---

## 13. Troubleshooting

### A flow run failed — how do I find out why?

1. Go to **FB Flow Run** in the Frappe desk
2. Find the failed run and open it
3. Expand the **Steps** section — red steps show the exact error and the input/output at that step
4. Click **Copilot** to ask the AI to explain the error and suggest a fix
5. Click **Retry** to re-run from the failed step (uses the original payload)

### The webhook isn't firing

- Check the webhook URL is correct — copy it from the flow's Trigger node settings
- Verify the source system is sending to the right URL with the right Content-Type
- Check **FB Event** for incoming events — if none appear, the request isn't reaching FlowBridge
- If signature verification is enabled, check the secret matches exactly

### An API endpoint returns stale data

- Check **Cache TTL** on the endpoint — if set, responses are cached for that many seconds
- Click **Clear Cache** in the endpoint settings to force a refresh
- Or call `bump_cache_version` to invalidate all cached responses instantly:
  ```
  POST frappe_flowbridge.flowbridge.api.api_perf_hardening.bump_cache_version
      slug=your-endpoint-slug
  ```

### Typesense search returns no results

- Check that **Enable Typesense Search** is on and the Typesense host/API key is set in **FlowBridge Settings**
- Run **Backfill** from the endpoint settings to index existing records
- Check **search_fields** — search only covers the fields listed there

### A connector returns 401 Unauthorized

- Open the **FB Credential** linked to the connector and check the token hasn't expired
- For OAuth2 connectors, click **Re-authenticate** to refresh the token
- Check that the credential is linked to the correct connector and environment (sandbox vs production)

### How do I see what SQL a Published endpoint is running?

Enable **Log Query** on the endpoint. Each request will log the generated SQL to **FB API Endpoint Log** (retained for 7 days).

### The Vanguard RAM-cache sidecar isn't responding

The Vanguard sidecar is optional. If it isn't running, FlowBridge falls back to Redis automatically. To start it manually:

```bash
cd apps/frappe_flowbridge/executor
go build ./cmd/prefetch_worker/
./prefetch_worker   # listens on :7779
```

Set `VANGUARD_PORT` to change the port. Check status at `GET http://127.0.0.1:7779/stats`.

agreement or commitment, however denominated, not to enforce a patent
(such as an express permission to practice a patent or covenant not to
sue for patent infringement).  To "grant" such a patent license to a
party means to make such an agreement or commitment not to enforce a
patent against the party.

            How to Apply These Terms to Your New Programs

  If you develop a new program, and you want it to be of the greatest
possible use to the public, the best way to achieve this is to make it
free software which everyone can redistribute and change under these terms.

  To do so, attach the following notices to the program.  It is safest
to attach them to the start of each source file to most effectively
state the exclusion of warranty; and each file should have at least
the "copyright" line and a pointer to where the full notice is found.

    
    Copyright (C)   

    This program is free software: you can redistribute it and/or modify
    it under the terms of the GNU General Public License as published by
    the Free Software Foundation, either version 3 of the License, or
    (at your option) any later version.

    This program is distributed in the hope that it will be useful,
    but WITHOUT ANY WARRANTY; without even the implied warranty of
    MERCHANTABILITY or FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE.  See the
    GNU General Public License for more details.

    You should have received a copy of the GNU General Public License
    along with this program.  If not, see .

Also add information on how to contact you by electronic and paper mail.

  If the program does terminal interaction, make it output a short
notice like this when it starts in an interactive mode:

      Copyright (C)   
    This program comes with ABSOLUTELY NO WARRANTY; for details type `show w'.
    This is free software, and you are welcome to redistribute it
    under certain conditions; type `show c' for details.

The hypothetical commands `show w' and `show c' should show the appropriate
parts of the General Public License.  Of course, your program's commands
might be different; for a GUI interface, you would use an "about box".

  You should also get your employer (if you work as a programmer) or school,
if any, to sign a "copyright disclaimer" for the program, if necessary.
For more information on this, and how to apply and follow the GNU GPL, see
.

  The GNU General Public License does not permit incorporating your program
into proprietary programs.  If your program is a subroutine library, you
may consider it more useful to permit linking proprietary applications with
the library.  If this is what you want to do, use the GNU Lesser General
Public License instead of this License.  But first, please read
